| injected defect | caught by |
|---|---|
| priority is ignored (insertion order) | test_11 |
| retry limit is not honoured (off by one) | test_12 |
| `max_concurrent` is ignored | test_14, test_15 |
| `max_concurrent` is not clamped | test_23 |
| a junk setting is not caught | test_23 |
| the queue re-checks nothing on Stop | test_08 (30 frames rendered after Stop) |
| a Stop before the launch is rendered over | test_19 |
| a paused renderer is killed while suspended | test_24 |
| progress is written onto another job | test_16 |
| `pause_on_error` is stored and ignored | test_20 |
| a job holding a slot can be picked again | test_25 |
| the queue reads a renamed config key | test_26 |
| removing a job leaves its renderer | test_18 |

- `tests/test_job_manager.py` → `Ran 28 tests ... OK` (28.7 s); `tests/test_mock_dcc.py` → `Ran 27 tests ... OK` (11.2 s); `tests/test_postprocessor_paths.py` → `Ran 6 tests ... OK`; `run_audit.py` → `100% (11/11 suites)`.
- Four of those pins exist because the obvious measurement could not do the job. test_24 asserts the *order* of calls on a stub engine: on Windows `TerminateProcess` overrides a suspension, so a real render cannot show why waking first matters - it does on macOS/Linux, and the ordering is pinned where it can be. test_25 puts the job in the slot-held-but-PENDING state directly, because the real gap is a few hundred microseconds wide. test_26 reads the two source files as text, because a key renamed on one side saves fine and changes nothing. And test_22/test_23 exist because `max_concurrent: 0` in a hand-edited file means no slot ever fills: Start would appear dead forever.
- Two runner lessons, both from this pass. Killing a mutation probe mid-run leaves the source **mutated** - it happened twice here, once showing up as an anchor that no longer matched, and the tree now has to be verified restored before any result is believed. And a mutation that is "not caught" is not always a coverage hole: stubbing the progress callback stayed MISSED because the 1 s timer covers the same ground, so the defect was rewritten to the one that actually breaks a job - writing progress onto a different one.
- **Still open here:** the 1 s `_update_progress` poll is redundant with the callback for every engine this app ships, so no test can see it removed; it stays because a renderer that reports frames only through `get_progress()` would otherwise freeze the bar. `max_concurrent` above the machine's core count is the user's call - only the 1..16 clamp is enforced.

### Phase 4d (part) - the dashboard's queue controls reach the queue

`pause_selected_job`/`resume_selected_job` read `job_table.item(row, 0)`, but column 0 is the select `QCheckBox` set with `setCellWidget`, so `item()` there is `None` and both buttons raised `AttributeError` on every click. `update_progress` was defined and connected to nothing, so the queue bar in the same panel sat at 0 while a render ran.

**`src/ui/dashboard_tab.py`** - `selected_job_id()` (`:586`) reads the id from column 1, the same cell `remove_selected_job` and `get_selected_jobs` already use, and both buttons now go through it; `update_progress`/`update_status` are connected to `job_updated` (`:515-526`) - every frame line is a signal, so the bar follows the render with no second timer to drift - and both read `running_jobs()`, which is the queue's own view of what is live (`update_status` says `(+N more)` when the Settings tab allows concurrency). `on_queue_stopped()` hides the bar on `processing_stopped`, because "Pause queue on error" ends the run without a Stop click. A button that cannot do its job now says so in the status line instead of doing nothing.

**Closed by 4d-2 and 4d-3 below:** the `jpg`→`JPEG` format bug and the dead Render buttons.

### Phase 4d-2 - a job asks for a format the renderer can be told about

`AddJobDialog` stored the extension the user picked (`png`, `jpg`, `exr`) and `BlenderEngine.get_render_command` upper-cased it onto the command line, so Blender was handed `--render-format JPG` and `EXR`. Those are not Blender format names: it reports `Unknown input format`, exits without rendering, and the job's only trace is a nonzero exit code several minutes later. Maya was worse because it fails quietly - `defaultRenderGlobals.imageFormat` takes a number, and the old lookup defaulted to `32` (png) for anything it did not know, so a `bmp` job wrote PNG files and the queue then looked for frames under an extension that never existed.

**`src/processing/render_engines.py`** - the vocabulary is now owned here: `_BLENDER_IMAGE_FORMATS` (`jpg -> JPEG`, `exr -> OPEN_EXR`, plus tiff/bmp/webp/tga/dpx/hdr) and `_MAYA_IMAGE_FORMATS` (the app's existing ids, unchanged), `image_format_choices(software_type)` for what a dialog may offer, and `image_format_value()` which translates or raises a `ValueError` naming the accepted options. `job.output_format` still stores the extension, because that is what a saved queue JSON carries and what the post-processor metadata reports; only the command line changes. Unreal and Cinema 4D translate nothing - the pipeline config carries the extension as written, and C4D's command line has no output-format switch at all, so the scene decides - and both keep `DEFAULT_IMAGE_FORMATS`. A refused format reaches `job.error_message` through `_run_job`, so the queue says *why* instead of "Render job failed".

**`src/ui/dashboard_tab.py`** - the format list is drawn from the engine, not from a literal: `update_format_choices()` (`:120`) repopulates on `currentIndexChanged` and keeps the user's choice when the new engine also accepts it, so Maya now offers four formats and never shows the `bmp` it cannot be told about. It falls back to `DEFAULT_IMAGE_FORMATS` while no engine is selected, so the field is never empty.

**`tests/mock_dcc/mock_dcc.py`** - `BLENDER_FORMAT_NAMES` is Blender's own list, kept independent of the app's table, and `parse_blender` now rejects a name that is not in it. That is what turns "Blender wants JPEG" from a claim into something the harness can fail on: with the old `upper()` restored, `test_21` fails in the mock's own error.

**`tests/test_dashboard_contract.py`** (new, 16 tests, and the 12th audit suite) - the pattern that found the Mesh Tools bugs, applied to the queue panel: real widgets, real signals, no `hasattr` guards. `SpyManager` (a `JobManager` subclass) records what the panel asks for, so the tests can tell "the button reached the queue with this id" from "the button did nothing"; `TestFormatChoices` requires every offered format to build a command the engine accepts, checked against the mock's list rather than a second copy of the table; `TestQueueControls` pins the id read, the no-selection refusal, and a refusal from the queue; `TestQueueReadout` emits a real `job_updated` and requires the bar to move, the status line to count live jobs, and a self-stopped queue to hide its bar. The `test_15` case is why this file exists: the panel had already been wired in 4d, and only a widget-level test can see that a bar with nothing connected to it stays at 0.

- Proof: `tests/test_dashboard_contract.py` → `Ran 16 tests ... OK`; `tests/test_mock_dcc.py` → `Ran 29 tests ... OK` (27 before); `run_audit.py` → `100% (12/12 suites)`; 5 injected defects, 5 caught by a named test (upper-cased extension, Maya's silent png fallback, `JPG` as a Blender name, one format list for every engine, `EXR` as an OpenEXR name).
- The probe's first run reported 2 of 5 as SKIPPED, not MISSED: the sources are CRLF, and an anchor containing `\n` never matches. The anchor now carries the file's own newline convention - a skipped mutation is a hole in the runner, not in the tests, and it looks identical to coverage from the summary line.
- **Not covered here:** the mock still writes `.png`/`.exr` frames from its *scene* hint rather than the command-line format, so nothing checks that a JPEG job's files end `.jpg`. The app's post-processor does not filter on that extension (it records `'format'` in metadata only), so the mismatch has no consequence today; it becomes a test when a renderer is asked to write a format the queue then looks for. And the four Maya ids are the app's own - no package here can say whether `imageFormat 8` is JPEG, so the mock accepts any number and the repair leaves them exactly as they were rather than guessing.

### Phase 4d-3 - the Rendering tab's toolbar does the thing its label says

Nine toolbar actions and two "Render" buttons were drawn and connected to nothing; only "Export Scene" had a `triggered.connect`. The Settings pane's "Render Image" and the status bar's "Render Frame" were the same widget with the same silence, so the tab that is supposed to be the lookdev front end could not produce a picture. Two of its controls were not switchable at all: "Toggle Overlays" had nothing to toggle (`paintGL` drew the axis labels unconditionally inside a `try/except: pass`), and "Focus Selected" promised a selection the tab offers no way to make. "MP4 (H.264)" and "Samples: 128" were on-screen settings no code path could honour - the queue and `VideoExporter` render video, this tab never did.

**`src/ui/model_viewer.py`** - `show_overlays` (`:231`) plus `set_show_overlays()` (`:1795`), and the three axis-label draws gated on it (`:1495-1506`). The flag is read through `getattr(self, 'show_overlays', True)` inside `paintGL`, so a viewer built before the attribute exists still draws.

**`src/ui/rendering_tab.py`** - `RenderSettingsPanel` now owns one table per setting (`RESOLUTIONS` `:597`, `FORMATS` `:599`) that both the combo boxes and the renderer read, so the label the user picks and the file that gets written cannot disagree; `values(viewport_size)` (`:636`) returns `(size, extension, tag, denoise)`, with "Custom" meaning the viewport's own size because nothing else here knows how big the picture is. The pane emits `render_requested`, and the tab connects it to the same `render_frame` as the toolbar button. `set_view_mode` (`:788`) maps the four modes onto the viewport's real switches - wireframe/fill, textures, vertex colours, and grid + overlays off for `rendered` - and does not pretend there is a second renderer behind the tab. `reset_view`/`toggle_grid`/`toggle_overlays`/`snapshot` (`:805-856`) reach the viewport and say what changed. `render_frame` (`:858`) writes `{model}_0001.{ext}` in a folder the user chose, at exactly the requested resolution: `_grab_view` (`:828`) scales to cover and centre-crops instead of stretching, so a 4K file really is 3840x2160. Denoising runs through the Post-Processing tab's own `ImageDenoiser` pass on a temp file that keeps the frame's extension (OpenCV picks its writer from it); if the pass refuses, the raw frame stays and the status line says `as rendered` rather than `denoised`. `model_path` is tracked (`set_model`) so output lands beside the model it came from. "Focus Selected", the MP4 entry and the Samples spinbox are gone.

**`tests/test_rendering_tab_contract.py`** (new, 15 tests, and the 13th audit suite) - the same widget-level pattern as Mesh Tools and the dashboard. `test_01` asks Qt directly: `action.receivers(action.triggered)` / `button.receivers(button.clicked)` must be nonzero, so a drawn-but-dead control fails the suite before any behaviour test has to notice. `TestViewportModes` pins each mode to the viewport state its label promises and checks the toggles flip both ways; `TestRenderFrame` reads the written file back with `cv2.imread` and requires the shape to be the resolution asked for, checks the format reaches the extension, that both buttons do the same job, that a refused denoise keeps the frame, that a declined folder writes nothing, and that nothing is renamed `Focus Selected`'s way. `QFileDialog` is shimmed to return a path - the real dialogs are modal and cannot be clicked headlessly - and restored in `tearDownClass`.

- Proof: `tests/test_rendering_tab_contract.py` → `Ran 15 tests ... OK`; `tests/test_all_tabs_headless.py` → `Ran 14 tests ... OK`; `run_audit.py` → `100% (13/13 suites passed in 117.30s)`; 10 injected defects, 10 caught by a named test (each Render button disconnected, `set_render_mode` not reached, rendered mode leaving overlays on the plate, frame written at pane size, format ignored, "Focus Selected" restored, a failed denoise claiming success, `snapshot` not saving, `reset_view` not reaching the camera).
- **Not covered here:** the suite asserts size, format, naming and viewport state - not pixel *content*. Headless GL gives no way to say "this is a shaded model, not a blank plate", so whether the preview looks right is still the user's eye. `_grab_view` centre-crops when the requested aspect is not the pane's, so a 4K frame from a wide window loses its edges rather than its resolution. And `rendered` is the OpenGL viewport with grid and labels off; it is a lookdev frame, which is what the status line reports, and a Cycles-quality still is still the queue's job.

### Phase 5a - the converter claims only what a library here can do

This is the one that mattered, because "it converts FBX and DAE" is the reason the app exists for game-dev hand-off, and **it had never once worked.**

What the machine said when asked (`trimesh 4.7.4`, `pymeshlab 2025.7`, `pyassimp 5.2.5` + `lib/assimp-vc143-mt.dll`, measured by exporting all nine formats each way and reading the result back):

- `SUPPORTED_CONVERSIONS` was a hand-typed list of nine source keys. trimesh has no FBX or Collada writer and no FBX or Collada *reader* at all; pymeshlab reads all nine but raises `Unknown format for save: gltf`; **assimp is the only FBX writer here** - and the assimp call was broken, so OBJ→FBX, glTF→FBX and every DAE output went nowhere.
- `pyassimp.load()` is a `@contextmanager`, not a loader that returns a scene. The old `scene = pyassimp.load(path)` bound a generator wrapper, `aiExportScene` answered `AssimpError` - and `AssimpError` inherits **`BaseException`, not `Exception`** (`pyassimp/errors.py:7`), so it walked straight past the `except Exception` and out of the converter.
- `pyassimp.export()` wants the exporter's **id**, not the extension: this DLL reports `gltf2`, `glb2`, `collada`, `fbx`, `3mf` via `aiGetExportFormatDescription`. Handing it `"dae"` is a silent no-op failure.
- assimp writes everything except `.off`. trimesh writes `.off` and `.3mf`. Both were in `SupportedFormat` and in **no** target list, so the two formats that needed no library at all were never offered.
- `get_conversion_preview` and `get_format_info` asked trimesh, which cannot open `.dae`/`.fbx`: the panel that is supposed to help you choose a conversion answered `Could not load mesh` for exactly the two formats you came for.
- **pymeshlab's FBX reader does not report a malformed file - it takes an access violation and the process dies with it** (measured: exit `0xC0000005` on bytes that were merely named `.fbx`). That was one Convert click away, inside a `QThread`, for the whole app. assimp raises a catchable error for the same bytes.
- `batch_convert` set `library_used` to the first *installed* library whether or not it had done anything, and `FormatConversionThread` ignored `result['success']` per file and reported `list(zip(inputs, outputs))`, so a batch in which every conversion failed displayed **"All N files converted successfully"**.
- `ScriptAPI.convert_format` (`:154`) called `converter.convert()`, a method that does not exist: every documented `rfx.convert_format(...)` call raised `AttributeError`.
- `os.makedirs(os.path.dirname(output_file))` raised `FileNotFoundError` for a bare `result.stl` - the Known-deferred item, now closed.

**`src/processing/format_converter.py`** - the hand-typed matrix is gone. `_CAPABILITIES` (`:190`) is the measured read/write set per library plus `applies_options`, and everything else derives from it: `supported_targets`/`get_supported_targets` (`:234`/`:273`, the instance one filtered by what is installed *here*), `conversion_libraries` (`:283`, the libraries that can carry the whole pair, in try order), `conversion_blocker` (`:311`, names the missing library), `can_convert` (`:333`). `_ASSIMP_EXPORT_IDS` (`:211`) is the DLL's id table; `_convert_with_assimp` (`:478`) uses `with pyassimp.load(...) as scene:` and the id, and treats an empty or missing output as a failure. `CONVERSION_ERRORS` (`:144`) is `(Exception, pyassimp.AssimpError)` so the BaseException-derived one is actually caught everywhere. `_source_blocker` (`:300`) refuses an `.fbx` with no `Kaydara FBX`/`FBXHeaderExtension` header before any reader sees it, and `_CLEAN_FAILURE_FIRST` (`:223`) puts assimp ahead of MeshLab for `.fbx` sources in case the header is right and the body is not. `_read_as_trimesh` (`:718`) is the preview bridge - trimesh, then pymeshlab, then assimp, returning `(mesh, which)` so `get_conversion_preview` (`:773`) can say `opened_with` - and it is the same reader the LOD chain and the preview use, so an LOD cut from `.fbx` works like the picture above it claims to. Options are now reported instead of silently dropped: `_meaningful_options` (`:88`) ignores the defaults the panels always send, and a success through pymeshlab/assimp carries `options_skipped` and says `Converted using assimp (scale not applied)`. `_prepare_output_dir` (`:81`) fixes the bare-filename crash; `update_faces(unique_faces())` replaces the deprecated call; `convert_with_lod_generation` (`:593`) takes `options` and, for a target trimesh cannot write, cuts the chain through a scratch `.obj` inside the output dir and re-exports via `convert_file`, skipping a level that fails instead of returning a phantom path.

**`src/processing/script_engine.py`** - `convert_format` (`:154`) calls `convert_file`, strips a leading dot from the format, and raises with the backend's reason instead of `"Format conversion failed"`.

**`src/ui/preprocessing_tab.py`** - `FormatConversionThread` (`:65`) checks `result['success']` per file and splits `converted`/`failed` with the real `method` and `options_skipped` (`conversion_complete` is now `pyqtSignal(list, list)`, `:71`), so `show_conversion_complete` (`:1091`) builds its table from those rows and a failed batch says how many failed. `generate_lods` is passed from the checkbox instead of being read nowhere.

**`src/ui/model_tab.py`** - the batch dialog lists every `SupportedFormat` (`:181`) so OFF and 3MF are reachable; the two checkboxes nothing ever read ("Merge all objects", "Apply transforms") are a line of text (`:197`), because the converter always joins the scene and bakes the transforms.

### Phase 5b - the released app has the FBX writer, and the picker says when it does not

The conversion stack is a python binding, a native DLL and a file dialog filter, and all three were wrong in ways that only show up away from this machine.

**`requirements.txt`** declared no `pyassimp` at all - so `pip install -r requirements.txt` on a fresh box gave an app with no FBX or Collada writer and no message. It is pinned `pyassimp==5.2.5` with the reason next to it. `pymeshlab>=2022.2` was a floor nothing was tested against; it is `pymeshlab==2025.7`, the release every filter, accessor and parameter name in this app was checked against (`test_pymeshlab_calls.py` reads them out of the installed build).

**`format_converter.py:24-45`** - pyassimp's `search_library()` looks in its own package folder and in **every directory already on `PATH`** (measured: `pyassimp/helper.py:202` `search_library()`, whose candidate folders are its own package dir plus `os.environ['PATH'].split(';')` added at `:52-55`); nothing looks in this app's `lib/`. The old bootstrap computed `lib/` as three directories above `__file__`, which is the repo root when run from source and the *bundle's* `_MEIPASS` when frozen - by luck, and only if the DLL is collected. `_assimp_lib_dirs()` now takes the source root, `sys._MEIPASS` and the executable's folder, keeps the ones that exist, and puts a folder that actually contains an `assimp*.dll` first. `conversion_blocker` appends where it looked, so a missing FBX writer is answered with a path rather than a guess.

**`RenderFox.spec`** collected neither the binding nor the library: `pyassimp` is now a hidden import and `lib/assimp-vc143-mt.dll` is a data file. Without both, the packaged app keeps the FBX menu entry and loses every FBX file - the `.pdb` stays out.

**`src/ui/preprocessing_tab.py`** - the batch picker's open filter was `*.obj *.ply *.stl *.off *.dae *.gltf *.glb *.3mf`: no `*.fbx`, so on Windows the flagship format was **invisible in the dialog**. It is in. And `_refresh_target_availability()` (`:1011`), called when files are added or cleared, now asks the converter for every loaded source and greys out a target nothing installed can write from it, with `conversion_blocker`'s sentence as the tooltip - and moves the current selection off a greyed-out row, because a picker left pointing at FBX with no assimp converts nothing and explains nothing.

**`src/ui/model_tab.py`** - the *other* conversion dialog had its own format list, and it disagreed with the converter twice over: its open filter was `*.obj *.stl *.ply *.glb *.gltf *.fbx *.dae *.x3d`, which hides OFF and 3MF and offers `.x3d`, a suffix `SupportedFormat` does not contain and `batch_convert` answers with "Skipping unsupported file". The filter is now built from `SupportedFormat` itself, and a suffix the converter does not know is not added to the list. Writing this caught a second bug in the same call: the filter had been passed as the *third* positional argument, i.e. into Qt's `directory` slot, so no filter reached the dialog at all.

**`tests/test_format_converter_contract.py`** (new, 23 tests, the 14th audit suite) - seeds are written by the libraries directly, never by the converter, so no test passes by believing the thing it checks. `test_01` converts **every pair in the capability table** and requires a non-empty file and a reported library that table says can do it; `test_02` reads each result back and requires the face count to survive (a cube is 12 faces to every reader here, 24 to none - the first draft asserted vertices and failed on a correct conversion); `test_05`/`test_06` reopen the FBX through *pymeshlab* and the DAE through the preview bridge, so a writer cannot pass by writing bytes only assimp can read; `test_09` hands a corrupt `.fbx` to a **child process** and asserts exit code 0, because the defect it guards is a segfault and a test that segfaults the runner reports nothing; `test_18` re-imports the module in a child with `lib/` stripped from `PATH` and requires it back (and, on a clone with no binaries, requires the blocker to say where it looked); `test_19` reads `requirements.txt` the way pip does - comments dropped, name split off the specifier - and checks the spec's `hiddenimports` and `datas` lists *as sections*, because "pyassimp appears somewhere in the file" was satisfied by my own comment about it; `test_20`-`test_22` drive the real Add Files button and the real combo, and `test_23` opens the Model tab's converter dialog and requires every `SupportedFormat` in its filter and an unknown suffix refused.

- Proof: `tests/test_format_converter_contract.py` → `Ran 23 tests ... OK`; `run_audit.py` → `100% (14/14 suites passed in 127.18s)`; **22 injected defects, 22 caught by a named test** (assimp's FBX writer removed from the table, the extension passed instead of the exporter id, `.off`/`.3mf` unoffered, the preview trimesh-only again, the FBX header gate removed, options ignored by trimesh, a skipped option unreported, `library_used` fabricated, the LOD chain exported straight through trimesh, the bare-filename `makedirs`, a refusal that does not name the format, `converter.convert()` restored, the `PATH` bootstrap disabled, `pyassimp` un-declared, the DLL not collected, the binding not imported, `*.fbx` out of the filter again, the picker not consulting the converter, a greyed-out target left selected, the Model dialog hiding OFF/3MF, it accepting `.x3d`, and its filter landing in the directory slot). One was caught by a *different* named test than intended - passing the extension instead of the id is caught by `test_01`, which reaches it first - so it is counted, not hidden.
- **Not covered here:** the build recipe is checked as *text*, not by running PyInstaller - a real bundle was never produced on this machine, so "the packaged app converts FBX" is argued from the spec plus the `_MEIPASS` path arithmetic, not observed. `pip install -r requirements.txt` was not run either; the pins are what this machine already has. Whether a genuine FBX *opens* in Blender/Unity/Maya is unverifiable here by definition - `test_05` proves a second independent library reads it, which is the strongest statement available without a DCC. And the Model tab's batch dialog still lists all nine targets; the backend refuses with a real reason, but that picker does not grey anything out the way the Preprocessing one now does.

### Phase 6a - the Automation tab watches real folders

The File Watcher pane was theatre, and in six places at once.

It opened with three folders nobody selected - `D:/Projects/Models/Input` ("Auto Import + Validate", Active), `D:/Dropbox/Shared/Models` ("Auto Convert to OBJ", Active), `D:/Temp/Downloads` ("Auto Cleanup Old Files", Paused) - written into the table by `populate_sample_watches()` with the status column as literal text. `start_file_watching()` printed "File watching started..." and started `QTimer().start(5000)` on `simulate_file_event()`, which picked one of four hard-coded strings (`"File created: new_model.obj"`, `"File deleted: old_model.dae"`, `"File moved: model.gltf -> archive/"`) and stamped it with the current time. `add_watch_folder()` opened a real folder dialog and wrote the answer into the table - and nothing else. `self.file_watcher = FileWatcher()` was constructed at `:76` and its three signals connected at `:89-91`; **no method in the tab ever called `watch_directory()` or `start_watching()`**, so the object existed only so `update_dashboard_metrics` could count its empty dict. `remove_watch_btn` and `stop_watching_btn` had no `.connect()` anywhere in the file. And `automation_engine.py:447-449` declared `file_created` / `file_modified` / `file_deleted` signals that **no line in the engine emitted** - the loop called python callbacks only - so even a correct UI could not have reached the slots.

**`import datetime` at `automation_tab.py:7`, with eight `datetime.now()` call sites.** `datetime.now()` under that import is an `AttributeError`, and it fired on the first statement of eight slots: `on_file_created`, `on_file_modified`, `on_file_deleted`, `on_task_executed`, `on_task_failed`, `on_workflow_started`, `on_workflow_completed_engine`, `on_workflow_failed`. The visible consequences were worse than missing log lines: in `on_workflow_completed_engine` and `on_workflow_failed` the `QMessageBox.information` / `.critical` sat **after** the raising line, so the "Workflow completed!" and "Workflow failed: <error>" dialogs never appeared, and `on_task_executed` / `on_task_failed` wrote nothing because the raise happened before the append. Only `simulate_file_event` used the correct `datetime.datetime.now()` - the one function that was faking it. Fixed to `from datetime import datetime`; the eight call sites are untouched.

**`src/features/automation_engine.py:1036-1096`** - the vocabulary the pane needed, none of which existed: `WATCH_EVENTS`; `_glob_list()` accepts `*.obj, *.fbx`, `obj,fbx`, `.obj` and space/comma/semicolon/pipe mixes, drops bare `*` and `*.*`, expands a token with no dot to `*.ext`; `_name_matches()` where an empty include list means *everything*; `_is_excluded()` where an empty exclude list excludes *nothing*; `_event_set()` for str / `'any'` / `'all'` / collection. The old filter was `filename.endswith(pattern)`, which matched **no** file for the `*.obj` style the UI displays, and there was no exclude or size support at all.

`FileWatcher` (`:1098`) is now a polling watcher that can be driven from a test. `watch_directory()` (`:1118`) raises `ValueError` for an empty or non-directory path instead of registering it, keys on `os.path.abspath` (a Windows `AYUSHM~1` short path and its long form are one folder), updates the filters when a folder is re-registered, accepts `callback=None` because the Qt signal is the primary channel, and **takes the baseline synchronously on the calling thread**: `info['last_modified'] = self._snapshot(path, info)`. Recording it on the thread's first pass, as the old loop did, swallows every file that lands between the Start click and that pass - and the first pass runs after `time.sleep(self.poll_seconds)`. `_snapshot()` (`:1181`) applies include/exclude and the `min_size_kb` floor. `_diff()` (`:1202`) pairs a (mtime, size) that vanished from one path and appeared at another in the same pass into a single `moved` event - the only distinction a polling watcher can make, and the tab's "File moved" line depends on it - then reports deleted, created, modified. `_watch_loop()` (`:1258`) iterates a `list()` copy of the dict, skips a folder that stopped being a directory, and wraps each folder so one unreadable path cannot kill the thread. `_report()` (`:1276`) finds the owning folder, **gates on the set of events that folder asked for**, emits the signal, then runs the callbacks each in their own `try` so a broken callback cannot stop the others or the loop. The old loop had a gate too, but on a single string - `info['event_type'] in ['created', 'any']` - so the pane's four Watch Events checkboxes had no representation, a folder could watch created *or* modified but never both, **deleted files were never looked for at all** (the loop only iterated the files that exist, so `file_deleted` could not fire even in principle), and there was no move detection. `stop_watching()` (`:1174`) also clears `self._watcher_thread`; `is_watched()` (`:1153`) is the abspath-normalising predicate the tab and tests use. `create_trigger`'s `FILE_WATCH` branch now raises `ValueError` for a missing directory rather than registering a folder that does not exist.

**`src/ui/automation_tab.py`** - the pane drives that engine and nothing else. Deleted: `populate_sample_watches()` and its call, `simulate_file_event()`, and the fake `QTimer` that drove it - the Stop Watching button had no handler of any kind, so `stop_file_watching()` is new code rather than a repaired one. `add_watch_folder()` (`:2285`) de-dupes against rows already present, writes the pane's own `file_extensions_edit` value into the filter column instead of the literal `"*.*"`, and registers live if watching is already running. `_watch_settings()` (`:2333`) reads the four Watch Events checkboxes into the event list, the exclude field and the min-size spin, and `_register_watch()` (`:2351`) hands them to `watch_directory`; `start_file_watching()` (`:2362`) refuses when the table is empty or no event is checked - both of which would otherwise "start" a watcher that reports nothing - marks a row "Missing folder" on `ValueError` instead of claiming it, and logs `Watching N folder(s) for created, modified (filter *.obj, *.fbx, over 50 KB; auto actions: ...)`. `stop_file_watching()` (`:2397`) and `remove_watch_folder()` (`:2320`) are connected and reach the engine. The event slots (`:1555-1569`) log through `_log_watch_event()` with a real `datetime.now()` and then call `_run_auto_actions(path)`; `on_file_moved` logs both paths.

The Auto Actions checkboxes are what the pane promised to run, and none of them ran anything. `_auto_action_settings()` (`:1582`) snapshots every checkbox and path **on the main thread** and passes the dict to a daemon worker; `_perform_auto_actions()` (`:1619`) works only on that dict and its file paths, and reports back through `auto_actions_finished(str, list)` so the results land in the same log, indented. The actions are the real ones: `_auto_import()` (`:1645`) gates on `SUPPORTED_MODEL_EXTS` and calls the same `LibraryManager.import_files()` the Library tab calls, distinguishing added from updated from refused; `_auto_validate()` (`:1665`) runs `QualityAnalyzer().analyze_quality` and prints `"<score>/100 grade <X>, N issue(s)"`; `_auto_thumbnail()` (`:1677`) claims a file only if it can `stat` it; `_auto_backup()` (`:1687`) copies with `shutil.copy2` into `<parent>/.renderfox_backups` and verifies the size; `_auto_run_script()` (`:1699`) runs `subprocess.run([python, script, file_path], capture_output=True, text=True, timeout=300)`, resolves the interpreter as `sys.executable`, or `shutil.which('python')` when frozen, and prints the exit code. Nothing here can raise into the watcher thread: every action returns a sentence.

**`src/core/library_manager.py`** - two things. `refresh_thumbnail(model_path)` (`:997`) is the public wrapper for `_ensure_thumbnail_placeholder(..., force=True)` that the auto-thumbnail action needs; there was no way to ask for a rebuild from outside the class. And an `import_files()` counting bug found while writing `test_18`: `_add_or_update_model` returned `was_update = row["file_hash"] != file_hash`, i.e. "the content changed", so re-importing a file that was already indexed unchanged was reported as **added** - every dashboard "New Models" figure and the Library tab's "N added, M updated" message was wrong for the common case of a folder scan that includes known files. The return contract is now `(recorded, row_was_new)` (`:370`), and `import_files()` (`:311`) and `scan()` (`:325`) count on it.

**`tests/test_automation_contract.py`** (new, 38 tests, the 15th audit suite) - `WatcherEngineCase` (14) drives the engine with a `manual()` helper that pre-sets `is_watching` so `_watch_loop` is never entered, then calls `_diff(info, _snapshot(...))` and `_report(...)` directly: glob typing, exclude polarity, `ValueError` on a file / missing / empty path, a silent baseline, created reaching **both** the signal and a callback, `modified` only when asked, a rename reported as one moved event, deleted, the per-folder event gate, exclude + min-size in the same snapshot, a callback that raises, abspath dedupe and unwatch, the baseline captured at registration, and `stop_watching` really stopping the thread. `AutomationTabCase` (24) clicks the pane with `QFileDialog.getExistingDirectory` and all three `QMessageBox` statics shimmed (a modal box cannot be dismissed headlessly - the first full run hung at `test_24` on `on_workflow_failed`'s critical box): `receivers()` on every button and signal; the table starts empty **and** the source contains none of `populate_sample_watches`, `simulate_file_event`, `D:/Dropbox/Shared`; both refusals; add/de-dupe/filter column; the pane's settings reaching the engine (`file_pattern == ['*.obj', '*.fbx']`, `min_size_kb == 5.0`, `events == {'created', 'modified'}`, row "Watching"); a missing folder marked and not claimed; button and row state in both directions; remove clearing engine + table + request dict; a real watcher thread delivering "File created ... hero.obj" to the log; an unchecked event leaving no line; a rename logging "File moved"; the dashboard card; the settings snapshot; no boxes checked yielding `[]`; backup bytes matching; import then re-import of the same file reaching the DB as added-then-updated; a non-model refused with `get_models() == []` to prove nothing was indexed; a real `/100 grade` string; a thumbnail claim only for a written file; a script returning 0, one returning 3, and a path that is not a file; and the worker results surfacing in the log.

- Proof: `tests/test_automation_contract.py` -> `Ran 38 tests in 3.491s / OK`; `run_audit.py` -> `100% (15/15 suites passed in 132.71s)`; **25 injected defects, 25 caught by a named test** (M01 signals never fire, only callbacks - `test_05`; M02 a folder reports events it was not asked for - `test_09`; M03 a rename is a delete plus a create again - `test_07`; M04 edits to an existing file go unnoticed - `test_06`; M05 the min file size is ignored - `test_10`; M06 the exclude field is ignored - `test_10`; M07 a typed extension stops matching - `test_01`; M08 an empty exclude list excludes everything - `test_02`; M09 the baseline left to the thread, so files already on disk arrive as created - `test_04`; M10 watching a folder that is not there is accepted - `test_03`; M11 stop watching does not stop - `test_14`; M12 the same folder written two ways becomes two watches - `test_12`; M13 a callback failure stops the report - `test_05`; M14 the Stop Watching button is dead again - `test_01`; M15 the Watch Events boxes are ignored - `test_04`; M16 starting with nothing listed still claims a folder - `test_03`; M17 auto backup writes an empty file and calls it copied - `test_17`; M18 a script that exits non-zero is reported as ok - `test_22`; M19 the auto import takes any file - `test_19`; M20 auto import always says added - `test_18`; M21 removing a folder leaves it running in the watcher - `test_09`; M22 the fake event simulator comes back - `test_02`; M23 the File Extensions field never reaches the watcher - `test_06`; M24 the datetime import goes back to the broken form - `test_24`; M25 the sample watch folders come back - `test_02`). M01+M13, M05+M06, M22+M25 and the two refusal tests are reached in pairs; each is named, none is hidden. `automation_engine.py` and `automation_tab.py` restored byte-for-byte (sha256 verified) and the scratch runner deleted.
- **Not covered here:** whether a real DCC drops files the way these tests simulate. The harness writes a file once and the next pass sees one `created` event; an application that saves by writing `hero.obj.tmp` and renaming lands as `created hero.obj` only if the poll misses the temp file, and as `created hero.obj.tmp` plus a `moved` - two runs of the auto actions - if it does not. Nothing here observes a real save. Thumbnail *content* is checked as "a file exists at the path the code returned", not as "the image shows the model", because the renderer needs OpenGL and this machine has no GPU path for it. The script action is proven with a python file; a `.bat` or a Blender `.py` that imports `bpy` would behave differently and is not exercised. And `sys.executable` vs `shutil.which('python')` under a PyInstaller bundle is argued from the code, not run - the packaged app was never built here.

### Phase 6b - the Scheduled Tasks pane schedules

Six fictions at once, and the pane asked for a seventh.

`populate_sample_tasks()` wrote five rows nobody created (`Nightly Backup`, `Weekly Report Generation`, `Auto Cleanup Temp Files`, `Model Validation Batch`, `Thumbnail Generation`) with statuses as literal text. Save Task read the fields, appended a row and said `"Task 'X' scheduled successfully"` - no engine call. `create_task_btn`, `edit_task_btn`, `delete_task_btn`, `enable_task_btn`, `disable_task_btn`, `test_task_btn`, `run_now_btn` had **no `.connect()` anywhere in the file**, so seven of the pane's controls did nothing at all. The Type combo listed seven invented names (`"Batch Processing"`, `"File Cleanup"`, `"Backup"`, ...) that `ActionRegistry` has never contained, so even a working executor could not have resolved one. `self.task_scheduler = TaskScheduler()` was built in `__init__` and **nothing ever called `start_scheduler()`** - and `AutomationEngine.start()` had no caller either, so no schedule in the app could fire on any clock. The parameters box went to `eval()`.

**What `schedule` 1.2.2 actually does, measured rather than assumed.** `every().week.at(clock)` raises `ScheduleValueError` (`.at()` is valid on days/hours/minutes only), so the weekly spec the pane composes is `every(7).days.at(clock)`. There is no month unit at all, so Monthly is composed from days and clamped with `calendar.monthrange`, which is what keeps a `monthly@09:00` saved on the 31st from vanishing in February. `schedule.every(0)` **never returns** - it loops inside `_schedule_next_run()` - so `every_0_hours` is refused before the library is called. And `Job.run()` has no `try`/`except`: it calls `_schedule_next_run()` only *after* `job_func()` returns, while `Scheduler.run_pending()` walks a sorted job list with no per-job guard, so one callback that raises leaves its job permanently due, first in every pass, **starving every other schedule in the app**. Two consequences are in the code: `callback = self._guarded(task_id, callback)` (`automation_engine.py:904`) and `self._jobs = schedule.Scheduler()` (`:891`) instead of the module default, because the default is a global that a second engine would share.

**`src/features/automation_engine.py`.** `app_data_dir()` (`:50`) and `_tasks_file()` (`:1607`) put the record in `~/.renderfox/tasks.json`; it was relative to the current directory, so the same task list meant a different file depending on how the app was launched, and the released one-file build had none. `create_task` (`:1654`) and `update_task` (`:1675`) are separate now - the old single method overwrote fields the caller did not pass. The id retry loop walks a millisecond stamp forward (`while task_id in self.tasks: stamp += 1`); the old one rebuilt the same string from `len(self.tasks)`, which only changes once the task is stored, so a second collision looped forever. `schedule_task` (`:1729`) refuses a spec it cannot honour instead of returning True, then marks `TaskStatus.SCHEDULED` and saves. `_load_tasks` (`:1619`) re-arms each enabled task and, when the re-arm is refused because a `once:` moment already passed, marks it `CANCELLED` rather than handing back a task that looks due forever. `enabled` is persisted and read (`from_dict`), and `set_task_enabled` / `delete_task` / `next_run` reach the scheduler. `system.run_command` returns the real exit code and the child's own output; `processing.generate_lod` (`:270`) now calls `LODGenerator(LODSettings(levels=levels)).generate_lods(...)` - the inventory's "returns 'Generated N LODs' without calling `LODGenerator`" is gone - and `processing.bake_texture` (`:247`) calls `TextureBaker`.

**`src/ui/automation_tab.py`.** Deleted `populate_sample_tasks`, `load_tasks`, `create_scheduled_task`, `on_task_executed`, `on_task_failed` and the orphan scheduler. The Type combo is built from `ActionRegistry.get_all_actions()` through `_task_action_choices()` (`:2076`), so a name in the list is a name the engine resolves. `_parse_parameters()` (`:2084`) tries `json.loads` then `ast.literal_eval` over `raw` and `'{' + raw + '}'` and returns a *sentence* when neither yields a dict - nothing is evaluated. `_validate_parameters()` (`:2105`) checks the registry's own spec: missing required names, wrong types, and names the action does not take. `_task_schedule_spec()` (`:2109`) composes exactly the vocabulary `TaskScheduler` documents (`hourly`, `daily@09:00`, `every_5_hours`, `once:2026-11-06T09:00`) and refuses a one-time moment that has already gone. `refresh_task_table()` (`:2143`) rebuilds from the engine, including the Next Run column read from `next_run()` and the last verdict from the task's own `result`/`error`. Save updates when `current_task_id` is a live task instead of duplicating it. Run Now executes on a worker thread and reports through `task_started` / `task_completed`; the widget is only touched on the main thread. `automation_engine.start()` is called in `__init__`, with the comment naming why. The pane opens on `Daily` because `"One Time"` beside today's date is a moment that has usually already passed, which would make the pane refuse its own Save click.

**The last `eval()` in the pane went with it.** `run_workflow` handed the steps table's parameter cell to `eval(f"{{{step_params}}}")` (`:1135`), so a workflow file imported from anywhere ran arbitrary python with the app's rights. It calls `_parse_parameters` now and refuses the run, naming the step, when a cell does not parse. That is what let `test_06` become a standing invariant: it walks the pane's syntax tree and fails on any `eval` / `exec` / `compile` call node. It needed strengthening to be worth anything - the original version only checked that a side-effecting payload was refused, and **a Windows path in that payload makes `eval()` die on the `\U` of `C:\Users` before the redirection ever happens**, so `eval()` put back at `:1135` passed all 61 tests. Mutation N21 found that; the payload that actually distinguishes is `"levels": (lambda: 3)()`.

Deleted `load_workflows()` loses nothing that ever reached a user: it read a cwd-relative `data/workflows.json` into `self.workflow_templates`, and at the baseline that dict was written at `:110` and `:1377` and **read by nothing**, while the engine's own `_workflows_file()` / `_load_workflows()` is a different path and the pane's list comes from hard-coded names. The 6c fix is the engine's, not a second loader in the tab.

**`main.py`** built a **second** `AutomationEngine` and called `engine.start()` (`:142-143`) next to the tab's. While the scheduler was a decoration that cost nothing; with a real one, two live instances read the same `tasks.json` and each gives every task its own calendar, so a nightly copy would run twice a night and each run would rewrite the record. The launcher registers actions and the tab owns the engine now. The registrations also move *before* `MainWindow()`: the tab fills its task-type list while it constructs, so on the old ordering `pcd.load` ... `pcd.reconstruct.poisson` were registered too late to ever appear in the pane - on any start.

**`requirements.txt`** - `schedule==1.2.2`, pinned for the four measured behaviours above, and `import schedule` at `automation_engine.py:17` is now on the path that runs at startup rather than in a class nobody instantiated. `RenderFox.spec` needs no `hiddenimports` entry: the module is imported by name from a module PyInstaller already reaches.

**`tests/test_automation_tasks_contract.py`** (new, 62 tests, the 16th audit suite) - `SchedulerCase` (14) drives `TaskScheduler` with no Qt: every spec the pane composes is accepted by the library, `every_0_hours` and an unknown unit are refused, a past one-off is refused, `next_run` is a real moment, **two schedulers do not share one job list**, a job fires on the polling thread, a one-off removes itself, a monthly job clamps the short month and re-arms, a callback that raises does not starve the others, re-scheduling replaces the old job. `EngineTaskCase` (16) puts `app_data_dir` at a temp home and checks the record lands where the app looks, that a refused schedule is not claimed, that the actions verdict decides the status, that a disabled task stays disabled **and off the calendar across a restart**, that a missed one-off comes back cancelled, and that an unreadable record is skipped rather than fatal. `TaskPaneCase` (32) clicks the pane with `QMessageBox` shimmed: `receivers()` on every control, the table starts empty and the source contains none of the sample rows or `TaskScheduler()`, an unknown / mistyped / missing parameter is named, a payload is never evaluated, Run Now of a real command lands its **own printed line** in the log and a failing one reports `FAILED` with the reason, the Next Run column equals the engine's, the schedule fields enable only what their type uses, and saving twice updates instead of duplicating.

- Proof: `tests/test_automation_tasks_contract.py` -> `Ran 62 tests in 11.865s / OK`; `tests/test_automation_contract.py` (6a, unchanged) -> `Ran 38 tests in 5.271s / OK`; `run_audit.py` -> `100% (16/16 suites passed in 145.50s)`; **35 injected defects, 35 caught by a named test** - N01 the thread is never started, `SchedulerCase.test_07`; N02 `start()` a no-op, `TaskPaneCase.test_29`; N03 a refused schedule claimed anyway, `EngineTaskCase.test_03`; N04 not marked scheduled, `test_04`; N05 a failed run recorded completed, `test_07`; N06 a disabled task runs, `test_06`; N07 the file opened and never written, `test_01`; N08 `tasks.json` back to the cwd, `test_01`; N09 restored tasks never re-armed, `test_11`; N10 a missed one-off returns as still due, `test_12`; N11 `enabled` lost on save, `test_05b`; N12 an update wiping parameters it was not given, `test_09`; N13 the guard removed so a raising job reaches the loop, `SchedulerCase.test_11`; N14 the scheduler back on the module global, `test_06`; N15 `next_run` always None, `test_05`; N16 `cancel_job` a no-op, `test_12`; N17 no month clamp, `test_09`; N18 a one-off left registered, `test_08`; N19 `every().week.at()`, the call this build rejects, `test_01`; N20 Save builds an id instead of a task, `TaskPaneCase.test_05`; N21 `eval()` back in the parameter parse, `test_06`; N22 the table never rebuilt, `test_04`; N23 an at-time spec losing its clock, `test_11`; N24 Run Now only popping a box, `test_17`; N25 Enable/Disable not reaching the engine, `test_21`; N26 Delete a no-op, `test_22`; N27 an unknown parameter accepted, `test_08`; N28 the invented type names back, `test_03`; N29 the dashboard counting nothing, `test_28`; N30 the Edit button dead again, `test_01`; N31 Edit restoring the name only, `test_24`; N32 every schedule field staying enabled, `test_16`; N33 a past one-time accepted, `test_13`; N34 the pane opening on a schedule it refuses, `test_04`; N35 `eval()` back at the steps table, `test_06`. Both sources restored byte-for-byte (sha256 verified) and the scratch runners deleted. N23 and N32 read as "not caught" on the first pass because the harness parsed `FAIL:` lines without the ` [subtest]` suffix unittest appends; both re-ran green against the fixed parser, and the parser now takes `Class.method` from the parenthesised id, which matters here because two cases in this suite both own a `test_01_`, `test_04_`, `test_05_`, `test_09_` and `test_11_`.
- **Not covered here:** `main.py` is never executed by a suite, so "the app starts exactly one engine and registers before the window" is enforced by `test_31` reading its source, not by launching it - the same limit as every other entry-point claim in this log. No test asks what happens when a task is still running when its interval next arrives: the polling thread fires and `execute_task` sets `RUNNING` again, so a slow job can overlap itself, and nothing here measures that. A `system.run_command` task runs through `shell=True` because that is what the action's contract says, so a command string is interpreted by `cmd.exe` on Windows - proven with real python child processes, not against a renderer. `tasks.json` is rewritten by whichever thread saves last with no lock and no atomic replace, so two saves in the same instant can interleave; only the single-engine case is exercised. The `pcd.*` actions the launcher registers are checked for *name reachability* through the combo, never executed - `PointCloudProcessor` belongs to the point-cloud inventory, not this one. And the workflow pane past `run_workflow`'s parameter cell is still 6c: `Workflow(name=, description=)` against a constructor that does not take those keywords, `WorkflowExecutor` treating `WorkflowStep` objects as dicts, `workflow_completed` emitted with three args into a two-arg signal, and no progress or cancel at all.

### Phase 6c - the Workflow Builder pane runs what it shows

Twelve controls, four of them wired, and the run path could not have worked at any depth.

`setup_connections` (`automation_tab.py:1047-1053`, as it stood) connected four things for this builder: the list's selection, New, Run and Add Step. **Save, Load, Duplicate, Delete, Test, Stop, Remove Step, Move Up and Move Down had no `.connect()` anywhere in the file.** `load_workflow_details` (`:1098`) answered a selection by writing `f"Description for {clean_name}"` (`:1106`) - an invented sentence about a workflow that does not exist - and the list itself was built by `self.workflows_list.addItems([...])` (`:214`) from five names (`Model Conversion Pipeline`, `Batch LOD Generation`, `Texture Optimization`, ...) that no engine, file or run ever held. `run_workflow` (`:1114`) disabled its own button, enabled Stop, and handed a `Workflow` **object** to `AutomationEngine.execute_workflow(workflow_id: str)` (`automation_engine.py:1228`), whose first line is `if workflow_id not in self.workflows: return False` - so the click greyed out Run, lit up Stop, and reported nothing, permanently. The step it built used `action=step_name.lower().replace(' ', '_')` (`:1138`): the step's *display name* as the registry id, which is never a registered id. `WorkflowExecutionThread` (`:34-70`) was a class nobody instantiated - `grep -c 'WorkflowExecutionThread('` returns the `class` line and nothing else - and its loop took a `# Simulation fallback` branch (`:63`) of `self.msleep(500)` for any step with no action, then emitted `workflow_completed(True, "Workflow completed successfully")` (`:67`). Back in the tab, `on_workflow_completed_engine` (`:1156`) opened `"Workflow completed!\nResults: {results}"` for whatever verdict arrived, and `on_workflow_progress` (`:1174-1178`) wrote the workflow's percentage into the **Batch Processing** tab's `batch_progress` bar and `batch_status_label` - one feature's status drawn on another feature's panel.

Under the pane the engine was wrong in a way that hid all of that. `create_workflow` (`:1212`) called `Workflow(workflow_id=..., name=..., description=..., steps=...)` against `Workflow.__init__(self, name, description="")` (`:362`) - **`TypeError` before anything was stored**, so `self.workflows` could never gain an entry through the API the pane was told to use. `WorkflowExecutor.execute_workflow` (`:842`) iterated `workflow.steps`, which holds `WorkflowStep` objects, and called `step.get('name')` (`:847`) on them: `AttributeError` on the first step, raised inside a daemon thread, so no dialog, no log line, no failure. `_execute_step` (`:883-894`) then asked each step for `task_type` while `WorkflowStep` keeps the registry id in `.action` - its own comment at `:886-889` argued about the two names instead of picking one. And `completion_callback` (`:1237`) emitted `workflow_completed(workflow_id, success, result)` into `pyqtSignal(str, dict)` (`:1009`): **three arguments into a two-argument signal**, so even a fixed executor would have died at the moment of reporting. There was no `update_workflow`, `duplicate_workflow`, `delete_workflow`, `stop_workflow` or `workflow_problems` anywhere, no progress and no cancel, and `on_error` / retry were not concepts at all - while `Workflow.variables` (`:371`) and `WorkflowStep.condition` were fields nothing ever read.

**`src/features/automation_engine.py`.** `ActionRegistry.validate(action_id, params)` (`:105`) is now the only place that knows what a parameter set means - a missing required value, a wrong type, a name the action does not take, an id nobody registered - and `execute` (`:132`) reads its complaints and refuses **before** calling anything, so the task pane, the builder and the executor cannot drift apart the way two private rule lists did. `WorkflowStep` (`:685`) owns `ON_ERROR = ('stop', 'continue', 'retry')` (`:688`), coerces an unknown policy to `stop` rather than trusting a file (`:696`), clamps `max_retries` through `max(0, int(...))` with a fallback for junk (`:697-700`), and carries `status` / `result` / `error` for the run it is in - which `to_dict` (`:705`) deliberately does **not** write, because a step's last outcome is not what the step *is*. `condition` and `Workflow.variables` are gone, not faked: a control nothing can honour is deleted, per the ground rule. `Workflow` (`:785`) keeps `current_step` and gains `error`, and `to_dict` / `from_dict` (`:810`, `:833`) round-trip every field the executor honours, which is what lets `test_13` demand that a stop verdict survive a save and a read.

`WorkflowExecutor.execute_workflow(workflow, callback, progress, should_stop)` (`:1304`) is the honest loop. An empty workflow returns `{'workflow': name, 'error': 'this workflow has no steps to run', 'success': False}` instead of running zero steps and calling that success. Each step runs in a `while True` (`:1338`) whose retry branch is `if step.on_error == 'retry' and attempts <= step.max_retries` (`:1348`) - first run plus exactly `max_retries` more, counted. `on_error == 'continue'` (`:1354`) adds `"<step>: <reason>"` to `tolerated` and carries on, and that list is in the verdict either way (`:1360`, `:1364`), so a run that went on despite two failures says so rather than reporting 4/4. `should_stop()` is asked **between** steps (`:1323`) and the answer produces `{'stopped': True, 'error': "stopped before '<name>' (step 3 of 4)", 'steps_completed': n}` with `TaskStatus.CANCELLED` (`:1325-1328`) - the step already running is allowed to finish, which is the only thing a python thread can promise without killing a renderer mid-write. Everything leaves through `_finish` (`:1367`), which sets the workflow's status and error and calls back once.

`AutomationEngine` got the API the pane needs: `create_workflow` (`:1790`) builds from step records and returns the id the workflow actually owns, `update_workflow` (`:1799`) changes only the fields it was given, `_apply_steps` (`:1816`) is the one place records become `WorkflowStep` objects, `delete_workflow` (`:1822`) stops the run before removing it, `duplicate_workflow` (`:1831`) copies through `to_dict`/`from_dict` and then replaces **both** the workflow id and every step id (`:1837`, `:1843-1844`) so the copy cannot overwrite the original's rows, and `workflow_problems(name, steps)` (`:1849`) names every reason before anything runs - no name, no steps, a step naming no action, each registry complaint per step, an `on_error` outside `ON_ERROR`. `execute_workflow(workflow_id)` (`:1870`) refuses an unknown id or an empty workflow, resets `self._stopped` (`:1879`) so a workflow stopped once is not stopped forever, and emits with the signal's real arity: `workflow_completed(workflow_id, result)` on success *and* on a stop, `workflow_failed(workflow_id, reason)` only for a failure (`:1893-1899`). `stop_workflow` (`:1906`) answers False for an id the engine does not have, so the pane cannot report a stop it did not arrange. `file.copy` and `file.move` (`:167`, `:183`) now create the destination's folder (`:158-160`, `:175-177`) - a pipeline stages into a directory it makes on the way, and `test_07`/`test_38` only pass because of it - and both verify the bytes they claim to have moved.

**The polling watcher's rename split, found by the audit and fixed here.** `_diff()` paired a (mtime, size) that vanished from one path and appeared at another *in the same pass*, so `automation_contract.py::test_13_a_rename_reaches_the_moved_line` passed most runs and failed some: at the pane's 0.05 s poll, `os.listdir` can catch a rename with the old name already gone and the new one not yet visible, and the watcher announced one user action as `File deleted: first.obj` plus, one poll later, `File created: second.obj`. A vanishing is now **held for one pass** (`:1220`, `info['pending_gone']`): held paths are matched against the next pass's appearances first (`:1228-1236`), and only a vanishing that produces no twin is announced as a deletion. The cost is stated in the docstring rather than hidden - a real deletion reaches the log one poll late - and the alternative is a log that lies about renames. Re-registering a folder clears the holds (`:1143`) so a folder re-added with new filters starts from what is on disk. `test_15_a_rename_split_across_two_passes_is_still_one_move` reproduces the split deterministically by handing `_diff` the two half-listings, which is the only way to test a race without racing it; `test_16_re_registering_a_folder_forgets_its_open_holds` pins the reset. The suite's `test_08` now asserts the *shape* of the new truth - empty after the first pass, `deleted` after the second - and `test_09` drives twice so a folder that was never asked for `deleted` cannot pass by accident. `tests/test_automation_contract.py` goes 38 -> 40 tests.

**`src/ui/automation_tab.py`.** Deleted: `WorkflowExecutionThread` (and `QThread` from the import, since nothing else used it), `load_sample_data`, the five `addItems([...])` names, the `"Description for X"` invention, `step_name.lower().replace(' ', '_')`, the pane's own second `WorkflowExecutor` (`self.workflow_executor`), the `Condition` column, and three tab-level signals - `workflow_started`, `workflow_completed`, `task_scheduled` - that were declared, connected to nothing and could never fire next to the engine's real ones (`auto_actions_finished` is kept; it has an emitter). The steps table is five columns (`:226`): `#`, `Step`, `Action`, `Parameters`, `On error`. Action (`:1213`) and On error (`:1215`) are combo cells whose items come from `ActionRegistry.get_all_actions()` and `WorkflowStep.ON_ERROR` - the backend's vocabulary, not a list typed into the UI - and `_step_record` (`:1226`) reads `currentData()` from them, so what is stored is an id and a policy, not a display string. `setup_connections` (`:1109`) wires thirteen builder controls, including the list's selection. `refresh_workflow_list` (`:1161`) rebuilds from `automation_engine.workflows` sorted by `created_at`, keeps the selection on the id you were on, and does it inside `self._rebuilding_list` because **`QListWidget.clear()` and `clearSelection()` emit `itemSelectionChanged` while `currentRow()` still returns the old row** - without the flag, Duplicate stored a copy, then the rebuild's spurious selection change reloaded row 0 and the editor went back to the original, and a second Save silently overwrote the first workflow instead of making a second. `load_workflow_details` (`:1258`) and `new_workflow` (`:1273`) both honour it. `store_workflow` (`:1286`) asks `_workflow_records` then the engine's `workflow_problems`, and refuses with that sentence; `save_workflow` (`:1312`) logs what was stored; `run_workflow` (`:1320`) stores and then calls `execute_workflow(id)` - the stored record, the same one the file holds, the same id the verdict comes back under; `test_workflow` (`:1336`) proves the order through `_workflow_records` + `workflow_problems` and runs none of it; `stop_workflow_run` (`:1352`) asks the engine about `self._running_workflow` and says "Nothing is running" when there is none; `duplicate_selected_workflow` (`:1367`), `delete_selected_workflow` (`:1380`), `remove_workflow_step` (`:1399`), `move_workflow_step` (`:1408`), `load_workflow_file` (`:1440`) and `_workflow_file_records` (`:1429`) all reach the engine, and the last refuses an importable-looking record by name instead of storing it. `_validate_parameters` (`:2105`) is three lines that delegate to `ActionRegistry.validate` - the pane keeps no private copy of the rules. The verdict slots (`:1484-1529`) write to this pane's own activity log: `"<name>" step 1/4: copy`, `completed 3/4 step(s), tolerating 1 failure(s): ...`, `stopped after 2 step(s)`, `FAILED: <reason>` - no success modal for a run that did not succeed, and nothing into the Batch tab's widgets.

**`tests/test_automation_workflows_contract.py`** (new, 52 tests, the 17th audit suite) - `RegistryCase` (1-5) the four ways a parameter set fails, each named, and `execute` refusing before calling a probe that records whether it was reached. `ExecutorCase` (6-13) drives the real executor on real files: an empty workflow says there is nothing to run, the copy lands on disk through a folder the action created, a failing step stops the run and is named, `continue` carries the failure and the run on, `retry` runs first-plus-`max_retries` and no more (counted through a probe), progress names each step before it runs, a stop asked between steps leaves the rest untouched, and the verdict survives `to_dict`/`from_dict`. `EngineWorkflowCase` (14-25) puts `app_data_dir` in a temp home and checks the id create returns is the key in `workflows.json`, update changes only what it was given, duplicate brings fresh workflow *and* step ids, delete leaves nothing in the list or the file, `workflow_problems` names every reason, the signals carry what the run really did (`progress == [(1, 1, 'copy')]`, `steps_completed == 1`, the copied bytes on disk), a failing run reaches `workflow_failed` with its reason and never `workflow_completed`, stop of an unknown id says no, and a source scan of the pane fails if `WorkflowExecutionThread`, `Simulation fallback` or `msleep(500)` comes back. `PaneCase` (26-52) clicks the builder with `QMessageBox` / `QFileDialog` shimmed: `receivers()` on all thirteen controls; the list is engine-fed and none of the five invented names, `Condition` or `Description for` appear in the builder's own code; the two combos offer registry ids and `WorkflowStep.ON_ERROR`; New detaches the stored id; Save writes a record the engine and the disk both have, including the `on_error` chosen in the table; a workflow with no steps or no name or a non-data parameter cell is refused **and stays unstored**; Test Workflow proves the order while a 0.6 s wait confirms nothing started and no file was written; Run stores, executes and the copy is on disk with `completed 1/1 step(s)` in the log and the batch widgets untouched; a failing run reports the reason with no success box; Stop reaches the engine and the steps left behind are not run; selecting a stored workflow loads what the engine holds; Move Up changes the order the engine will run, and moving past either end is refused rather than ignored; Duplicate and Delete reach list, engine and file; both refuse a workflow that was never saved; Load imports what can run and refuses by name; the dashboard card counts the engine's workflows.

- Proof: `tests/test_automation_workflows_contract.py` -> `Ran 52 tests in 5.115s / OK`; `tests/test_automation_contract.py` (6a, now 40 tests) -> `Ran 40 tests in 5.425s / OK`, three consecutive runs green; `tests/test_automation_tasks_contract.py` (6b, unchanged) -> `Ran 62 tests in 12.131s / OK`; `run_audit.py` -> `100% (17/17 suites passed in 176.71s)`. **30 injected defects, 30 caught by a named test**: W01 an unknown parameter is passed to the action - `RegistryCase.test_02`; W02 a missing required value reaches the action - `test_01`; W03 `execute` calls before reading the reasons - `test_04`; W04 retry silently becomes no retry - `ExecutorCase.test_10`; W05 the `continue` policy is ignored - `test_09`; W06 a workflow with no steps reports success - `test_06`; W07 `file.copy` cannot stage into a folder it creates - `test_07`; W08 the copy shares its step ids - `EngineWorkflowCase.test_17`; W09 `stop_workflow` says yes and tells the worker nothing - `test_23`; W10 `workflow_completed` is emitted with an empty verdict - `test_21`; W11 a failed run is announced as completed - `test_22`; W12 `update_workflow` reports success keeping the old steps - `test_16`; W13 a nameless workflow is no longer a reason - `test_19`; W14 a held vanishing is never answered - `WatcherEngineCase.test_15`; W15 a rename seen in one pass splits again - `test_07`; W16 a re-baselined folder keeps stale losses - `test_16`; W17 the baseline is not written at registration - `test_04`; W18 a folder reports events its user left unchecked - `test_09`; W19 the move signal loses the old path - `test_07`; W20 Save stores what the engine would refuse - `PaneCase.test_32`; W21 Run Workflow runs nothing - `test_38`; W22 Test Workflow really performs the steps - `test_36`; W23 Duplicate leaves the editor on the original - `test_47`; W24 Move Up and Down are decoration - `test_44`; W25 the step numbers fall behind the rows - `test_46`; W26 the rebuild guard is off and a stale row re-attaches the id - `test_30`; W27 New leaves the old steps on screen - `test_30`; W28 Load stores whatever a file holds - `test_50`; W29 the On error column is read and thrown away - `test_31`; W30 the pane forgets which run is live - `test_41`. Both sources restored byte-for-byte (sha256 verified after every injection).
- Two things the sweep said about my own tooling, not about the app. The runner only parsed `FAIL:` lines, so W10 - which makes `test_21` raise a `KeyError` on `result['success']` - read as "survived" while the test in fact went red as an *error*: `tools/mutation_check.py` now counts `FAIL` and `ERROR`. And W22 survived honestly: `test_36` checked the destination file was absent on the same tick as the click, so a worker thread that had just been handed the run could write it a moment later and the test would still pass. It now waits 0.6 s, asserts `workflow_started` never fired, `_running_workflow` is still None and `active_workflows` is empty, then looks at the disk. W26 and W28 also landed on the wrong names in my list - the guard is owned by `test_30`, the Load refusal by `test_50` - and the spec now says so instead of the tests being bent to match my guess. The harness and this spec are kept (`tools/mutation_check.py`, `tools/mutations_automation.py`) because the automation pane changes again in 6d and re-running 30 defects by hand is not something to repeat from memory.
- **Not covered here:** no renderer, converter or DCC is involved in any of this - a workflow step is `file.copy`, a probe action or `system.run_command`, so "the step already running is allowed to finish" is argued from the code and never measured against a 20-minute frame. Stop is checked **between** steps only: a step that hangs cannot be interrupted, and nothing here pretends otherwise. Retry has no delay and no backoff, so on Windows a retry burst against a file a renderer still holds open can exhaust all its attempts in microseconds. Two runs of the same workflow id share one `Workflow` object and its step statuses, so the pane's disabled Run button is the only thing preventing an overlap - the engine does not refuse one. `workflows.json` is rewritten whole with no lock and no atomic replace, and the app does not notice if another process edits it. A workflow file whose steps name `blender.render`-style actions is refused only if the id is not in the registry; a registered action that needs a renderer will be accepted here and fail at run time. `Completed Today`, the Active Processes table and the monitor metrics labels are still decoration - they belong to 6d. And the pane's Load accepts the app's own export shape and a bare list, which is what `test_50`/`test_51` cover; a workflow file from outside the app with `steps: []` and no `name` is refused by sentence, not repaired by guess.

### Phase 6d - the Batch Operations pane runs a batch of real files

Nine operation names in a combo, five source types, and nothing anywhere in the app that could have run one file.

`start_batch_operation` (`automation_tab.py:1636`, as it stood at HEAD) read the source line, enabled Pause and Stop, then asked `if self.batch_progress_value >= 100` (`:1646`) and called `self.batch_timer.stop()` (`:1647`) - **neither name is assigned anywhere in HEAD**, only those two reads exist - so every click with a path typed in raised `AttributeError`, and the only click that came back clean was the one with an empty box. No code anywhere called `process_batch`: the pane built its own `BatchProcessor` at `:45` and listened to that at `:62-63`, so even a fixed Start would have run on the engine's processor and told the bar nothing. `operation_combo` was filled with nine names (`:313-317` - Format Conversion, LOD Generation, Texture Optimization, Model Validation, Metadata Extraction, Thumbnail Generation, File Organization, Quality Analysis, Custom Script) and no registered action answered to any of them; `source_type_combo` had five (`:297-299`) and three of them (File List, Search Results, Selected Models) had no way to be filled. Pause (`:436`), Stop (`:440`) and Preview (`:444`) were built, added to the layout (`:448-450`) and never connected - `setup_connections` touched Start (`:1027`), the operation combo (`:1026`) and the two Browse buttons (`:1028-1029`) and nothing else for this tab. "Parallel Processing" and "Max Threads" (`:348-359`), "Target Format" with Preserve Materials and Optimize Geometry (`:372-384`) and "LOD Levels" with "Reduction Factor" (`:393-403`) were read by `update_operation_params` (`:1622`) into nothing at all.

The accounting under the pane would have hidden every failure that did happen. HEAD's `process_batch` stamped `'success': True` on any item whose callable returned without raising, and every registry action answers `{'success': False, 'error': ...}` instead of raising - so a batch in which all twelve files failed reported twelve successes, `batch_completed` carried only `results` and `total`, and the class had no stop and no pause in it. `on_batch_progress` (`:1609`) divided by `total` with no guard, and `on_batch_completed_engine` (`:1613-1620`) printed "Batch operation completed!" over whatever the summary actually said.

**`src/features/automation_engine.py` - the registry decides what may be repeated.** `register` (`:78`) gained a `batch` argument and `_batch_specs` (`:72`) keeps it, so an action states how a batch hands it one file: `file.copy` and `file.move` bind `('source', 'destination')` (`:167`, `:183`), `file.delete` binds `('path', None)` (`:199`), `system.run_command` binds `('command', None)` (`:219`) and `processing.generate_lod` binds `('input_file', 'output_dir')` (`:274`). `processing.bake_texture` is deliberately left unbound - it needs a high-poly and a low-poly in the same call, so "run it over a folder" has no meaning - and `batch_actions()` (`:610`) consequently does not offer it, which `test_01` and `test_35` hold shut. `bind_batch_params(action_id, item, output_dir, params)` (`:631`) is the only place that turns one file into the exact kwargs or the reason there are none: an unknown or unbound action, a path that is not a file right now (`:642`), a command with no `{file}` in it (`:650`), a path with a space quoted so the shell sees one argument and not two (`:653`), a writing action with no folder chosen (`:658`), a folder that is not a folder (`:661`), a copy that would land on its own source (`:675`), and then `cls.validate` for the parameters (`:679`). It touches no disk, which is what lets Preview and the real run share one answer instead of holding two opinions. `BatchProcessor` (`:1378`) now owns the loop: `request_stop` (`:1402`), `set_paused` (`:1407`) and `_hold` (`:1411`), where asking to stop cuts a pause short so a paused run cannot wedge the Stop button. `process_batch` (`:1416`) emits progress **before** each item, records a refusal (`success is False`) as a failed file with the action's own reason, keeps a raised exception as one failed file rather than a dead loop, and in `finally` builds `total / ran / succeeded / failed / not_run / stopped`, clears the claim and emits that summary - so what the pane prints is arithmetic over what the loop did. `run_batch` (`:1966`) claims the processor and hands `_run_batch` (`:1978`) to a thread; `stop_batch` (`:1987`) and `pause_batch` (`:1994`) answer False when nothing is running, so the pane cannot report a stop it did not arrange. `engine.stop()` (`:2019`) now asks the batch to stop and joins the threads it started, which `_spawn` (`:1953`) has kept since `WORKER_SHUTDOWN_WAIT` (`:1532`). That last piece came out of the suite dying at exit 139: a daemon thread posting `batch_completed` into an event loop whose test case has already been collected is an access violation on Windows, not a Python error, and no assertion ever fires. `test_29b` now pins the rule the product needed - three files of two seconds each, stop asked after the first, and `stop()` owes the file in hand and nothing behind it.

**`src/ui/automation_tab.py` - the pane offers only what the backend honours.** The private processor is gone and the pane listens to the engine's (`:62-64`). `create_batch_operations_tab` (`:292`) keeps a source combo of the two things it can actually fill (Folder, Model Library) and an operation combo built from `batch_actions()` (`:1725`). `_batch_hint` (`:1732`) is written from the binding, so the sentence names the parameters the action will really be called with, and `_update_batch_controls` (`:1757`) disables the folder fields for a library source, the output fields for an action that writes no file, and the overwrite box except where one named file lands (`binding[1] == 'destination'`) - so a folder-filling action like `generate_lod` never offers "replace what is there". `_batch_extensions` (`:1772`) reads a blank filter as the formats the app already knows and `*` as every file; `_walk_batch_folder` (`:1786`) prunes `.renderfox_backups` so a batch cannot feed on its own backups; `collect_batch_items` (`:1800`) checks each library row with `os.path.isfile` before believing it and says which rows went missing. `_batch_plan` (`:1831`) is what Preview prints **and** what Start runs: every file through `bind_batch_params`, then the overwrite check, then up to 200 `file -> target` lines and up to 50 `cannot run - <reason>` lines. `_batch_operation` (`:1937`) reads the switches once at the start, takes the backup the plan promised, and calls `ActionRegistry.execute`. `start_batch_operation` (`:1962`) refuses a plan with anything blocked instead of running the half that works, warns when the engine says no, and otherwise prints what it started; `on_batch_completed_engine` (`:2032`) writes one line per file with the action's own verdict (`_batch_verdict` `:2021`) under a header that counts successes, failures and the files that never ran. Deleted for good: the nine operation names, three source types, the parallel/threads pair, the format group, the LOD group, `update_operation_params`, the `batch_progress_value` crash path and the log's placeholder text. *(6d's rule was "delete what cannot tell the truth". The user's rule of 2026-11-05 is "write the backend instead" - those deletions are now debt, listed in the Phase 9 section, and get rebuilt.)* Parallel processing was deleted rather than faked: the mesh loaders are not reentrant, so two threads sharing one MeshSets is a crash and not a speedup - a batch is one thread, and the pane says which file it is on.

**`tests/test_automation_batch_contract.py`** (new, 80 tests, the 18th audit suite) - `BindingCase` 1-14 asks the registry rather than the pane: only one-file actions are offered, the description travels with the action, the file goes into the parameter that takes it, an unbound action says why, and no folder chosen / a folder that does not exist / a copy onto itself / a command without `{file}` / a path with a space / a parameter the action does not take are each refused **by name**, with a thousand bindings writing nothing. `ProcessorCase` 15-27 drives the real loop over real files: every item reaches the callable, a refusal is a failed file and not a successful one, a refusal with no reason still says which file it was, a raised exception is one failed file and not a dead loop, the summary counts what ran, progress names each file before it runs, an old-style callback still arrives, stop bites at the next boundary, pause holds and a resume finishes, a stop asked while paused ends the run, and the processor is free again afterwards. `EngineRunCase` 28-32 runs on a thread, refuses a second batch while one is going and refuses to start an empty one, with 29b above. `PaneCase` 33-80 clicks the tab: `receivers()` on every batch control, the operation list is the registry's own, an unbound action cannot crash the plan, the hint names the real parameters, enablement
