follows the source and the binding, the filter and the recursive switch decide which files are collected, the backup folder is never offered as input, a ghost source says so, Preview prints the exact calls and writes nothing, a real copy / move / delete batch lands on disk with the bytes read back, an existing file is refused unless overwrite was on at the start, the bar tracks the real count, stop starts no more files, pause holds the next file, the setup closes while files are being written, a second start is refused and announced as refused, progress with no total leaves the bar alone, and both Browse buttons put the chosen folder where it is used.

- Proof: `python -X utf8 tests/test_automation_batch_contract.py` -> `Ran 80 tests in 22.502s / OK`, exit 0; `tests/test_automation_contract.py` (6a) and `tests/test_automation_workflows_contract.py` (6c) both still `OK`; `run_audit.py` -> `Overall Health Score: 100% (18/18 suites passed in 238.39s)`, exit 0. **48 injected defects, 48 caught by a named test**, both sources restored byte-for-byte (sha256 verified after every injection) - `tools/mutations_automation_batch.py`.
- Four things the first pass of the sweep said, none of them about the app. **[07]** `test_05` only looked for the words "output folder", which both guards' sentences contain, so deleting the "no folder was chosen" check looked harmless; the test now requires the reason to name the action and say *chosen*, and `test_06` requires "is not a folder". **[28]** nothing asserted the overwrite box's enabled state at all - `test_38` checked the two output fields and let the box say whatever it liked. **[39]** the backup line sits *after* the overwrite refusal, so `test_66` could never reach it; `test_77` now drives both directions with no conflict in the way. **[36]** was the interesting one: making the plan ignore a bind refusal dereferences `None`, which raises inside a Qt slot, and PyQt6 aborts the process there (exit `0xC0000409`) instead of failing a test - so the defect "survived" with a silent run and no report line. `test_78`/`test_79` now call `preview_batch()` directly, and `tools/mutation_check.py` sorts a suite that dies before reporting into its own CRASHED list and fails on it, alongside SURVIVED. This harness has now found a hole in my own method twice, not once.
- **Not covered here:** no renderer, converter or DCC is involved - a batch file is `file.copy`, `file.move`, `file.delete`, `system.run_command` or a probe, so the promise that "the file in hand finishes before the batch halts" is argued from the code and never measured against a forty-minute export. The engine is still not on the shutdown path: `main_window.py:429-452` asks only about `job_manager.is_processing`, and nothing in the product calls `automation_engine.stop()`, so a batch running when the window closes is cut mid-file - the same question the Monitor pane has to answer, so 6e owns it. `BatchProcessor.current_batch` (`:1430`) is created with `done: 0` and `done` is never advanced, so an outside reader would see a run that never progresses; the Monitor pane needs that number and 6e takes it. `Completed Today`, the Active Processes table and the metrics labels are still decoration (6e). A batch is one thread on purpose, so "process 200 models in parallel" is not a claim this app can make yet. The plan is fixed at Start: files added to the folder while a batch runs are not picked up, and a file that appears in the output folder after Preview is refused at its own turn by the same check rather than overwritten in silence. The suite prints the registry's own "Registered action:" line for every default action, because `register_default_actions()` runs at import - that noise belongs to the audit-dirt item, not to this phase.
- **New item found while choosing the backend, not fixed here.** The other batch implementation, `src/features/smart_batch_processor.py`, is the reason this pane was pointed at `ActionRegistry` instead: `_execute_single_task` (`:373`) sets `task.status = 'completed'` (`:395`) whatever the handler returned, `_convert_format` (`:435`) comments `# Would save to new format here` (`:440`), runs `pass` (`:441`) and returns `{'format': output_format, 'success': True}` (`:443`), and `_optimize_mesh` (`:445`) answers `{'operations': operations_performed, 'success': True}` (`:466`) after doing nothing to the mesh. It is reachable in the shipping app: `src/ui/preprocessing_tab.py:19` builds `smart_batch_processor_ui.py:21`, the Pre-Processing tab is created at `main_window.py:113`, and `library_tab.py:2735` opens it. That is a whole pane of claimed conversions that write no files, and it needs the same treatment as 6d - its own phase, not a paragraph inside this one. **Deleted in Phase 6f**, with the two ways it was reachable.

### Phase 6e - the Monitor pane reports what it can actually see

Four status cards, a table of "Active Processes", a Performance tab and an error log - and almost none of it came from the engine.

At HEAD `create_monitor_tab` filled the Activity list with six invented lines and then cleared it in the same breath; Active Processes was built with three hardcoded rows (`WF001 Model Conversion Pipeline 65%`, `BT002`, `TS003`) that no engine ever produced; the Performance tab was four fixed strings (`CPU Usage: 45%`, `Memory: 2.1 GB`, `Disk I/O: 15 MB/s`, `Active Threads: 8`) beside a label saying charts "would be displayed here"; Errors & Warnings opened on "Errors: 2 / Warnings: 5" over seven invented failures. `refresh_processes_btn`, `kill_process_btn`, `process_details_btn`, `clear_log_btn` and `export_log_btn` were all built and never connected, and `update_dashboard_metrics` wrote three of the four cards - `completed_today_label` was created and never written, so it stayed on "0" for the life of the app.

The truth the pane needed did not exist yet, so the backend gained it rather than the pane inventing it. `BatchProcessor.current_batch` (`automation_engine.py:1430`) is now `total / done / item / started_at`, with `item` set as each file is taken (`:1437`), `done` advanced when it finishes (`:1451`) and the whole record cleared in `finally` (`:1460`) so a finished batch cannot stay on screen as a run in progress. `Workflow` had no start time at all; it now carries `started_at` (`:797`, in `to_dict` `:820`, read back `:844`) and `execute_workflow` stamps it (`:1883`). Nothing in the app ever counted a finished run, so the engine owns date-keyed counters: `_stats_file` / `_load_stats` / `_note_finished` / `completed_today` (`:1573-1600`), one lock so two threads closing together lose neither count, called from a task (`:1725`), a workflow (`:1891`) and a batch (`:1982`) - and a batch counts as completed only when every file ran and every one of them said yes. `live_work()` (`:2006`) names each kind of run a close would cut off, `stop()` (`:2019`) asks the batch to stop and joins the threads it started, and `main_window.py:447` now asks the user before cutting any of it.

**`src/ui/automation_tab.py` - only what the engine says is happening.** `_row`/`_process_rows` (`:911`, `:918`) build a row for a RUNNING workflow, a RUNNING task, or the live batch and for nothing else; a batch is named `_batch_run_name` = `"<action> over N file(s)"` (`:1989`) so two runs are tellable apart. `_eta_text` (`:948`) refuses to guess - `unknown` before anything has finished or without a recorded start, `finishing` at the last unit, otherwise the run's own pace - and `_started_text` (`:963`) says `unknown` rather than inventing a clock. `rebuild_process_table` (`:968`) puts the selected row back after a rebuild through `_process_keys`, which is what makes Kill and Details act on the run you were looking at. `kill_process` (`:996`) reaches `stop_batch`/`stop_workflow`, reports a run that finished under the click, and for a task states the reason it cannot be cancelled instead of pretending; `show_process_details` (`:1019`) prints the record it has. `refresh_metrics` (`:1038`) and `_disk_traffic_text` (`:1060`) read psutil for **this process** and use honest words where the system gives nothing: `measuring` for the first CPU sample, `could not be read`, `could not be counted`, `not reported by this system`. `_record_problem` (`:1075`) is the only way a line enters Errors & Warnings, and the counts are arithmetic over that list, with six real call sites (`:1519`, `:1545`, `:1580`, `:1979`, `:2045`, `:2062`); `_log_activity` (`:1548`) trims at 200 lines; `update_dashboard_metrics` (`:2409`) writes all four cards from engine state and refreshes the table; the five dead buttons are connected at `:1133-1137`. Deleted: the invented activity lines, the three hardcoded rows, the four fixed performance strings, the seven invented failures, the "would be displayed here" label, and the pane's own `active_workflows` dict - a workflow that was only saved is not a run.

**Two 6c tests were pinning the old shape, and one of them found a real bug.** `test_52` asserted that a name written into the pane's dict moves the Active Workflows card, which is exactly the behaviour 6e removes; `test_36`/`test_38` asserted that dict empties. All three now read the card against the engine (`tests/test_automation_workflows_contract.py:661`, `:695`, `:884-895`). `test_13_the_verdict_survives_a_save_and_a_read` failed for a different reason: `Workflow.from_dict` re-dated `created_at` on every read, so a workflow's own record never round-tripped - a saved workflow silently got a new birthday each time `workflows.json` was loaded. `Workflow._stamp` (`automation_engine.py:825`) now restores both timestamps from the record and falls back to this moment only when the record has none. The first version of this fix was not proven: when the injected defect was swept, `test_13` passed anyway, because the mutation and the record were written inside the same clock tick and their `isoformat()` strings matched - the test had been passing by luck all along. It now writes known dates into the record and asks for them back (`tests/test_automation_workflows_contract.py:232-233`, `:242-243`), so the defect cannot hide, and the 6e mutation list carries two entries for it that run 6c's own file through the `suite` override.

- Proof: `python -X utf8 tests/test_automation_monitor_contract.py` -> `Ran 88 tests in 13.78s / OK`; 6c `Ran 52 in 5.85s`, 6d `Ran 80 in 21.54s`, 6b `Ran 62 in 12.62s`, 6a `OK` - all after the engine and pane changes. **85 injected defects, 85 caught by a named test**, both sources restored byte-for-byte (`tools/mutations_automation_monitor.py`), and 6c's own 30-defect sweep re-run clean after the read-path change. `run_audit.py` - now nineteen suites - -> `Overall Health Score: 100% (19/19 suites passed in 221.04s)`, exit 0, with the Monitor contract registered as the 19th (`run_audit.py:124-129`).
- The sweep itself had to be repaired first. Three defects - `test_84`'s missing stop, the `_eta_text` `done <= 0` guard and the `except psutil.Error` in `refresh_metrics` - were "detected" only by the process dying at exit `0xc0000409`: PyQt6 aborts on an exception raised inside a slot, so unittest never reached the line that would have named the test. The suite now installs a recording `sys.excepthook` (`SLOT_FAULTS`, `_keep_slot_fault`) and a `WatchedCase` base that fails the running test for any fault a slot was handed, and `test_84` counts the files a batch actually took after `engine.stop()` instead of only noting that the run ended - so a close that never asked for the stop is a named failure, not a silent one.
- **Not covered here:** the psutil reads are real but have never been watched against a long render, because no renderer is installed here; `closeEvent`'s Yes/No branch is pinned by a stubbed dialog and a scan of the source (`MAIN_SOURCE` in the suite), not by a real window close; `completed_today` is a per-date file, so two app windows share one counter and a run killed by a crash counts as nothing; and the Performance tab's "charts" are still labels, not plots.

### Phase 6f - the batch can do mesh work, and the second fake batch is gone

A batch that can only copy files is a file manager - and this app had a *second* batch pane that could not copy files either.

**1,894 raw / 1,540 non-blank lines deleted, and why nothing was lost.** `src/features/smart_batch_processor.py` (970) and `src/ui/smart_batch_processor_ui.py` (924) went whole. The backend was the reason: `_execute_single_task` (`:395`) set `task.status = 'completed'` whatever its handler returned, `_convert_format` (`:435-443`) commented `# Would save to new format here`, ran `pass` and answered `{'success': True}`, and `_optimize_mesh` (`:466`) answered `{'operations': operations_performed, 'success': True}` without touching a mesh - **no handler in that file ever saved a file**. The UI side was the same shape: `start_batch` (`:683-685`) computed an `output_path` that nothing read, Pause/Stop/Preview were built and never connected, `on_batch_progress` (`:723`) read a `model` key the worker never sent, `execute_batch` put a `ThreadPoolExecutor(max_workers=4)` over loaders that are not reentrant (the conclusion 6d reached and acted on), `BatchProcessingWorker` shadowed `QThread.finished`, "AI Suggestions (Confidence: 85%)" were hard-coded thresholds, and `create_template`/`list_templates` wrote a JSON file no operation ever read - which is what the deleted `verify_all_fixes.py` test proved by passing. It was reachable from two places in the shipping app: the Pre-Processing tab's sixth sub-tab (`preprocessing_tab.py:19`, `:204-206`) and the Library context menu's "Add to Batch Queue" (`library_tab.py:2740-2748`). **That jump also carried a live crash**: `_open_in_batch_processor` asked `preprocessing_tab.tab_widget` (`library_tab.py:2746`) and no widget of that name has ever existed in the file - its sub-tab bar is `operation_tabs` (`:185`) - so every click on that button raised `AttributeError`.

Deleted lines, file by file: `smart_batch_processor.py` 970 and `smart_batch_processor_ui.py` 924 (whole modules, `git rm`); `preprocessing_tab.py` 6 (the import, the `# Tab 6` comment, the two construction lines, and the blank lines around them - replaced by 5 lines saying why there is no sixth tab); `library_tab.py` 18 (`_open_in_batch_processor` and the context-menu row, replaced by 21 lines that reach the real pane); `tests/verify_all_fixes.py` 17 (the module-level `from ...smart_batch_processor import SmartBatchProcessor` and `test_06_smart_batch_processor_template_persistence` - the numbering gap is left on purpose so a reader sees a test was removed here, not renumbered away). Ten more removed lines are rewrites, not losses: seven in `thumbnail_renderer.py` (`-> bool` became `-> dict`, `return True`/`return False` became the verdict, the load-failure `pass` became the reason) and one each in `automation_tab.py` (the flat hint sentence that now branches on the extension), `library_manager.py` (`success = ...` / `if success:` read the dict instead).

**Nine operations the registry can actually run**, each registered `batch=('input_file', 'destination')` and each calling the same backend the single-model panes call: `processing.convert_format`, `simplify_to_faces`, `simplify_by_ratio`, `repair_mesh`, `weld_vertices`, `center_pivot`, `normalize_size`, `generate_uvs` and `file.thumbnail` (`automation_engine.py:319`, `:352`, `:383`, `:409`, `:437`, `:464`, `:494`, `:561`, `:601`). Every one of them calls `landed(path)` (`:281`) before it speaks - the size of the file it was meant to leave behind, or `None` - so a backend that reports success and writes nothing is a **failed file** naming itself (`:307-311`, `:341-344`), and the numbers in the sentence are the ones that came out of the run: faces before and after from the simplifier, the repairer's own operation list verbatim, vertices removed as before-minus-after, the centroid and the extents measured on the written file, UV coordinates counted back off the export. `generate_uvs` refuses `.stl` because STL carries no UVs (`"an .obj or .ply"`) and refuses an unknown method by name; `convert_format` refuses a destination whose extension equals the source's and tells the caller to set `output_format` (`:298-302`); `normalize_size` refuses `target_size=0` rather than writing a model made of nothing.

**A conversion says what it will write, before it writes.** `register` gained a `batch_extension` keyword kept in `_batch_extensions` (`:75`), and `bind_batch_params` (`:631`) now decides the *destination name* at bind time: the extension from `output_format` if the caller gave one, else the extension the action declared when it registered (`file.thumbnail` → `png`), else the file's own name (`:666-673`). That single rule is what lets Preview, the overwrite switch and the "would write the file onto itself" refusal (`:675`) all speak about the file that will actually exist - before 6f a thumbnail batch Previewed `hero.obj -> out\hero.obj` and then wrote `hero.obj.png`. The pane reads the same table: `_batch_hint` (`automation_tab.py:1732`) writes the sentence from the binding *and* the declared extension, and adds "Set output_format to name a different extension." only where `parameter_names(action_id)` says that parameter exists (`:626`). `output_format` is a **free-text cell in the pane, not a combo of nine formats**, because the converter's real capability table (5a) is per-pair and this pane cannot know which pair a folder holds.

**The thumbnail tells the truth about itself.** `generate_3d_thumbnail` (`src/utils/thumbnail_renderer.py:26`) returns `{'success', 'rendered', 'error'}`. Until now it returned `True` in both cases, and the fallback it draws when a model cannot be rasterised is an isometric wireframe **badge** - so a library of unreadable models each got a picture of a cube labelled as that model, and `library_manager.py:983-986` stored the path as the thumbnail. `file.thumbnail` refuses a non-`.png` destination, refuses a `size` that is not a number or outside 16..4096 **without writing anything**, and refuses a picture whose subject contributed no triangles (`automation_engine.py:590-594`) - the drawn PNG is still on disk, and the verdict says what is in it. Two real defects went with the rewrite: with no `QCoreApplication`, `QImage`/`QPainter` abort the process (exit `0xc0000409`, the same class of death 6d and 6e hit), so the function now returns a refusal before touching either (`thumbnail_renderer.py:42-46`); and `mesh.simplify_quadric_decimation(2500)` was passing 2500 as the **percentage of faces to keep**, not a face count - a no-op that can raise for a value over 100 - and is `face_count=2500` now (`:93-95`).

**The pane offers a file a batch can use, and no more than that.** `batch_prepare_for_file` (`automation_tab.py:1909`) is where the Library's context menu lands now: the folder that file sits in, the extension filter set to its extension, subfolders excluded, and a log line that says what was filled in and that the status line below **still describes the last run**. Deliberately not done: it does not queue that one file (the batch model has no per-file queue - it has a folder and a filter, and inventing a queue here would be the second fake batch), it does not touch `batch_status_label`, and it does not call `preview_batch()` because that pops a modal. `library_tab.py` calls it through `getattr` on the automation tab and the widget reference the pane now keeps (`self.batch_operations_tab`, `:108`), because `addTab` swallows the widget and the old code reached for a `tab_widget` that was never assigned.

**`tests/test_automation_batch_contract.py` goes 80 -> 110 tests.** `PaneCase` 80-88: the destination naming rule for a conversion, a thumbnail's `.png` name and its hint sentence, `output_format` offered only where the action takes it, a real two-file conversion batch that lands `a.stl`/`b.stl` and prints `Finished 2 file(s): 2 ok, 0 failed`, the overwrite switch reading the *renamed* destination (refusal by name, and `Nothing ran.`), a `size 4` batch that reports `0 ok, 2 failed` with the output folder still empty, `batch_prepare_for_file` filling exactly the three fields and `collect_batch_items` then agreeing about which files it means, the library jump driven through the unbound method against a stub window (`_open_in_batch_operations` must reach the pane and the pane must be showing), and a deletion guard that fails if `smart_batch_processor` appears in any `src/**/*.py`, if `preprocessing_tab` keeps `batch_processor_tab` or `SmartBatchProcessorUI`, or if `library_tab` keeps `Add to Batch Queue`, `_open_in_batch_processor` or a `preprocessing_tab.tab_widget` reference. `MeshOperationCase` 89-109 asks the registry, not the pane: the binding shape of all nine, each action's real output read back with trimesh, `output_faces` equal to the face count of the file, `removed` equal to before-minus-after, the PNG signature and `struct.unpack('>II', drawn[16:24])` for the size drawn, every refusal writing nothing, a missing file named for every action, an unknown parameter refused before the disk is touched, and the renderer verdict (`rendered` False with no triangles, a 20,480-face sphere still rasterising after the `face_count` fix, and the face count of the *picture* under 2,500 for that sphere). `test_109` leaves the action alone and calls `generate_3d_thumbnail` with `size 8` and `size 'large'`, naming each in the refusal and proving no file was written - the sizes are chosen so that deleting the guard fails by name instead of killing the run.

- **The first sweep left four defects unclosed, and each said something different about the suite.** The run of 75 reported `[55]`, `[70]` and `[75]` as SURVIVED and `[63]` as CRASHED. **[55]** the suite asked for `target_faces=200` and pymeshlab lands on exactly 200, so "the number the file has" and "the number it was asked for" were the same number and reporting the requested one was invisible; it now asks for **197**, which that build leaves as **196**, and the test says out loud if a future build ever closes that gap (`this build now lands exactly on the target, so the test can no longer separate a measured face count from a requested one`). **[70]** the library-jump test built its window with **one tab**, so `currentWidget()` already returned the automation tab before the jump ran and deleting the switch was undetectable - it starts on a decoy `QLabel` tab now, and asserts it was on the decoy first. **[75]** passing 2500 positionally raises `ValueError: target_reduction must be between 0 and 1`, the renderer's own `except Exception: pass` swallowed that, the mesh was drawn whole and the picture was still a picture - nothing in the suite asked *how many triangles reached it*, so the verdict now carries `faces` and three defects on that line (the percentage form, a no-op form, a `faces: 0` lie) are each named by `test_108`. **[63]** was the instructive one: `test_95` *does* name that defect, but unittest prints results only at the end of the run, and a 9000×9000 `QImage` aborts the process before the report line - so a caught defect read as CRASHED, which this log treats as unclosed. The fix is not a better reader: `generate_3d_thumbnail` now bounds its own canvas (`16..4096`, the floor because the background grid divides by `size // 8`, the ceiling because that is what one ARGB32 image may cost) and converts `size` itself, so **no caller can hand Qt a number that kills the app**, and `test_109_the_drawing_function_refuses_a_canvas_it_cannot_allocate` pins the drawing function rather than the action. Two guards on one range is deliberate: the action owns the policy sentence, the drawing function owns not dying.
- Proof: `python -X utf8 tests/test_automation_batch_contract.py` -> `Ran 110 tests in 40.347s / OK`. The full sweep - `python -X utf8 -u tools/mutation_check.py tests/test_automation_batch_contract.py tools/mutations_automation_batch.py`, **79** injected defects - reports `all 79 defects were caught by a named test` with exit 0; `grep -rn "mutation:" src --include=*.py` is empty afterwards, and the contract suite was run again *after* the sweep (the 40.347s above) to prove the five files the harness touches came back byte-exactly rather than merely looking plausible. `run_audit.py` on this exact tree -> `Overall Health Score: 100% (19/19 suites passed in 266.64s)`, exit 0 - the suite that had to grow by 30 tests and pass the same 79-defect sweep is the 18th, and nothing else regressed. `_audit_6f.txt` was the raw capture of that run and is deleted; the numbers above are what it said.
- **Not covered here, stated so the green sweep is not read as more than it is.** No DCC exists on this machine, so nothing in 6f is a render: `file.thumbnail` draws with Qt's own rasteriser and the test reads the PNG's IHDR bytes back, which proves a picture *of that file*, not a frame from an engine - the command-line side belongs to Phase 3's mock. The mesh operations are proven on synthetic spheres (`icosphere`) and small OBJ/PLY/STL files, not on a production FBX with materials; UV survival is only asserted where a reader exists here (`.obj`, `.ply`). pymeshlab on this build lands on the requested target for even numbers and 197 -> 196, so the tests assert the **measured** count and a different pymeshlab build will change the number, not the meaning. The 16..4096 canvas bound prevents a Qt allocation abort; it does not prove a thumbnail is *legible*, only that triangles reached it. `output_format` is free text and is not cross-checked against the 5a capability table per format pair - a bad pair is refused by the converter with its own sentence. Batch remains one file at a time: the concurrency the deleted pane claimed is Phase 11's debt, not something 6f quietly restored.

### Phase 6g - the Pre-Processing pane reads files the way the rest of the app does

Five sub-tabs that each had their own idea of what a 3D file is - and the answers they gave were mostly their own invention.

This was the last pane still carrying a private loader. `ModelExtractor` called `trimesh.load` itself (`model_extractor.py:44` at HEAD), so the FBX and DAE that the pane's own browse filter named could only ever come back as `Failed to extract models from scene: file_type 'fbx' not supported`; `LODGenerator._load_mesh_safe` did the same (`lod_generator.py:37`) while that module's `get_supported_formats()` hand-typed nine extensions including `.dae` and `.x3d`, which **no library here can read at all**; and the Advanced sub-tab answered its three checkboxes by printing `isChecked()` into a bulleted dialog. Every long run in the pane was `QProgressDialog(..., 0, 0, self)` - a busy bar that cannot show progress, with a Cancel button connected to nothing, three of them freezing the window on the click thread. Five extraction methods existed in the module and the pane could reach **one** of them: no method control was ever built, and `ModelExtractionThread.__init__(self, file_path, min_faces)` had nowhere to put one.

**`src/processing/format_converter.py` (169 added / 11 deleted) - one reader, and it is the only one.** `read_mesh` (`:745`), `read_scene` (`:809`), `mesh_write_formats` (`:840`), `write_scene` (`:852`), `write_mesh` (`:908`) and `optimized_target` (`:914`) are public now; `_read_as_trimesh` is gone rather than aliased, and its four test call sites moved (`tests/test_format_converter_contract.py:132`, `:137`, `:189`, `:298`). The important change is a word: all three reader branches used to accept a file that gave them **vertices but no faces** (`if mesh is not None and len(mesh.vertices)`, `if current.vertex_number()`), and a faceless "mesh" then flowed into decimation, into extraction and into a report; each branch now requires faces (`:772`, `:784`, `:801`) and the refusal names every library that was tried and what each said. `readable_formats` (`:273`) / `writable_formats` (`:286`) are asked of the installed libraries, and `is_format_supported` (`:358`) reads that list instead of the hand-typed one, which is what lets the pane's filters and pickers be derived rather than declared.

**`src/processing/model_extractor.py` (315 / 234) - a method now means the split it names.** `read_source` (`:68`) is the module's only door into a file and it asks the converter; the blanket `try/except` that re-wrapped everything as `Failed to extract models from scene: <str(e)>` is gone, so a refusal is the reader's own sentence and not a paraphrase. `extract_from_scene` (`:91`) gained `method`, `progress` and `should_cancel`; `_split_part` (`:191`) is the single place a piece gets its name, so what the table shows is the method that ran. Two of the five methods were not methods at all. `spatial_clustering` used `DBSCAN(eps=np.mean(mesh.extents) * 0.1, min_samples=5)` and skipped label `-1`, so on a real sphere nearly every face was noise and **the run returned nothing**; `_spatial_clustering` (`:237`) is now a partition - `min_samples=1`, eps from `edges_unique_length` - and it keeps the faces, which `test_every_method_returns_every_face_it_was_given` pins as arithmetic (`sum(face_count) == the fixture's faces`). Writing that exposed a second trap: `mesh.submesh()` takes a **sequence of masks**, and a bare mask is read as one scalar index and raises `len() of unsized object`, so the call is `mesh.submesh([np.flatnonzero(labels == label)])` (`:263`) with the reason in a comment. `geometric_analysis` called `mesh.convex_decomposition()`, which raises on this build because there is no `vhacdx`, fell back to a connectivity split and printed the reason to console, then returned the pieces under the chosen method's name; `_geometric_separation` (`:276`) still falls back - it cannot do otherwise - but the `notes` list (`:191`, `:237`, `:276`, carried out as `stats['notes']`) now travels to the sentence the pane prints (`_extraction_sentence` `:1752`), so the file says *which split actually ran and why*. `_group_by_materials` is deleted outright: its body was `material_groups[mat_name] = mesh  # Placeholder`, so "material_based" handed back the **whole mesh once per material** under different names; `_material_label` (`:27`) reads the real slot names from both material classes, and `material_based`/`size_based` refuse a file that reads as one lump **by name** rather than inventing groups. `_collect` (`:210`) applies `min_faces` once, for every method, and counts what it dropped into the summary; `_safe_filename` (`:41`) stops a part named `../../evil` from writing outside the chosen folder; `export_selected_models` (`:356`) writes one `.mtl` per exported part and refuses an extension no writer here can hold.

**Three backends for the Advanced switches, because the rule is to write the backend, not delete the control.** `src/processing/texture_optimizer.py` (363 lines): `available_formats` (`:42`) asks the installed Pillow build, so "Convert to: WEBP" is an offer this machine can keep and a choice it cannot encode arrives greyed with the reason as its tooltip; `find_textures` (`:142`) reads both of trimesh's material classes through `TEXTURE_SLOTS` (`:29`), so an `.obj` with a hand-written `.mtl` gets the same treatment as a `.glb`; `DATA_SLOTS` (`:33`) names the maps that hold numbers rather than colour, because re-encoding a normal map as JPEG is a corruption the user should be told about; `optimize` (`:214`) reports bytes measured **from files it wrote**, with `baseline_bytes` (`:171`) as the same pixels through the same encoder at its defaults - a decoded image has no file size of its own, so the baseline is named instead of invented. `src/processing/texture_rewriter.py` (370): a run is not finished when the small `.png`s exist - the engine loads the model file, so `rewrite_glb` (`:201`), `rewrite_gltf` (`:235`) and `rewrite_mtl` (`:315`) edit only the image entries, the buffer views and the buffer length, and copy the mesh bytes through, which is what lets the report say the shape is unchanged. Files packed with draco, meshopt, basisu or `EXT_texture_webp` are refused (`_UNREPACKABLE` `:31`, `_refusable` `:101`) instead of quietly corrupted. `src/processing/scene_optimizer.py` (435): `hidden_names` (`:147`) calls an object hidden only when the file actually marks it (glTF header flag, COLLADA `hidden`) or when it has `_EMPTY` = no faces to draw, and a format carrying no visibility flag says so instead of deleting what merely looked unused; `_merge_static` (`:391`) merges only parts whose material is the same pixels *and* the same factors, because trimesh drops one of two differing materials without saying so (measured on this build: the merged mesh comes back with no material), so an unpaired part is left alone and counted.

**`src/ui/preprocessing_tab.py` (814 / 207) - two invariants that came out of the process dying, not out of an assertion.** (1) `_keep_worker` (`:339`): reassigning a running `QThread`'s only Python reference makes Qt abort the process (exit `0xC0000409`) - this is how the pane's test run died at 127 through a pipe with no FAIL line anywhere, and it was a **real product bug**, because clicking Extract twice, or Extract then Convert, dropped the first worker mid-file. Every worker is now held in `self._workers` and released on `finished`, at all seven sites, and `test_no_worker_can_be_dropped_while_it_still_runs` fails if any start appears outside the registry. (2) `_show_stage` (`:1178`): `QProgressDialog.setValue()` runs a **nested event loop**, so a worker's terminal signal can null the pane's dialog attribute *between two lines of one stage handler* - the crash surfaced as `AttributeError: 'NoneType' object has no attribute 'setLabelText'`. Handlers now take the dialog as an argument and `test_a_stage_handler_never_asks_the_dialog_from_the_attribute` forbids the attribute form by source scan. Both are the same class of failure 6d and 6e met, and neither is testable by asserting on a value.

Every long run is off the click handler, on a worker with a range that means something and a Cancel that reaches it: `LodGenerationThread` (`:49`), `ModelExtractionThread` (`:84`), `FormatConversionThread` (`:117`), `_ExportThread` (`:207`), `OptimizationThread` (`:240`) beside the baker's existing thread, and `test_every_progress_dialog_cancel_reaches_its_worker` stalls each one and clicks Cancel. The verdicts were rebuilt around the stop, not around the happy path: `lods_written` (`:1196`) reports a stopped chain as a **partial run** with the levels that landed and the failures of each; `show_conversion_complete` (`:1531`) names the files that never ran; `baking_finished` (`:1027`) replaced "Baking Successful!" with the maps the baker actually returned. The offers come from the backends: `readable_filter` (`:1047`) builds every browse filter from `readable_formats()`, `_refresh_target_availability` (`:1446`) greys a target no installed library can write from the files in the list and puts `conversion_blocker`'s own sentence in the tooltip, and `export_selected_models` (`:1352`) offers `mesh_write_formats()` mapped **by label** (`labels = {ext.upper().lstrip('.'): ext}`) - the old `format_ext = f".{format_choice.lower()}"` handed `.FBX` to `mesh.export()` and then said `Successfully exported 0 models.` The Advanced pane gained the button its switches were always pointing at: `_advanced_settings` (`:1586`) reads the controls once with no invented values, `preview_optimizations` (`:1609`) counts and writes nothing, `apply_optimizations` (`:1617`) refuses when neither group is armed, and `_preview_text` (`:1694`) / `_optimization_text` (`:1735`) print only measured before/after; `opt_apply_btn` stays disabled until a switch is armed *and* a file is loaded (`_refresh_optimization_buttons` `:1599`). Per the slider rule, the `quality_label = QLabel("80%")` echo is replaced by a typable `texture_quality_value` (`:767`, 1..1000) whose out-of-range values survive - typing 500 used to collapse to 100 because the slider echoed back over the box, so `_sync_quietly` (`:1682`) blocks signals while the two follow each other - and `texture_max_size`'s 0 is read as `None` ("No cap"), not as "rescale to nothing". `_forget_previous_run` (`:440`): loading a second file wipes the first file's rows, summary line and optimization report, because a stale report under a new filename is a claim about the wrong file.

**Deleted lines, file by file, with what each was doing.** `model_extractor.py` 234: the five method bodies rewritten in place (`_extract_from_scene_object`'s and `_extract_from_single_mesh`'s four `elif` branches, ~90 lines, replaced by `_split_part` + `_collect` so the branches cannot disagree again), `_group_by_materials` whole (17, the placeholder), the old `spatial_clustering`/`geometric_separation` bodies (58, including the two `print(f"... failed")` handlers that turned a crash into a quiet empty list), `export_selected_models` (41, replaced by 57 lines that use the converter's writer and name the refusal), and the module-level `supported_extensions = ['.obj', ... '.fbx']` the batch helper read. `preprocessing_tab.py` 207: the "Optimization Preview" dialog (~30 lines of `• `-bulleted text whose only content was three `isChecked()` calls), the four `QProgressDialog(…, 0, 0, …)` bodies and their inline `generate_lods()`/`extract_models()` runs on the click thread (~55), the hand-typed browse filters (7 - every `*.obj *.ply *.stl *.off *.dae *.gltf *.glb *.3mf *.fbx` string, now `readable_filter`), `quality_label` (4), `baking_error`'s duplicate dialog close (9), and `format_ext = f".{format_choice.lower()}"`. Nothing in those 207 was a capability the pane kept losing: each is either a claim the pane could not support or a filter that was a lie about the reader. `lod_generator.py` 42 / `texture_baker.py` 9 / `format_converter.py` 11 / `tests/test_format_converter_contract.py` 4 are call-site moves and one deletion worth naming: `preview_lod_reduction` appended the **last LOD's dict twice** (`lod_generator.py:464-476` at HEAD, using the `i`/`ratio` leaked out of the loop), so a 4-level chain previewed 5 rows; those 13 lines are gone and the 4-level chain now reads as 4.

**`lod_generator.py` (38 / 42) and `texture_baker.py` (37 / 9).** `_load_mesh_safe` (`:37`) delegates to the converter - it used to be a second loader only trimesh could serve, while the same file promised `.fbx` and `.dae`, which is why every FBX job died there. `generate_lods` (`:51`) gained `progress`/`should_cancel` and a per-level `self.errors`, so a level that fails is one failed level and not an aborted chain, and `get_supported_formats` (`:492`) asks `readable_formats()`. `bake` (`texture_baker.py:73`) gained `should_cancel` asked **between** maps: a PyMeshLab filter stopped mid-call leaves the `MeshSet` in a state this build does not recover from, so the map in hand finishes, `_Stopped` (`:39`) names the maps that never ran, the ones already on disk are still reported, and `request_stop` (`:280`) is the thread's own. Both of the baker's early returns now end in `baking_complete.emit(False, {})` - **a path that starts must end in a verdict signal**, or the modal dialog the pane opened never closes.

**`tests/test_preprocessing_contract.py`** (new, 1,088 lines, 48 tests, the 20th audit suite). `ExtractionBackend` (15 tests) asks the backend rather than the pane, and the key apparatus is `OnlyReader` (`:192`): with all three libraries installed, deleting the face guard from *one* branch changes nothing a test can see, because the next branch answers, so each stub test leaves exactly one library able to reply and `test_no_reader_branch_calls_a_faceless_read_a_mesh` refuses all three (`:772`, `:784`, `:801`) by name. Also here: every method returns every face it was given across 3 fixtures x 5 methods; a partition must not shatter a sphere into 60 fragments; a method that could not run names the one that did; a stop leaves the parts it saw and says it stopped; a part name cannot escape the output folder. `OptimizationBackend` (4): a quality the encoders reject is applied at the nearest value **and said**, the Convert-to list is the installed Pillow's own list, a texture run hands its model to the scene run, and a stopped batch names the files that never ran. `BakingBackend` (4): every refusal ends in a verdict, a stop keeps the landed map and names the rest, one failing map does not stop the others from landing, a full bake reports success with every map. `PreProcessingPane` (18) clicks the real controls through `RecordingDialogs` (`:236`) with every worker stalled by `StalledWorkers` (`:265`) so a Cancel that reaches nothing is caught rather than raced; `lod_rows(levels)` (`:735`) arms the switch, drains the pane (`drain`, `:705`) so a preview started by an earlier click cannot answer after the clear, and reads the chain back - `test_the_lod_preview_shows_one_row_per_level_asked` wants exactly `levels` rows named `LOD0..LOD{levels-1}` at 2, 4 and 6 levels (that is the phantom duplicate), and `test_the_lod_preview_prints_faces_the_file_has_and_no_guesses` wants LOD0's number to be `int(this file's faces x the first ratio spin)` and forbids `MB`, `Recommended`, `hero objects` and `crowd/fill` anywhere in the text, which is where the two invented fields used to land. `PaneSourceContract` (7) scans the source for the four rules that a green run cannot show: no private reader in the pane, no `Yes`/`No` answer in the Advanced pane, every defined worker started, Cancel wired at every long run, no browse filter the reader cannot honour, no worker startable outside the registry, and no stage handler reading a dialog from the attribute.

- Proof: `python -X utf8 tests/test_preprocessing_contract.py` -> `Ran 48 tests in 3.717s / OK`, exit 0; `python -X utf8 -u tools/mutation_check.py tests/test_preprocessing_contract.py tools/mutations_preprocessing.py` -> **19 injected defects, all 19 caught by a named test**, exit 0, with the sources restored byte-for-byte after each (`tools/mutations_preprocessing.py`, 176 lines; `git diff -- src` is empty after the sweep). `run_audit.py` on this exact tree -> `Overall Health Score: 100% (20/20 suites passed in 246.13s)`, exit 0, the new suite registered as the 20th (`run_audit.py:100-105`) and passing in 5.98s; that run carried the suite at 46 tests, and the two LOD-preview tests added after it touch only `tests/` and `tools/`, so the product tree the audit scored is the tree committed. The suites that had to survive a shared reader change all re-ran green in that audit: Format Conversion Contract 10.17s, 3D LOD & QEM Pipeline 3.29s, Mesh Repair & Analysis E2E 1.45s, Mesh Tools UI-to-Backend 12.58s, PyMeshLab Filter Contract 2.58s, Texture Bake Output 2.08s, Core Backend & Regression 43.83s, the Post-Processing folder suite and the 15-tab headless suite at 25.84s. The raw capture was root `_audit_out.txt` and is deleted; the numbers above are what it said, and `git status` afterwards is product/test changes only.
- Two of 6g's deletions were **unpinned when the first sweep finished**, which is the same hole the log has now caught three times. The duplicate LOD row and the two invented preview fields were both removed, and no test named either - so a future edit could put them back and stay green. `test_the_lod_preview_shows_one_row_per_level_asked` and `test_the_lod_preview_prints_faces_the_file_has_and_no_guesses` close it, with three defects on those lines (the duplicated last level, `target_faces = original_faces`, and the row quoting `estimated_file_size_mb` again) each caught by name. Writing them found a test-harness trap worth keeping: `lod_levels.setValue(n)` starts several previews at once - the switch's own toggle, the rebuilt ratio spins, each `valueChanged` - and an answer from the ask *before* the one under test lands after the clear and writes rows nobody asked for. `drain` (`:705`) waits for the pane's whole worker list, including any it starts while waiting, so the rows read are the rows asked for.
- The first sweep of 16 taught one thing about the suite. Reverting `_spatial_clustering` to `min_samples=5` **survived**: with the noise faces merged back together the method still returned the whole mesh as one lump, so every face still arrived and no test could tell a partition from a shrug - an equivalent mutant, not a weak test. The `eps = np.mean(mesh.extents) * 0.1` revert was caught, and the hole was closed by writing `test_a_group_of_faces_is_a_group_and_not_a_fragment`, which asks how many pieces came back rather than whether they all did. Three defects live on lines that appear more than once in a file (`if notes is not None:` x3, `baking_complete.emit` x1-of-2, `self.extracted_models = []` x2) and the mutation spec names the occurrence with `which`, because "replace the first one" tests a different line from the one that was fixed.
- **Not covered here, stated so a green sweep is not read as more than it is.** No renderer is installed, so "optimized" means measured bytes on disk that this app can read back, not a frame from an engine, and a texture run is never checked against a game engine's texture budget. `preview_lod_reduction` still carries two invented fields - `estimated_file_size_mb` from `(vertices * 30) + (faces * 15)` (`lod_generator.py:472`) and `recommended_use` from a threshold table (`:479`); the pane stopped printing both in this phase, `test_the_lod_preview_prints_faces_the_file_has_and_no_guesses` fails if either reaches it again, but `tests/test_renderfox.py:74` still reads the second, so the arithmetic is Phase 8's dead-code debt with a name, not something 6g quietly kept. `LODGenerator.analyze_mesh_quality` (`lod_generator.py:311`) became callerless when the bulleted dialog went, and `batch_extract_models` / `preview_extraction` still have no caller either. `LODPreviewThread` still guards a method that is defined in the file it imports (`:39`), which is dead code, not a bug. Convex decomposition cannot run on this build at all - `geometric_analysis` is proven to *say so*, not to separate anything; a machine with `vhacdx` installed gets a different, untested path. A stopped run keeps what landed: a halted LOD chain leaves its levels on disk, which is the honest behaviour and not a cleanup, and no undo exists. Each run is one thread - the same reason 6d gave for a serial batch - so "process this whole folder at once" is still the Automation pane's one-file-at-a-time loop, not parallel work.

### Phase 6h - the Point Clouds pane stops doing its own version of the work

One pane that re-implemented its backend inside click handlers, answered itself in dialogs, and named files by rewriting every dot in the path.

Read at HEAD, `src/ui/point_clouds_tab.py` was a second copy of `src/processing/point_cloud_processor.py`: six `import open3d` inside click handlers, twelve `o3d.` calls, eight `np.` calls and a `matplotlib` figure, all of it measuring numbers the backend also measured. Two of its handlers read state that the class never assigns - `self.current_cloud_path` (`:1088`, `:1107`) and `self.processor` (`:1107`, `:1635`) - so **'Poisson Reconstruction' raised `AttributeError` on its very first line**, and 'Compute Nearest Distances' started its real worker and then fell through into a *duplicate copy of its own body* and raised there; an exception in a slot is answered by PyQt6 with a process abort, so the click that looks like it did nothing is the click that can end the app. Three bodies were written twice in the same class (`run_clustering` `:1109` shadowed by `:1534`, `compute_distances` `:1589` and `:1617`, `crop_interactive`'s inner `demo_crop_geometry` `:1296` and `:1308` - the second copy of that one is never called, and the comment above it says *"Actually, VisualizerWithEditing is limited. Let's just launch it and let the user explore."*: the button promised `Press 'S' to save` and wrote nothing). **Fourteen controls moved nothing**: ten named buttons with no `connect` anywhere (`add_model_btn`, `clear_history_btn`, `create_convex_hull_btn`, `detect_planes_btn`, `estimate_normals_btn`, `export_mesh_btn`, `extract_features_btn`, `remove_duplicates_btn`, `save_pcd_btn`, `uniform_downsample_btn`), `Fit to View` (its connect is commented out with *"Check if supported"*, and `UnifiedOpen3DViewer` indeed had no such method), the point-size slider and both display checkboxes - 42 `QPushButton(` built against 32 `.clicked.connect`. Naming was `self.current_model_path.replace('.', '_x.')` at **13 sites**, which rewrites every dot in the string: `D:\scans\v1.2\site.ply` becomes a folder that does not exist, and `scan.ply` with `.replace('.', '_bpa_mesh.obj')` becomes `scan_bpa_mesh.objply`. Every long run used `QProgressDialog(..., 0, 0, ...)` (an indeterminate bar with no claim behind it) beside a `Worker`/`QThreadPool` pair (`:93`) that has no cancel channel at all.

**`src/processing/point_cloud_processor.py` (1,446 lines, +1,308/-645) - the module owns the vocabulary and the numbers.** `PointCloudProcessor` is a plain class now; it was a `QObject` with two signals nobody emitted and nobody connected, which forced the automation launcher to import PyQt before it could process a file. Twenty-five verbs in `OPERATIONS` (`:552`) each have one `op_*` handler, and `run()` (`:1419`) is the only door: it never raises, and every path out of it carries `success`/`stopped` plus either an `output_path` the file is really at or an `error` that says why there is none (`_base`/`_refuse`/`_accepted`/`_cancelled`, `:758`-`:777`). **Nothing is reported from memory**: `write_points` (`:786`) checks the folder, refuses an empty cloud, then re-opens the file it wrote and returns the point count that file gives back - the old `save_point_cloud` returned `True` whenever `o3d.io.write_point_cloud` did not raise, so a run that wrote nothing still told the user which file to go and open - and `write_surface` (`:838`) refuses a mesh with no faces instead of reporting `success` with `triangles: 0`. `.obj` for a cloud is the one thing Open3D cannot write here, so `_write_vertex_obj` (`:814`) writes the `v` lines itself and counts them back off the disk before saying a point moved (`VERTEX_ONLY_OBJ`, `:30`). Naming is one rule in two places: `cloud_output_path` (`:33`, `os.path.splitext`, with the `v1.2` folder in its docstring) and `target_path` (`:658`). The automation surface is data: `AUTOMATION_TABLE` (`:569`, 24 rows of verb/action id/description/extra params/shape - `pick` is deliberately absent, because a scheduled task that waits for someone to left-click would run at 3am and report nothing), `_SHAPES` (`:623`), `automation_action` (`:701`) and `automation_actions` (`:728`), so a nightly batch and the button read the same table and answer with the same dict. Two mismatches between the specs and the verbs surfaced the moment the actions were actually run: `op_translate` (`:1028`) and `op_rotate` (`:1045`) declared `dx/dy/dz` and `rx/ry/rz` optional but refused a task that named only one of them, so an unnamed axis is now 0.0 (an axis a task does not name is an axis it does not move) while the all-zero case is still refused; and `target_path`'s `output_path` escape hatch is deleted, because only `op_export` (`:1348`) is allowed to honour a name a person typed into a Save dialog. `cluster_palette` (`:48`) replaces the DBSCAN colouring that asked `plt.get_cmap('tab20')` and fell back to `np.random.rand`, which made one epsilon give different colours on every run.

**`src/ui/point_clouds_tab.py` (1,652, +1,198/-1,270) - one worker, one verdict, one name per file.** `CloudRunThread` (`:95`) runs one verb off the click thread and posts the dict it answered with; `_keep_worker` (`:200`) is the 6g rule again (dropping the last reference of a running `QThread` aborts the process). `_run` (`:216`) holds the one-run guard (`:224`, and the sentence of the run in flight is what a second click is told) and builds `QProgressDialog(sentence, "Cancel", 0, max(expected, 1))` where `expected = VERB_OUTPUT_FILES[operation]` (`:46`) - **the maximum is the number of files the verb was asked to make**, not a percentage guessed from how far along the operation feels. `SURFACE_OPERATIONS` (`:39`) is read off the backend (`PointCloudProcessor.SURFACE_VERBS`, `:562`), `OP_NAMES`/`RUNNING_TEXT`/`VERB_OUTPUT_FILES` are all keyed by the same 25 verbs, and `_adopt` (`:329`) makes each cloud verb's output the current model so downsample then filter then reconstruct chains - the old pane moved `current_model_path` for three operations and not for the rest, so which button you had last clicked decided which file the next one used. Refusals keep their own sentence on the status line and in a dialog (`_verdict` `:267`), a stopped run still names the files that landed (`:297`), and `_end_run` (`:314`) is the back stop for a thread that ended without reporting. The private measurements are gone: `_statistics_block`/`_headline`/`_detail`/`_fill_info` (`:367`/`:380`/`:458`/`:503`) print the verdict's numbers, `_measured_bounds` (`:514`) asks the file for the box the crop dialog opens on (`:123`, whose spins now reach ±1e12 because a survey scan in metres passes the old ±10000 the day it is opened), `_cloud_filter` (`:1151`) is `readable_formats()`, `show_distance_histogram` (`:1503`) draws the bins the distance verb measured into the pane instead of `plt.show()` (a nested GUI event loop that waited inside the click handler until someone closed the chart), `crop_interactive` (`:1391`) hands the picked indices to `op_pick` (`:1368`) which writes exactly those points and refuses an empty selection, and `add_to_library` emits to the Library pane through the connection it never had (`main_window.py:533`). The value-box rule holds: `point_size_box` (`:1578`) takes any number, and out of the slider's reach the slider is *moved and shown moved* rather than the value being clamped back.

**Two defects that only a live run could find, both pinned by mutation rather than by reading.** (1) `QProgressDialog.wasClosed` is not exposed by PyQt6, so every verdict raised inside the slot before it could report anything - the file was written, the count was measured, and the pane said nothing at all; `_dismiss` (`:258`) asks `isVisible()` instead, which is how every Qt binding answers that question. (2) `finished` from an **older** thread silenced the newer one: on the first live run every verb that measured its own result produced eight `ended without reporting a result` warnings, because the handler asked `self._active_thread` who was running instead of being told - so `_verdict` and `_end_run` take the dialog *and* the thread as `functools.partial` arguments and compare identity (`:319`), the same nested-event-loop trap 6g met through `setValue`. Both classes of failure end a test process before a FAIL line is printed, which is why the suite installs a `sys.excepthook` that records slot faults (`tests/test_point_clouds_contract.py:68`).

**`src/ui/unified_viewer.py` (369, +51/-11) - the viewer could not show the files this pane is for.** `load_from_file` chose by `has_vertices()`: a scanned `.ply` has vertices and no triangles, so a faceless "mesh" went to the previewer (which renders triangles), the panel stayed blank, and the caller was told the load succeeded; and `PointCloud` has no `has_vertices` at all, so every `.pcd` raised `AttributeError` inside the function and answered `False`. It now decides on `has_triangles()`/`has_points()`. `fit_to_view()` and `preview_pixmap()` are the two methods the pane's buttons were written against - one of them never existed, the other was read straight off the label, so a screenshot taken before the first render "saved successfully" a picture that was not there. `PreviewWorker.error_occurred` was connected to nothing, which left the words "Rendering Preview..." on screen forever: the one state that reads as work in progress and is really work that stopped (`_on_preview_failed`, `:297`).

**`main.py` (+11/-7) - the six registrations were registered and unusable.** They wrote their specs as bare types (`{'file_path': str}`) while `ActionRegistry.validate` reads rule dicts (`automation_engine.py:119`), so calling any `pcd.*` action raised `type object 'str' has no attribute 'get'` - from `validate`, which `execute` calls **before** its own `try` (`:134`), so it escaped into the task runner. The list is now built from `processor.automation_actions()`, and `test_37`/`test_39` keep the table and the verbs from drifting apart again; the `registered_point_cloud` counter prints the number it actually registered. `src/features/point_cloud_processor.py` (the 6-line shim) is deleted with no importer left in the tree.

**Deleted lines, file by file, with what each was doing.** Pane 1,270: 77 `QMessageBox` lines (its verdicts were modal dialogs over a click thread; they are a status line, a history row and a measured report now), 51 `setText`, 35 `progress_bar`, 17 `try:`/17 `except Exception`/11 `finally:` (the click-thread bodies), 13 `.replace('.'` naming sites, 6 `import open3d` + 12 `o3d.` + 8 `np.` + 7 `plt` (its private measurements), the three duplicate bodies (~110 lines: the "requires Open3D implementation" excuse dialog, `compute_distances`' unreachable second copy, `crop_interactive`'s uncalled `demo_crop_geometry`), the `Worker`/`threadpool`/`execute_async` framework (11 lines) that no Cancel could reach, 49 docstring lines, and no handler behind any of the ten dead buttons - not one of those ten names appears as a `def` in the class, so the buttons were decoration with a label on them. Processor 645: the twelve wrapper methods (`:488`-`:760` at HEAD - each re-read the file, re-saved it and paraphrased the failure; 20 `print(` and 22 `except Exception` among the removed lines, 36 `'success':` and 26 `'error':` literals), `reconstruct_mesh`'s discarded write result and its `if OPEN3D_AVAILABLE:` guard that still returned `success` when the guard was False, and `cluster_dbscan`'s input-path-as-output branch. Viewer 11, `main.py` 7, the shim 6. Nothing in any of it was a capability the app lost: each line was either a second copy of the backend's work, a name the backend owns, or a control that was decorative.

**`tests/test_point_clouds_contract.py`** (new, 1,314 lines, 40 tests, the 21st audit suite). The apparatus: `RecordingDialogs` (`:238`) turns a modal into data so a refusal can be asserted instead of clicked away; `FileChoosers` (`:274`) answers the Save/Open dialogs with a path the test chose; `StalledThread` (`:329`) holds a real run at the dialog so Cancel, a second click and a verdict that never arrives can each be measured - and it posts the **backend's own** verdict, because a stub of the right shape is how a suite ends up testing its own invention; `WatchingProcessor` (`:347`) is used only to prove the stop reaches a verb in flight. `write_cloud`/`cloud_count`/`mesh_counts` (`:140`/`:151`/`:156`) re-open every file the pane claims, and the three AST scans (`pane_names_used` `:80`, `pane_chains` `:102`, `pane_replace_literals` `:128`) check rules a green run cannot show - grepping the file would find `open3d` in the docstring that names the defect this phase deleted. Five groups: the controls are accounted for (test_01 asks Qt for the buttons of all 40 driven names and fails on any anonymous one; test_02 forbids private math, forbids `self.processor`/`self.current_cloud_path`/`current_mesh_path`, and pins by parse tree that **exactly one** dict in the pane names a verb's output file); the verbs (test_03-test_22: the file exists where the sentence says, the counts in the sentence are the counts in the file, a refusal names the reason and writes nothing, runs chain onto what the previous run wrote, `planes` leaves two clouds that add up to the input, the histogram is drawn from the measurement, each surface verb writes a mesh this app reads back, `.obj`/`. ply` choices survive contact with the writer); the run itself (test_23-test_27); the display and the hand-offs (test_28-test_36, including `test_36` asserting the two `main_window` connections this pane depends on); and the automation door (test_37-test_40: the table equals the verbs, every registered action leaves the files it claims with the counts it reports, and `test_40` runs six verbs over a folder whose input is deliberately named `site.1.ply` and requires that file to be byte-identical afterwards).

- Proof: `python -X utf8 tests/test_point_clouds_contract.py` -> `Ran 40 tests in 66.871s / OK`, exit 0. `python -X utf8 -u tools/mutation_check.py tests/test_point_clouds_contract.py tools/mutations_point_clouds.py` -> **20 injected defects, all 20 caught by a named test** (`tools/mutations_point_clouds.py`, 180 lines), sources restored byte-for-byte after each (`git diff --numstat -- src` empty after the sweep). `run_audit.py` on this tree -> `Overall Health Score: 100% (21/21 suites passed in 347.46s)`, exit 0, the new suite registered at `run_audit.py:142-147` and passing in 69.47s; the neighbours that share these files all re-ran green inside that audit (Pre-Processing 7.33s, Format Conversion 13.59s, Mesh Tools 19.61s, Core Backend & Regression 40.68s, 15-Tab Headless 34.18s). The sibling suites did regress once in this phase, in the way worth recording: the first audit on the finished tree read **95%** because `verify_all_fixes.py` hit its 120 s timeout while other work shared the machine - the same suite runs 38.5 s / 9 tests / OK standalone and 40.68 s inside the idle audit on the identical tree, so the score was a timeout budget, not a defect, and re-running it idle is what produced 21/21. **Read a `[ERROR] (120.19s)` as "the budget was met" before reading it as "the app is broken"** - and check the suite by hand, because a real hang looks exactly like this in the report too.
- What the mutation sweep taught, four times over now. **[14] `SURVIVED` first**: `target_path` had a `named = params.get('output_path')` branch that static reading had called "the single naming rule", and setting it to `None` changed nothing any test could see - because the pane pre-derived byte-identical names at three sites and `ActionRegistry.validate` rejects a parameter the spec does not declare, so no task could ever name `output_path` either. The branch was unreachable *with a difference*, so the fix was to the product: delete the branch, delete the pane's three pre-naming sites, delete `op_planes`' `plane_path`/`rest_path` reads, add the test_02 parse-tree invariant, and retarget the defect at `op_export`'s own read - now caught by four tests. A surviving mutant is a design finding, not a weak assertion.
- **An action id is not a verb name.** test_39 first derived the verb from `action_id.split('.', 1)[1]` and reported `nothing is registered as 'filter_statistical'`; `pcd.filter_statistical` legitimately answers the verb `outliers_statistical`. The suite now maps through `AUTOMATION_TABLE`'s first column and asserts both id sets, so the drift it caught is pinned rather than papered over.
- **The equivalent-mutant pattern again**: `run()` re-wraps any raise as `'<verb> did not finish: <type>: <message>'`, so a defect that turns a known refusal into a crash still produces a red sentence with the same words in it. test_08 now asserts `'did not finish'` is absent for every listed condition - a condition the backend knows about is a refusal, not a crash.
- **A test can be measuring its own setup.** test_38 (the surface format list is the mesh writer's list) passed with `setCurrentText('.ply')` deleted, because the pane under test had already written a `.ply` surface and re-selected it. Rewritten against a fresh pane, the defect is now caught. Two more of that family: the dialog-maximum defect is only visible with a verb that writes two files (`planes`), and the Cancel defect is only visible with the worker held at the dialog - `QProgressDialog.cancel()` does not emit `canceled`, so the click that means Cancel is `findChildren(QPushButton)` with text `Cancel` (`cancel_button_of`, `:169`).
- **Not covered here, stated so a green sweep is not read as more than it is.** `pick` cannot be driven end to end - `VisualizerWithEditing` needs a real window, so what is proven is the empty-selection refusal, the stop path and the instruction text that now matches what that window does; nobody has left-clicked a cloud on this machine. No renderer is installed, so "shown" means the pixmap Open3D rendered offscreen, checked by writing a screenshot and re-opening the bytes, not by a window. A chained run keeps appending suffixes (`scan_downsampled_filtered_uniform_centered_normalized_deduped_normals.ply`) - that is the no-overwrite rule working, and the automation `output_dir` shape is how a folder stays tidy, but the pane has no "start a new chain from this file" control. The Library hand-off is one-way: `add_to_library_requested` works, and there is still no "Open in Point Clouds" on the Library side (`main_window.open_in_point_clouds` is unwired dead code, Phase 8). `op_export`'s `.obj` path is checked by counting `v` lines, not by opening the file in another tool. The pane still has no undo, one thread per run, and no batch over a folder of clouds - that is the Automation pane's `output_dir` loop, which test_40 exercises but only one verb deep per task.
- **What is left in Phase 6**: ~~the Pipeline pane~~ **closed in 6i-1b/6i-2** (`pipeline_engine.py:843-845` "Rendering not fully implemented, skipping" and `:997` "not yet implemented. Passing data through." are gone; both are quoted from the pre-rewrite file, which `git show 5ebac13:src/processing/pipeline_engine.py` answers at `:843-845` and `:997`). Performance (`performance_analyzer.py:127-135` GPU 0.0, `:541+` a "GPU benchmark" that is CPU normal estimation on random points, `performance_tab.py:945` "(placeholder)"), Animation (`animation_tab.py:316-332` a fake sine waveform as an audio track, `:907` dummy seeded tracks, `:1143-1183` whole-model transforms only), `painting_tab.py:299-304` ("to be implemented"), the `is_winding_consistent`-labelled-"Manifold" reports (`mesh_tools_tab.py:1876`, `mesh_processor.py:117`) and `verify_software`, which settings writes and nothing reads.

### Phase 6i-1a - the raster the Pipeline's Render node needs, measured on this machine first

The Pipeline pane is the last surface Phase 6 has to make honest: `pipeline_engine.py:843-845` logs "Rendering not fully implemented, skipping" and returns `True`, and `:997` logs "is not yet implemented. Passing data through." Its engine rewrite is 6i-1b/1c - but writing 25 node handlers over a raster nobody has measured would put a third and fourth invented result into this app, so the picture maker comes first, on its own, with every number taken from this machine.

**`src/processing/preview_render.py` (469 lines, new) draws a file; it is not a render engine and does not claim to be.** The docstring says what it is: the picture RenderFox makes for itself, so a DCC is not required to see a model. Final images stay Blender's, Unreal's, Cinema4D's, and the render queue talks to those. `render_file` (`:327`) is the one entry: geometry path in, image path out, a dict of measurements back, or a `PreviewError` (`:43`) with the reason and no half-written file claimed. `renderer_blocker` (`:88`) answers up front when Open3D or Pillow is missing, instead of failing at the frame.

**Every claim in that verdict is counted from the file on disk, after it was written and re-opened** (`:415-419`) - width, height, mode, `covered_pixels`/`coverage_percent`, `silhouette_width`/`silhouette_height`, `mean_rgb`, `distinct_colors`, `transparent_pixels`, `bytes`. A buffer that was handed to an encoder is not evidence: a JPEG drops the alpha it is given and a PNG re-quantises a frame, so an RGBA run additionally requires `opaque == covered` (`:432`) and a size that is not the asked one raises (`:436`).

**What Open3D 0.19 does on this machine, measured rather than assumed.** Four of these cost a debugging cycle each:
- `OffscreenRenderer` does not start here (`EGL Headless is not supported`), so the Filament path is out; the legacy `Visualizer` with `create_window(visible=False, w, h)` + `capture_screen_float_buffer(do_render=True)` draws, ~0.5 s at 640x480 (`_capture` `:261`, window always destroyed in `finally` `:293`).
- The `extrinsic` the Visualizer wants is **not** the plain look-at basis: its own baseline (eye on +Z at the origin, world up +Y) is `diag(1,-1,-1)`, so the rows are `[right, -vertical, forward]` (`_extrinsic` `:196`). Handing it the textbook basis puts the camera behind the model looking away and every frame comes back as nothing but background - which is how all-black frames were traced here.
- Field of view is set by writing `parameters.intrinsic.intrinsic_matrix` **in place** (`:252-256`); building a fresh `PinholeCameraIntrinsic` and handing that back measured a degenerate projection (the model filled every pixel with one flat colour and no fov changed anything), and the matrix arrives as a read-only view, hence `copy=True`. fov is **vertical**: focal = `(height/2)/tan(fov/2)`, `cx/cy` at centre. `change_field_of_view(step=)` is unreliable (it reported 5.0 for a -15 step) and is not used.
- `o3d.geometry.Image` has no `write_png` in these bindings, so the encoders are Pillow's, and `IMAGE_FORMATS` (`:22`) is that build's list: png/jpg/jpeg/tif/tiff/bmp/webp. `.exr` is refused by name, not written and hoped for.

**The lamp node only means something if the picture answers to it, so the shading path was proved first.** With `options.light_on = False` (`:275`) and `mesh_shade_option`/`mesh_color_option` = `Color` (`:279`, the constants at `:37`), a vertex painted 0.9 prints 229 and one painted 0.15 prints 38 - the albedo verbatim, one flat colour per flat shade. Leaving the engine light on adds its own head light on top and clips lit faces to 255, so a dim lamp and a bright one print the same pixels; that pair is why `light_on=False` is the module's contract and `_luminance` (`:101`) does the Lambert term itself (directional `:110`, point with inverse-square `:113`, spot with cone + falloff `:120`, any other kind refused `:131`). There is no `RenderMaterial` / `set_geometry_material` in 0.19's Python bindings, so "material preview" is not a claim this module can make.

**A window is a real window, and this machine has opinions about its size.** It will not open narrower than 148 px (heights are honoured exactly: 32x16 opened as 148x16), and will not open larger than the desktop (4000x2400 opened as 1924x1055). So the frame is drawn at the size the window realises, LANCZOS-resampled to the size that was asked (`_resample` `:297`), and the `note` field says which happened (`:386-391`); `MAX_WINDOW = 8192` (`:29`) is the only hard ceiling. `Image.fromarray(..., mode=...)` is deprecated in the pinned Pillow and will be gone in 13, so the mode is inferred from the array's own shape (`:302`, `:309-312`).

**`Camera` (`:47`) and `Light` (`:71`) are the two nodes' knobs, and `fit` is the one that makes the pane usable.** `fit=True` puts the eye at the distance the model's own bounding sphere and the requested fov need (`_fit_distance` `:188`), so a 2 mm scan and a 2 km terrain both arrive whole; direction, up and fov stay the caller's. `fit=False` puts the eye exactly where told - which is how a saved camera is reproduced, and also how a camera that misses the model is drawn: the empty-frame refusal (`:398`) is what exposes it rather than reporting a background-coloured picture as a render.

**`tests/test_preview_render_contract.py`** (new, 525 lines, 52 tests, the 22nd audit suite) is the permanent proof, so the numbers above are re-measurable after any edit instead of living in a deleted script. `FramingCase` reads the written file back and requires its own size and byte count, and requires two runs of the same node to produce the **same sha256** - a raster that drifts between runs makes every "before/after" in the Pipeline pane meaningless. `CameraCase` moves the picture (side and corner each change >1,000 px against the base frame), proves a wide lens shrinks the silhouette and a tight one grows it while fit still keeps the model inside, and holds fit true across a 6-unit tall box, an 8-unit wide box and a small one. `LightCase` is the monotonicity suite: five intensities strictly brighter in order, a lamp behind darker than one in front, the lamp colour in the pixels, a closer point lamp brighter, a wider cone lighting more, ambient carrying the frame alone. `DisplayCase` and `PointCloudCase` cover all six modes, a faceless `.ply` drawn as points with its own colours, and `point_size` reaching the frame. `FormatAndSizeCase` writes and re-reads all five encoders at the asked size, the 148 floor and the desktop ceiling, transparency accounting for every pixel, and an ask beyond `MAX_WINDOW`. `StopCase` proves a stop before the frame writes nothing and a stop after the write names the file that *is* on disk. `SourceContractCase` pins the four Open3D traps a green run cannot show, because Open3D answers each of them with a silent picture rather than an error: the engine head light never turned on, the fov written into the live intrinsic matrix (and no `parameters.intrinsic = o3d.camera.PinholeCameraIntrinsic`), the `[right, -vertical, forward]` row order, and `destroy_window()` in the `finally` - plus `'width': measured_width` present and `'width': final_width` forbidden, and no capability word the module does not deliver.

- **Three defects the suite found in its own first draft, which is the usual bill.** (1) Two tests asked for the same output name at different sizes, so the second frame overwrote the first file and `test_the_size_and_bytes_reported_are_the_ones_on_disk` read back 320x320 under a verdict that said 640x480. The helper now hashes the settings into the filename, so one ask is one file and a stale read is impossible. (2) `MAX_WINDOW` was being proven by drawing 8192x8192, which put the suite at 254 s - over the audit's flat budget for no extra truth. The cap is now tested at a patched 900 px ceiling, which exercises the same branch and says `capped at 900 px` in the note, and the suite runs in **45 s**. (3) The refusal helper returned the sentence it caught, and unittest deprecates a non-None return from a test case; it returns `None` now.
- Proof: `python -X utf8 tests/test_preview_render_contract.py` -> `Ran 52 tests in 45.21s / OK`, exit 0. `run_audit.py` gained a per-suite `timeout_seconds` (default unchanged at 120) and a distinct **TIMEOUT** status that prints the budget it exceeded, because the Known-deferred note about `verify_all_fixes` reading as a defect was exactly this ambiguity; this suite registers at 180 s (`run_audit.py:148-154`).
- **Not covered here.** No mutation list yet - `tools/mutations_preview_render.py` belongs with the engine's own sweep in 6i-1c, so the source-contract assertions are currently the only guard on the four Open3D traps. The raster is standalone: no Pipeline node calls it yet (`pipeline_engine.py:843-845` still logs "Rendering not fully implemented, skipping"), which is what 6i-1b/1c exist to close. Supersampling (`samples` 1..8 on the Render node) is not implemented here - the module draws one frame per call, and the node has to loop it or stop offering the knob.

**`tools/probe_backend_safety.py` (138 lines, new, and repaired before it was believed) - which pymeshlab filters survive a mesh that carries UVs, one process per case.** pymeshlab 2025.7 answers a bad filter with a segfault rather than an exception, and a crash inside a worker thread is a crash of the app, so each case runs in its own interpreter. The first version of this checker lied in two ways: the child converted only `isinstance(value, tuple)` into `PercentageValue`, but JSON carries `('pct', 5.0)` across the process boundary as a **list**, so `clustering`, `depth_smooth`, `isotropic_remesh` and `weld_close` reported "must be a pymeshlab.Percentage object" - a defect in the checker, not in the app (`:77-79`); and three filter names had been typed from the MeshLab menu instead of copied from the call sites - `meshing_remove_non_manifold`, `meshing_fix_normals` and `meshing_orientation_consistency_by_edge_collapses` **do not exist in 2025.7**. They are replaced by the three `src/processing/mesh_processor.py` actually calls (`meshing_repair_non_manifold_vertices` `:50`, `meshing_invert_face_orientation` `:51`, `meshing_re_orient_faces_coherently` `:53`), and the header now says where each name came from.
- The table on this machine: **no filter takes the process down when the mesh has UVs, and none drops them** - quadric decimation (both its plain and `planarquadric` forms), clustering, laplacian/taubin/depth smoothing, isotropic remesh, smart-triangle quad pairing, all four subdivisions, vertex weld and the whole repair set keep their 3,840 wedge rows. `compute_texcoord_parametrization_triangle_trivial_per_wedge` and `_flat_plane_per_wedge` both produce UVs on a bare mesh; `generate_voronoi_atlas_parametrization` runs and produces **none** (0 rows), which is why `MeshUV` parametrizes with the two trivial paths and refuses `cube`/`sphere`/`cylinder`/`smart` by name; `compute_texcoord_parametrization_harmonic` refuses a closed sphere with its own sentence ("can be applied only on meshes that have a boundary"), so it is not offered as a method either.

### Phase 6i-1b - the Pipeline engine, rewritten against the backends its own nodes name

Everything below was read out of `git show 5ebac13:src/processing/pipeline_engine.py` (1,054 lines) before it was replaced, because the sentence "the engine used to lie" is worth nothing unless the lie is quoted. It lied in six ways, all of them verified in that file:

- `export.render` logged "Rendering not fully implemented, skipping actual render" and returned `True` (`:843-845`) - a green row in a table, for a node that wrote nothing.
- twelve transform nodes logged "Transform node 'X' (transform.x) is not yet implemented. Passing data through." and returned `True` (`:997`) - the same green row for Scale, Rotate, Center, Mirror, Align.
- `transform.optimize` had `if node.parameters.get("remove_loose", True):` with the body `pass` (`:988-989`), then logged "Mesh optimized successfully" - a knob whose only effect was the sentence.
- the handlers read `factor`, `angle`, `x`/`y`/`z` while the same file's `NODE_DEFINITIONS` declared `scale`, `rotation_x`, `offset_x`. Every number typed into the pane was discarded before it reached the geometry, and no check could have caught it, because the names came from two halves of one file.
- geometry lived in `self._node_data[node.id] = {"geometry": geometry, "type": geo_type}` and was copied with `import copy` inside the execution loop (`:864`) - the wire carried Python objects, so nothing on screen was ever a fact about a file.
- seven `pyqtSignal`s (`:547-553`) meant an exception inside a node was an exception inside a slot, which PyQt6 answers by aborting the process (`0xC0000409`); and `add_connection` (`:563`) needed `engine._current_pipeline` to have been set by the caller, so the pane assigned that private attribute from four different places.

**`src/processing/pipeline_engine.py` (3,147 lines) is now a Qt-free object you call, and the module docstring says so at `:1-30` in the same words.** Three rules carry it:
