1. **A node writes a file or says why it did not.** `Item` (`:900`) is `(kind, path, label, facts)` and the wire carries items; `measure()` (`:1268`) re-opens every product and counts vertices/faces/extents/watertight/uv rows (mesh) or points/colours/normals (cloud) off the bytes, so the number in the verdict is the written file's, not the filter's claim. `PipelineEngine.each_mesh` (`:1472`) and `each_cloud` (`:1516`) are the two loops every processing node is written with, and both put their per-file verdicts in `evidence['steps']`.
2. **A knob the backend cannot receive is refused by name, in the engine's own sentence.** 65 `raise Refused` sites; 4 `except Exception`, each one turning a backend crash into a row. `smart`/`cube`/`sphere`/`cylinder`/`hdri`-style asks stay in their enums so the refusal reaches the person who asks: `orthographic` camera `:1985`, `hdri` lamp `:2027`, `exr` on the three image nodes `:2708`/`:2799`/`:2824`, `clay` display `:2712`. An `hdri` refusal says which lamp kinds exist; `unwrap_uv` hands the method straight to `MeshUV.parametrize` (`src/processing/mesh_processor.py:1471`), which runs only the two pymeshlab paths `tools/probe_backend_safety.py` measured and refuses the rest.
3. **A refusal is a status, not a crash, and it does not empty the wire.** `Refused` `:883` / `Invalid` `:887` / `Stopped` `:891` answer `STATUSES` `:76` = done / refused / invalid / failed / stopped / skipped, and `_carried_on` (`:1792`) hands the next node the stream that arrived at the node that said no - so `Scale(factor=0)` in the middle of a chain shows as one refused row and the export still exports. Only `failed` aborts. `coerce()` (`:1169`) deliberately ignores min/max ("they seed the pane's box, they do not gate the backend"), so typing 1e9 into a factor reaches the geometry and the extents say what happened; `coerce_node` (`:1239`) raises `Invalid` with every bad knob at once.

The graph rules moved to the object that owns the graph: `Pipeline` (`:937`) refuses a self-wire, a duplicate wire, a loop and a missing endpoint, and `problems()` (`:1080`) answers sentences - "X has nothing on its wire; connect a Load node to it", "the wires contain a loop", "a wire starts at ghost, which is not in this pipeline". `CLOUD_VERB_KNOBS` (`:97`) is the one table of which of the 18 cloud verbs reads which knobs, and the note a Process-Point-Cloud row prints is built from that table, so the sentence cannot drift from the mapping: `{verb} reads <sorted reads>` plus "N knob(s) of this node belong to other verbs and were not sent". `each_mesh`'s batch note dedupes identical step sentences and caps at 3 with "and N further step(s), each with its own numbers under this row" - all verdicts stay in `evidence['steps']`.

Disk is part of the contract: `save_pipeline` (`:3059`) writes then re-reads and counts nodes and wires back; `load_pipeline` (`:3086`) never touches the file and names each damage in its own words - schema mismatch ("older node set"), two nodes both called `n1` ("a wire into one of them is a coin toss"), unknown definition, a knob the node type does not have any more, a dangling wire, a loop, an empty pipeline. And because the previous engine's fatal bug was a definition pointing at a method that did not exist, the file now exports `definition_not_backed` (`:3033`) and `unbacked_definitions` (`:3049`), which assert each `handler` string resolves on this module: **all 26 definitions are backed, and a suite runs that check on every audit**.

Backends that the rewrite needed and did not exist: `mesh_processor.py` (+411/-21) - `MeshTransformer.scale/center/rotate/align_to_plane/mirror` (`:1056`, `:1089`, `:1139`, `:1182`, `:1238`) are file-in/file-out now, each returning a verdict with the extents counted off the output, and the 21 deleted lines were the old in-memory signatures; `MeshUV` (`:1391`) is new. `preview_render.py` (+30/-7) gained `supersample`, which folds into the window ceiling (`MAX_WINDOW // max(1, final_width, final_height)`) - that is what makes the Render node's `samples` knob a real ask instead of decoration. `lod_generator.py` (+11) exposes `lod_ratios` so the Generate LOD node reads the app's own ratios instead of the pane guessing.

### Phase 6i-2 - the pane shows the run's answers, the 22nd audit suite proves it, and 19 defects are pinned

The old pane (`git show 5ebac13:src/ui/pipeline_editor_tab.py`, 1,101 lines) was where the engine's lies became visible, and three of them were the pane's own:

- `run_pipeline` commented "# Execute in separate thread" and then called `QTimer.singleShot(100, lambda: self.engine.execute_pipeline(self.current_pipeline))` (`:1063`) - the whole graph ran on the GUI thread, one deferred call later. A 26-node chain froze the window and the "thread" never existed.
- `stop_pipeline` (`:1065-1067`) re-enabled Run and disabled Stop before anything had stopped, because `engine.stop()` flipped a flag no handler read.
- it connected five of the engine's seven signals (`:753-758`); the two it dropped were `progress_updated` and `pipeline_failed`, so a failed run's only report was a line of prose, and it set the engine's private `_current_pipeline` from four places (`:897`, `:914`, `:938`, `:953`) so that a connection could be checked at all.

**`src/ui/pipeline_editor_tab.py` (1,302 lines) has no compute imports and no engine private attributes.** `PipelineRunThread` (`:824`) owns a `PipelineEngine(staging_root=get_temp_directory())` and emits three signals (`log_line`, `node_state`, `run_finished`) - the Qt boundary lives only in the worker, which is why an exception in a node is now a red row rather than a process abort, and why the pane can be driven headless. Stop is a flag the engine reads between files (`request_stop`, `:844`): the node in flight finishes the file it is on. The table's colours come from `STATUS_COLORS` (`:39`) keyed on `STATUSES`, `:1198` refuses a second run with "NOT STARTED: a run is already in progress. Stop it first." and `_reopen_pane` (`:1189`) puts the controls back from the run's own finish. A modal is opened only when `counts['failed']` is non-zero (`:1288`): a node that said no is a row, not a dialog, and a suite asserts `MODALS == []` across every refusal path. `remove_stale` (`:782`) is the control for a knob an older `.json` carried - the box stays visible with its value, and the button deletes it rather than the run inventing a default.

**`tests/test_pipeline_contract.py` (1,019 lines, 55 tests, 3.4 s) is the 22nd entry of the audit's 23 (`run_audit.py:155-161`).** The engine half (test_01-35) starts with `unbacked_definitions()` empty and knob-type coverage - a declared `ParameterType` no node uses is a defect in the list, not a spare. Then the claims: every done row names a file that exists with bytes in it, and the exported cube re-measures to 12 faces; the four source refusals and three destination refusals each say the file and the reason; `factor=0.0` reaches the geometry and the extents come back `[0, 0, 0]`; graph rules; determinism (two identical runs, identical verdicts); the batch note cap over six differently sized meshes; the 18 verb-knob sentences read off `CLOUD_VERB_KNOBS` rather than retyped; the raster and camera-roll rows; Export Stats' JSON parsed back, with `dict(payload['measured']) == measure(payload['file'])` and the wire unchanged; staging surviving a run; and pipelines on disk, where a damaged file is loaded as it stands with every damage named. The pane half (test_40-60b): one widget per knob across all 26 definitions, palette completeness off `NODE_DEFINITIONS`, 1e9 / -1e9 / 2,000,000,000 typed into the boxes, a bad colour kept verbatim so the run refuses it, an AST walk proving the pane imports no `open3d`/`pymeshlab`/`trimesh`/`PIL`/`numpy` and does import the engine, flat paint plus an emoji scan, table rows equal to engine rows, refusal paints refused with no modal, the one-run guard, a stop, a worker held by reference until it finishes, an unknown status word flagged rather than painted, the node layout surviving save+reload, the Library hand-off arriving as a Load Model, `staging_root == get_temp_directory()`, and two knob-level refusals (`format='tifff'`, `output_path=3`) that must not open a dialog.

**`tools/mutations_pipeline.py` (154 lines, 19 defects) is the list, run by `python -X utf8 tools/mutation_check.py tests/test_pipeline_contract.py tools/mutations_pipeline.py`.** Nine are engine sentences (`row['files'] = []`, `_carried_on` -> `return []`, `keep_staging` -> `if True:`, the batch cap -> `if False:`, `reads = []`, `unused = set()`, ENUM validation -> `if True:`, the stats payload's `measured: {}` and its `if geometry:` -> `if False:`, loading rewriting the file) and ten are pane behaviour (a widget never registered, `node.position` no longer read from the scene, any status word painted, the one-run guard removed, the worker not retained, a refusal raised to a crash modal, staging in the current directory). **All 19 defects were caught by a named test, none as a CRASHED line.**

- **Two defects the injection pass found in the suite itself, which is the bill for writing 55 tests at once.** (1) `_carried_on` -> `return []` survived: the suite ran refusals one node deep and never asked what the *next* node received, so a chain whose middle node refused silently emptied the wire - and the export would have reported "nothing on its wire" while the input sat there unread. `test_29b_a_refusal_in_the_middle_leaves_the_next_node_s_work_intact` is that test. (2) `facts['faces'] = 4` survived: every fixture in the file was a tetrahedron, so the read-back was proving a constant that happened to be right - the same class of self-answering setup as Point Clouds test_38. A 12-face `CUBE` fixture (`test_pipeline_contract.py:92`) now carries test_04 and test_28, so a measured 12 and a hardcoded 4 are different sentences.
- Proof: `python -X utf8 tests/test_pipeline_contract.py` -> `Ran 55 tests in 3.439s / OK`, exit 0; `tools/mutation_check.py` -> `all 19 defects were caught by a named test`. Nine sibling suites re-ran after the shared-state edits (`main_window.py` constructor, `library_tab.py` hand-off, `preview_render.py` signature, `mesh_processor.py` API): `test_all_tabs_headless`, `test_preview_render_contract`, `test_point_clouds_contract`, `test_preprocessing_contract`, `test_mesh_tools_contract`, `test_rendering_tab_contract`, `test_automation_batch_contract`, `test_dashboard_contract`, `test_format_converter_contract` - all OK. Both sources were byte-identical after the mutation sweep.
- Accounting for this area: `pipeline_engine.py` 1,054 -> 3,147 (+2,786/-693; the 693 deletions are the QObject with its seven signals, the `_node_data` geometry cache and its `import copy`, the twelve "not yet implemented" narrations, the `export.render` placeholder, the `remove_loose: pass` body, the 20-line `add_connection` prose, and handlers reading knobs their definitions never declared); `pipeline_editor_tab.py` 1,101 -> 1,302 (+1,161/-960; deleted: five connections to signals the new engine does not emit, four `_current_pipeline` assignments, the fake-thread `singleShot`, `stop_pipeline`'s unconditional button reset, and the `is_executing`/`is_completed`/`is_failed` booleans that painted a row from a signal rather than a verdict); `mesh_processor.py` +411/-21; `preview_render.py` +30/-7; `lod_generator.py` +11; `library_tab.py` +17/-9; `main_window.py` +1/-1; `run_audit.py` +7; `tests/test_pipeline_contract.py` 1,019 new; `tools/mutations_pipeline.py` 154 new.
- **What this suite does not prove, stated so a green sweep is not read as more than it is.** The raster still has no mutation list of its own - three of the 19 entries touch the engine's image call sites, but `tools/mutations_preview_render.py` and the four Open3D traps stay guarded only by `test_preview_render_contract`'s source-contract assertions. `supersample` is a resolution-multiple anti-alias on the app's own raster: it is not path tracing, and the word "render" on the node means the preview RenderFox makes for itself, which the docstring says and the refusal on `exr`/`clay` enforces - not a final image, and final images remain Blender's, Unreal's and Cinema4D's. The pane has no undo, one thread per run, and its LOD rows are checked by re-measuring the files the node wrote, not by opening them in another tool. Nobody has clicked this pane with a real mouse: every drive is offscreen Qt, so the interactive pass is still the user's, and the 22-second-to-open question (#9) is untouched by this area.


### Phase 6j-1 - the two machine panes answer this machine, and the drive the app writes to is the drive it reports

Both panes were reading a computer that is not this one. Measured here before touching anything, because every number below is the reason the code changed:

- one display adapter: Intel(R) Iris(R) Xe Graphics, Intel Corporation, driver 31.0.101.5186, `AdapterRAM` 128 MB - which is the 32-bit field Windows keeps, not the card's usable memory. `GPUtil.getGPUs()` returns `[]` on this machine: it reads NVIDIA through `nvidia-smi`, AMD through `rocm-smi`, Intel through `intel_gpu_top` (`Linux only`, its own docstring says), and none of those answers here.
- one physical disk (KBG50ZNV1T02 KIOXIA, `MediaType` 4 = SSD) carrying **nine partitions**, so `C:`, `D:`, `E:` and `F:` genuinely all sit on the same SSD. The join `DriveLetter -> DiskNumber -> DeviceId` is therefore correct as written, and a first reading of the output ("every letter says SSD, the join must be broken") was wrong - `Get-Partition` proved it.
- the app's own temp directory is `C:\Users\AYUSHM~1\AppData\Local\Temp\renderfox`, and **C: is 90.8% used / 19.7 GB free**, while `psutil.disk_usage('/')` - the question the health check actually asked - answers D: at **25.8%**. That is the difference between "run out of room" and "all right" on the machine that ships this build.

**What was false before, and where.** `SystemInfo.get_gpu_info()` built `{'name': 'Unknown GPU', 'details': 'Install GPUtil package for GPU monitoring'}` on a machine with GPUtil installed, and `HealthChecker.check_gpu_availability()` called that row **"1 GPU(s) available"**, which is what let the Diagnostics pane call the machine healthy without knowing which chip is in it. `check_disk_space()` measured `psutil.disk_usage('/')` and printed a percentage with no drive named. The Performance pane's timer wrote `"45%"`, `"6,847 MB"`, `"72°C"`, `"285W"` into its four GPU rows **every two seconds** - those four literals were in the source, and `on_metrics_updated` also stamped `gpu_percent` (a figure from a CPU-side reading) over whatever the adapter row said. `get_cpu_info()` used `psutil.cpu_percent(interval=None)`, which returns the mean since the last call and, on the first call, `0.0`: the pane printed a number that measured no window. The CPU badge was painted from `health_checks['memory']` - the file called `update_health_indicator_status(self.cpu_health, status_map.get(mem_status...))` - and any status that was not exactly `healthy` printed `[OK]`, so a critical drive got the warning word. `check_package()` did `__import__(package_name.replace('-','_'))`, so it called `pillow` and `scikit-learn` missing on a machine that has both, and paid **7.63 s** to say it. `get_recommendations()` prefixed every line with `⚠️`/`✅` and never looked at the CPU check at all.

**`src/features/diagnostic_engine.py` (823 lines) now answers two separate questions and says which one it asked.** *Which adapter is installed* is the Windows device store (`_cim_rows`, one PowerShell question that returns rows **or** the reason there are none - off-Windows, no shell on PATH, timeout, nonzero exit with its first error line, silence, nonsense JSON, one row delivered as a dict); *what it is doing right now* is GPUtil, and when GPUtil answers nothing the report carries `load_reason` as its own field (`:338`) instead of a `0`. `get_gpu_report()` (`:182`) returns `{adapters, reasons, load_reason, text, identity_source}`, `_gpu_text` (`:251`) is the one block of words both panes print - `Vendor: not reported` rather than `Vendor: None`, the 32-bit memory caveat said once, `Identified by: <which check said this>` - and `check_gpu_availability` (`:571`) closes with the true pair of sentences: adapters "identified here: Intel(...) . Nothing reports what it is doing right now, because GPUtil is installed but answered no adapter...".

**One volume reader, one helper that picks the row, and the verdict travels with it.** `get_storage_info()` (`:107`) is the only volume reader left in the app: psutil for the byte sizes, Windows for the disk kind and the disk behind each letter, `f'disk kind {raw}'` plus a reason for a number this app has no name for, and `working_volume(storage, path)` (`:86`) - longest matching mount prefix, whole drive letter only, case-insensitive, tolerant of a mount reported as `C:` or `C:\` - is what every caller uses to answer "which drive does this write land on". `check_disk_space` (`:523`) measures **that** volume and prints its name: `19.7 GB free on C:\ (KBG50ZNV1T02 KIOXIA) (90.8% used)` with `warning`, where it used to print `Disk usage at 25.8%` with `healthy`. A path no mounted volume carries returns `free_gb: None` and says so instead of inventing a figure. `PerformanceTab.paint_storage_info` (`:865`) and the Diagnostics text both read the same helper; `report['disk']` (a second, differently-shaped volume list) is gone.

**One measurement per report, and the vocabulary is the backend's.** `get_full_system_report()` (`:764`) takes the storage, adapter and CPU readings once and hands them to `run_all_checks(storage, gpu, cpu)` (`:644`), so a row's figures and the word beside it are the same look at the machine: `run_all_checks` measured for itself in **1.83 s**, and with shared readings it costs **0.00 s**; the whole report is 2.41 s. `check_cpu_usage` (`:486`) is a CPU verdict (`>95 critical, >85 warning`) built from the sample the pane shows. `check_package` (`:396`) looks the name up in the package records - `importlib.util.find_spec` on the module name from `MODULE_NAMES` (`:390`, `pillow -> PIL`, `scikit-learn -> sklearn`) plus `importlib.metadata.version` - so **nothing is executed**: 7.63 s -> **0.06 s** (127x), `pillow 11.3.0` and `scikit-learn 1.7.1` are reported installed, and a library the installer recorded but whose module name differs is still not called missing. `get_recommendations(health)` (`:803`) walks all five checks and prefixes `Warning:` / `Good:` in plain ASCII because that is how the issues table decides a row's column - the picture characters made every line, including "all systems healthy", unclassifiable.

**The two panes paint what they are handed.** Performance: the GPU rows open with `GPU_NOT_READ_NOTE` / `GPU_POWER_NOTE` (`:35`, `:292`) instead of `0%`/`0 MB`/`0°C`/`0W`, and the power row says outright that nothing in this build measures chip power; `detect_gpu_hardware` (`:795`) and `refresh_storage_info` (`:838`) run on the worker pool with `progress_callback=None` and `signals.error` connected, so a Windows question that dies names the failure instead of leaving zeros, and the two-second timer touches nothing but the readings it is given. Diagnostics: `words`/`mark` (`:759`) file `healthy`/`warning`/`critical`/missing as `[OK]`/`[Warning]`/`[Critical]`/`[Not checked]`, the overall line counts the five checks the pane prints (`:767`) rather than whatever answered, `update_health_indicators` (`:798`) pairs each badge with its own check, and `run_full_diagnostic` prints a `=== STORAGE ===` block (`:869`) with every volume, its filesystem, its used-of figures, its disk kind, the reasons Windows did not say, and the one line that names where the app writes.

**`tests/test_hardware_report.py` (1,127 lines, 86 tests, 27.0 s, exit 0) is the 24th entry of the audit (`run_audit.py:162-168`).** It asks the live machine first (`test_01-08`: whatever it answers, the shape holds - a reason wherever a figure could not be), then pins the rules with fixtures: `working_volume` prefix, case, longest-mount and no-match (`test_10-15`); the disk verdict's thresholds, its named volume, and **"the start-up drive is never asked about"** read off the parsed source (`test_20-25`); all six ways a Windows question fails (`test_30-36`); adapter identity mapping, the `None` leaks, JSON-serialisability and the OpenGL-only answer (`test_40-47`); rounding, count disagreement and `load_reason` as a field (`test_50-53`); the verdict wording singular/plural/hot/partial (`test_70-75`); the CPU verdict following its sample (`test_80-83`); one-measurement-per-report with the second question rigged to raise (`test_90-93`); the dependency translation, its speed, and a source pin that no library name appears in the check that only asks if it exists (`test_100-105`); the Performance pane's seeding, its working volume, its unknown-kind reason, its no-adapter answer, its timer and its two failed-reader paths (`test_110-119`); the Diagnostics pane's one-badge-per-check, missing-check words, `[Critical]` filing, `=== STORAGE ===` and the four invented figures gone from the parsed file rather than only from the screen (`test_130-136`); and the letter-to-disk join, which is seven tests because it has seven ways to come up short (`test_140-146`).

**`tools/mutations_hardware.py` (317 lines, 41 defects) is the list, run by `python -X utf8 tools/mutation_check.py tests/test_hardware_report.py tools/mutations_hardware.py`.** Twenty-two are backend sentences (the CPU window, the pick of the write volume, the whole-drive prefix rule, the four disk-join failure paths, `load_reason` as a field, the rounding, the `None` leak, the plural, the thresholds, two ignored shared readings, a report that measures twice, advice invented from a status word, two name translations, and a check that loads what it is only asking about) and nineteen are pane behaviour (four seeded figures, the first-row volume, the `if kind:` truth test, an unread adapter shown idle, four `Worker` signature/fault-channel sites, three badge and mark defaults, the storage block, the overall count). **All 41 defects were caught by a named test, none as a CRASHED line, and both sources were byte-identical after the sweep** (`--list` reports 41 unique anchors, which is also the residue check).

- **Two defects the injection pass found in the suite itself, both worth the two hours they cost.** (1) A green suite exited **127** (`0xC0000409`): `Test12.tearDown` called `self.tab.deleteLater()` and left the widget's death in the event queue the interpreter reaches on its way out, so PyQt6 aborted the process after `OK` was printed - a run the audit reads as a suite that never reported. The fix is the pattern the other GUI suites already use (`waitForDone` on the global pool, stop the pane's timer, `close()`, `processEvents()`), and the rule now carried forward is **check the exit code of a green suite, not only its report line**. (2) Defect 36 (`mark`'s fallback becoming `[OK]`) **survived**: the missing-check rule was pinned for the badges but never for the printed text, and while chasing it the overall line was found to be the same defect twice - it counted `health.values()`, so a report whose storage check never ran printed "All systems operational". `test_136_a_check_that_never_ran_is_not_counted_as_healthy` now pins both, `check`-that-did-not-run prints `[Not checked]` and the line `no storage check ran`, and defect 21's `expect` was corrected to `test_93` after it first reported CRASHED - which was this suite's exit-code abort wearing a different mask.
- Proof: `python -X utf8 tests/test_hardware_report.py` -> `Ran 86 tests in 27.002s / OK`, **exit 0**; mutation sweep -> `all 41 defects were caught by a named test`. Five sibling suites re-ran because `diagnostic_engine.py` is shared state: `test_all_tabs_headless` (14 tests, OK, exit 0), `verify_all_fixes` (9 tests, 33.8 s, OK), `test_automation_monitor_contract` (88, OK), `test_automation_tasks_contract` (62, OK), `test_pipeline_contract` (55, 4.3 s, OK). Nothing else in the app imports the changed functions (`grep` over `src/`, `tests/`, `main.py`): the only remaining callers are `diagnostics_tab.py`'s two `get_full_system_report()` sites and `PerformanceTab`.
- Accounting for this area: `diagnostic_engine.py` 521 -> 823 (+472/-170; the 170 deletions are `get_disk_info` whole - a second psutil-only volume list whose rows were pre-formatted strings - the old `get_gpu_info` body with its GPUtil-string rows, its `wmic path win32_VideoController` fallback, its OpenGL fallback and its `Unknown GPU / Install GPUtil` placeholder row, `check_package`'s `__import__` with the `pyassimp` special case and three except branches, `check_disk_space`'s `disk_usage('/')` version and its out-of-vocabulary `'status': 'error'`, `check_gpu_availability`'s `f"{len(gpus)} GPU(s) available"` with the `°C`-string temperature parse and a bare `except: pass`, the four `⚠️`/`✅` recommendation lines plus the missing CPU line, the two `cpu_percent(interval=None)` calls, and the report's `'disk'`/no-arg `run_all_checks()` lines); `performance_tab.py` 1,024 -> 1,102 (+128/-50; deleted: the four `0%`/`0 MB`/`0°C`/`0W` seeds, the timer's `"45%"`/`"6,847 MB"`/`"72°C"`/`"285W"` block and its `gpu_percent` stamp, the `psutil.disk_usage('/')` storage block, the pane's own 18-line GPU text loop over `get_gpu_info()`, an unused `get_temp_directory` import and a stale `QListWidget` import fragment); `diagnostics_tab.py` 1,151 -> 1,181 (+71/-41; deleted: `import os` and its temp-dir import, `disk = report['disk'][0]`, the CPU badge painted from `mem_status`, the four hand-written `[OK]/[Warning]` ternaries, the 11-line per-GPU print loop that duplicated `_gpu_text`, and `get_recommendations()` called with nothing); `run_audit.py` +7; `REPAIR_LOG.md` this entry +3 verification lines; `tests/test_hardware_report.py` 1,127 new; `tools/mutations_hardware.py` 317 new. One throwaway probe (`rf6j_probe.py` in the temp directory) was deleted; no other file in the tree changed.
- **What this area does not prove, stated so a green sweep is not read as more than it is.** No real render was run and none is claimed: every figure here is a system reading, and the app still has no renderer on this machine. The Performance pane's two **benchmark** buttons are still defective on purpose - `run_cpu_benchmark` (`:911`) and `run_gpu_benchmark` (`:951`) keep the `def worker_func():` signature the house worker rejects, still invent "Top 1%"/"Excellent" ranks, and six of that pane's buttons have no `clicked` receiver at all; that is 6j-3, and `test_119` says in its own docstring which two readers it covers. `diagnostic_engine.PerformanceBenchmark` (the second ratings engine the Diagnostics buttons use at `diagnostics_tab.py:934`/`:990`) is untouched - 6j-2. GPU **power** is not measured by anything in this build, which is what the row now says. The adapter answer comes from the Windows device store plus GPUtil and nothing else: a machine that reports through another route still shows `load_reason`. Nobody has clicked either pane with a real mouse - every drive here is offscreen Qt, so the interactive pass is still the user's, and the 22-second-to-open question (#9) is untouched.
- **Corrected by 6j-2, so the bullet above is not read as today's state.** The two benchmark buttons this bullet left defective on purpose are fixed: `run_cpu_benchmark` (`performance_tab.py:912`) delegates to `start_benchmark('cpu', 'CPU')` (`:925`), the GPU button is now `run_geometry_benchmark` (`:916`) over a `Geometry Benchmark` label, the pane's `run_gpu_benchmark` is deleted rather than relabelled, the "Top 1%"/"Excellent" rows went with the two `on_result` closures that painted them, and `diagnostic_engine.PerformanceBenchmark` is **deleted**, not untouched - `benchmark_engine()` is the one object both panes now reach. Still open for 6j-3, re-measured after those fixes: `grep` for a receiver on `benchmark_memory_btn`, `benchmark_storage_btn`, `export_results_btn`, `clear_results_btn`, `save_profile_btn` and `trim_memory_btn` returns **0 connections for all six** (each is built and placed and nothing listens), even though the engine now performs the memory and storage kinds those two buttons ask for; and `performance_tab.py`'s `max_threads_spin` has no receiver either, while `postprocessing_tab.py:510-513` builds its own `max_threads` box *with* one - two knobs, one setting, one of which is decoration.

### Phase 6j-1 companion: what the pane still owes (carried into 6j-2 / 6j-3)

- `src/utils/system_utils.py` keeps a **third** copy of `get_gpu_info()` (`:269`) and a `check_disk_space(path, required_bytes)` (`:383`). Nothing imports that module (`grep -r "system_utils" src/ tests/ main.py` -> no matches), so no user sees a contradiction today; deleting it belongs with the dead-module pass (#10) so the removal and its stale wiring land in one commit rather than being patched into this one. **Phase 6r landed that commit**: the module is gone with the other four, `git show 92a5961:src/utils/system_utils.py` is the last copy anyone can read, and the two functions this bullet names are the pair whose redundancy is a file:line reading rather than a guarded fact - see 6r's own non-proof bullet.
- The Performance pane's Optimization knobs still duplicate the Post-Processing pane's: `max_threads_spin` (`performance_tab.py:534`, range 1-64, default 8) and `max_threads` (`postprocessing_tab.py:510`, range 1-16, default 4) are one setting with two boxes and two answers, and six of the pane's fourteen buttons have no `clicked` receiver at all - `benchmark_memory_btn` (`:422`), `benchmark_storage_btn` (`:424`), `export_results_btn` (`:484`), `clear_results_btn` (`:486`), `save_profile_btn` (`:518`), `trim_memory_btn` (`:665`). All of it is 6j-3, where the pane either reaches the engine or refuses by name.

### Phase 6j-2 - the frame rate comes from frames, the benchmark comes from work, and one machine has one engine

**What was false before, and where.** `PerformanceMonitor.sample` (HEAD `:142-157`) computed `frame_time = current_time - self._last_frame_time`, where `current_time` is the wall clock and `_last_frame_time` is when the *previous monitoring sample* ran - the timer fires once a second whether or not anything drew (`start_monitoring(interval_ms=1000)`, HEAD `:102`, called from `performance_tab.py:770` at HEAD), so the app's only frame rate was `~1.0 fps / ~1000 ms` for a viewer sitting behind another tab - and the `else 0.016` on the line below it could never be reached, because the `append` above it put a sample in the deque first. `gpu_percent: float = 0.0` was the type default and the value both when the adapter was idle and when nothing read it, which on this machine - GPUtil answers no adapter, pinned in 6j-1 - is every second. `get_average_metrics` / `get_peak_metrics` averaged and maxed those fields, so a record of nothing came out as a number. `BottleneckDetector.get_performance_score` answered **100** when its inputs were missing. `OptimizationSuggester` promised "30-50%" and "double or triple FPS" from no measurement of either. The two benchmark engines were `performance_analyzer`'s `run_cpu_benchmark`, whose `score` was `int(ops_per_second * 1000)`, and `diagnostic_engine.PerformanceBenchmark`, which summed `math.sqrt` over a million integers and graded the elapsed time Excellent/Good/Average on thresholds that loop always beat - and did the summing on the interface thread, so the pane stopped answering while it claimed to be measuring. The Performance pane's four columns were `["Component", "Score", "Performance", "Rank"]` holding `f"{result['score']:,}"`, `"Excellent"` and `"Top 1%"` - a percentile against a leaderboard RenderFox has never collected from anybody - and its button said `GPU Benchmark` over Open3D's CPU build. Its `def worker_func():` could not take the `progress_callback` the house `Worker` always passes (`src/utils/worker.py`), `signals.error` was not connected, and `setRange(0, 0)` made Qt throw away every `setValue` the engine reported, because **`QProgressBar` ignores `setValue` while `minimum == maximum`**. `main.py:global_exception_handler` called `get_telemetry().log_error(...)`, a method that does not exist (`track_error` is the one that does), inside a bare `except:` - so an unhandled crash left no record on disk while the Diagnostics pane printed a crash count as if it had read one. Two more fabrications came out of the same pane's construction, and they were the only ones here that a user saw without clicking anything: HEAD's `create_reports_tab` (`:391`) seeded `reports_list` with four invented rows - `Crash Report - 2024-08-29 14:30:15`, `Memory Leak Report - 2024-08-29 12:15:22`, `Performance Report - 2024-08-29 10:05:45`, `System Report - 2024-08-29 09:30:12` (`:432-440`) - so the Reports pane opened with four crashes and leaks RenderFox had never had, and its Application State Dump printed fourteen lines about a machine and a session it had not asked about (HEAD `:1028-1050`, one f-string handed straight to `debug_output` at `:1052`): `Platform: Windows 11`, `Python Version: 3.11.5`, `PyQt Version: 6.5.2`, `Available Memory: 8.5 GB`, `Current Tab: Diagnostics`, `Loaded Models: 3`, `Active Processes: 12`, `GPU Acceleration: Enabled`, `Total Allocated: 1.2 GB`, `Peak Usage: 1.8 GB`, `Active Objects: 45,232`, `- Model loaded: building.obj (2.3MB)`, `- Mesh processing completed`, `- Cache cleared (150MB freed)`. Measured on this machine: Python **3.11.8**, PyQt **6.7.1** over Qt **6.7.1** - so two of those four lines were false the moment they were printed. The seeded rows are gone and a list row now appears only when the app really generates a report (`diagnostics_tab.py:1494`); the dump reads `SystemInfo.get_build_info()` (`diagnostic_engine.py:50`), which names the operating system build, architecture, processor, interpreter, whether the process is frozen, and the Qt and PyQt runtimes - and answers None with a reason when PyQt's own version strings cannot be imported.

**`src/utils/frame_clock.py` (134 lines, new) is the app's only source of frame timing.** Each view stamps its own finished frames (`note_frame` `:36`, `note_failure` `:42`, `note_blocked` `:46`) and `stats()` (`:57`) is arithmetic over the gaps between stamps, with the window anchored on *now* rather than on the newest stamp so a burst that stopped five seconds ago cannot keep being reported as the current rate. Where there are no gaps to take it names the kind of nothing: no stamps at all (`"no 3D view has drawn a frame since the app started"` plus what the view itself said, when it said anything), nothing inside the window, one frame (`"one frame is not a rate"`), or stamps sharing a timestamp. `ModelViewer3D` is the only stamping view: `frame_clock.note_blocked(...)` where PyOpenGL could not be imported (`model_viewer.py:1220`), `note_frame(name)` after a `paintGL` that finished (`:1518`) and `note_failure(e)` in its `except` (`:1521`) - so a viewer that raises every paint is a count of failures, not a frame rate. `shared_clock()` (`:119`) is the one object the views stamp and both panes read; `diagnostics_tab._live_readings` (`:1050`) and `diagnostic_engine:1082` take its `stats()` in the same pass as the CPU and memory readings, so the frame lines and the machine lines describe one window.

**One engine, four kinds, and the vocabulary is the engine's.** `BenchmarkRunner` (`performance_analyzer.py:688`) performs `KINDS = ('cpu', 'memory', 'storage', 'geometry')` (`:699`) - square float64 matrix multiplies for a bounded time with the BLAS thread count in the machine note, a memory copy, a write into `get_temp_directory()` i.e. the volume the app actually writes to, and Open3D normal estimation naming the device that answered - each returning `ran` / `reason` / `work` / `result` / `machine` / `measured_at`, and `run` (`:721`) answering a fifth kind with `'"gpu" is not work this engine performs; it performs cpu, memory, storage, geometry'` rather than a number. `describe` (`:1015`) and `row_cells` (`:1039`) are the two places the sentences are built, so the paragraph and the table cell cannot disagree, and `BenchmarkHistory` (`:600`) keeps only this app's own earlier runs, which is what the column `Against this app's own earlier runs` means. `benchmark_engine()` (`:1185`) is the singleton the Diagnostics pane asks for (`diagnostics_tab.py:1035`) - and now also the object the Performance pane reaches, because `PerformanceAnalyzer.__init__` kept a second `BenchmarkRunner()` while the module docstring claimed both panes shared one.

**The pane paints what it is handed.** The two benchmark buttons converge on one method: `run_cpu_benchmark` (`performance_tab.py:912`) is still the CPU button's receiver (`:759`) and is now a one-line delegator to `start_benchmark('cpu', 'CPU')` (`:925`), which calls `engine.run(kind, record=True, progress_callback=progress_callback)` inside the house `Worker`, connects `signals.error` to `on_benchmark_failed`, connects the progress signal to the bar and keeps the bar's range at `(0, 100)` with `setValue(0)` at the start of both run paths (`:935`, `:1008`), so a reported percentage reaches the screen. The pane's `run_gpu_benchmark` is deleted and `run_geometry_benchmark` (`:916`) sits on the button relabelled `Geometry Benchmark`. In the engine the old verb `run_gpu_benchmark` (`performance_analyzer.py:1008`) answers with a refusal naming the kind to press instead; `run_cpu_benchmark` (`:1005`) is an alias that nothing in `src/` or `tests/` calls - the pane reaches `engine.run('cpu')` - so it goes in the sweep-hole commit, where a suite can prove the deletion rather than me asserting it. The headers are `Kind / What ran / Measured / Against this app's own earlier runs`; `show_benchmark_result` paints `BenchmarkRunner.row_cells(result)` and nothing else, and `test_106` walks those six methods' syntax trees and fails on any numeric literal or rating word they hold.

**Four holes this pass found - three of them by a mutation surviving, one by reading a docstring.** (1) Defect 50 (`peak_wset` vs `peak_rss`) **survived** because the fallback it tests is platform-inert here: `psutil` on Windows always has `peak_wset`, so no mutation of a branch this OS never takes can be noticed. `test_158_the_peak_is_read_under_the_name_the_records_of_either_platform_use` closes it by handing `Process.memory_info` four shaped records - Windows-only, other-platform-only, both, neither - and demanding `40.0 / 30.0 / 40.0 / None-with-a-reason` and that the "neither" row lists the fields that *were* present. (2) Defect 58 was reported CRASHED and then, standalone, **survived**: nothing drove `run_engine_kinds` at all, so the hardware test's own worker signature was unpinned - `test_119`'s docstring had deferred those buttons to 6j-3 (`tests/test_hardware_report.py:849`). `test_177b_the_hardware_test_button_hands_the_engine_a_worker_it_can_answer` drives `run_hardware_test()` through a fake engine that records whether it was handed a callable, reads **both** channels, and fails if the pane took the failure path. (3) Retargeting 58 exposed a fourth: with the worker signature pinned, deleting `worker.signals.error.connect(self.on_benchmark_failed)` **survived**, because 177b only ever takes the success path - so `test_177c_a_hardware_test_that_raises_is_said_out_loud` runs an engine that raises and requires `Benchmark failed: RuntimeError: ...` on the screen the button writes to, and defect 59's `expect` moved to it. (4) The second engine inside `PerformanceAnalyzer` was found by reading the singleton's own docstring against the code, not by a mutation: `test_90` now asserts `benchmark_engine() is PerformanceAnalyzer().benchmark` and that both share one `history`, with mutation 88 pinning it.

**Corrections owed to this log.** `src/utils/file_utils.py` was listed as a dead file in Phase 2 and is not: `get_temp_directory` (`:232`) has three product importers (`diagnostic_engine.py:219`, `performance_analyzer.py:871`, `pipeline_editor_tab.py:31`) - **four, not three: Phase 6r's import walk adds `ai_asset_tagger.py:168`, and this grep missed it for the same reason it missed the module itself, a relative `from ..utils.file_utils import ...` line holds no dotted name**, so it must not be deleted by #10; the other 23 names in it are unreferenced and that is the part that is dead. `src/utils/system_utils.py` (1,059 lines) now genuinely has zero importers - `grep` over `src/`, `tests/`, `main.py` returns nothing - so its third `get_gpu_info()` and its `check_disk_space()` die with the dead-module pass, as 6j-1 said.

**A figure this log and four docstrings got wrong, and what the measurement says.** This section first said HEAD's frame fallback printed "16.7 ms, the frame time of a demo that never ran". It did not print it, and the figure was never 16.7: HEAD:146's fallback constant is `0.016` (16 ms, and 62.5 fps), and it is unreachable because HEAD:144 appends a sample to `_frame_times` on the line above, so the deque is never empty when the ternary runs. The number a user would have got is the one the arithmetic makes: `_frame_times` held the wall-clock gap between two monitoring samples, the timer fires at 1000 ms (`start_monitoring(interval_ms=1000)`, HEAD:102, called from `performance_tab.py:770` at HEAD), so every sample read `~1.0 fps / ~1000.0 ms` whatever the screen did. Second half of the correction, and the more useful one: **nothing displayed any of it.** `grep` over HEAD `src/`, `main.py` and `tests/` finds no reader of `fps` or `frame_time_ms` in either pane, `get_current_analysis()` - the only caller of `analyze_metrics` and `get_performance_score`, the two places those fields turn into a "Low FPS" line and a ~18-point score deduction - had no caller, and `PerformanceLogger.log_metrics` had none either. So HEAD's frame rate was a fabricated number inside unreachable code, which is a different defect from a fabricated number on screen, and 6j-3 still owns the second half: today `get_current_analysis()` is reached only by `export_performance_report`, which no control calls, so the findings, score, advice, historical trends and profiler remain a backend with no pane. The GPU sentence above is now measured rather than asserted: GPUtil 1.4.0 **is** installed here, `nvidia-smi` is not on PATH, and `GPUtil.getGPUs()` returns `[]` in 0.041 s - so `gpu_percent` was `0.0` because no reader answered, every second, on this machine.

**Six causes the first run of the new suite found, every one of them in code this pass wrote.** The run is worth quoting because it is the reason the commit is safe: `python -X utf8 tests/test_performance_backend_contract.py` on the finished tree came back `Ran 109 tests in 206.992s` / **`FAILED (failures=9, errors=4)`**. (1) `src/utils/worker.py:45` handed the wrapped function `self.signals.progress` - **a bound PyQt6 signal object is not callable** - so any pane that reported progress died with `TypeError: native Qt signal is not callable` at the engine's first `progress_callback(37)`. That one line is what put the seven pane rows `test_99`-`test_105` on the report, and it is fixed in the product (`.emit`) rather than in the tests, because six phases of panes had been taught to accept a callback none of them could ever have called. (2) `BenchmarkHistory.record` did `os.makedirs(os.path.dirname(self.path), exist_ok=True)`, and `exist_ok` suppresses only the case where the path *is* a directory: with the folder slot holding a file, `dirname` of `blocked/benchmarks.json` names that file and Windows answers `FileExistsError [WinError 183]` (test_65, test_82). The call now sits inside the `try`, behind `if folder:` (`performance_analyzer.py:636-638`), so the failure becomes the `recorded: False` plus reason the two tests were asking for - and the same fix answers a bare filename, whose `dirname` is `''`. (3) `storage_benchmark` asked `psutil.disk_usage(folder)` before checking the folder was there, so naming a path that is not a directory escaped the engine as `FileNotFoundError [WinError 3]` (test_75); `:873` refuses it with the folder inside the sentence. (4) The geometry kind called `cloud.estimate_normals(search_radius=0.1)` - Open3D 0.19 has no such keyword, so the round never ran and the verdict field came back `None` (test_77); the call is now `KDTreeSearchParamHybrid(radius=0.1, max_nn=30)` passed as `search_param=` (`:964`, `:969`). (5) The shared volume helper needed **two** red runs to reach its bottom: `test_74` first died with `AttributeError: type object 'DiagnosticEngine' has no attribute 'working_volume'`, and after the call was retargeted at the helper that does exist the second run read `Ran 112 tests / FAILED (errors=1)` with `TypeError: list indices must be integers or slices, not str`, because `get_storage_info()` returns a list and `SystemInfo.working_volume` (`diagnostic_engine.py:222`) was indexing it like a dict. That is the case the rule *a suite is not green until the failure it names is closed* exists for. (6) One red was the test's own wrong expectation, not the product's: `test_31` demanded the blocked note appear inside the `reason` string when a frame had been stamped, but that note belongs to the no-stamps branch - `stats()` carries it in its own `blocked` field, and the assertion now checks the shape (`tests/test_performance_backend_contract.py:429-434`) instead of the sentence I had guessed.

- Proof: `python -X utf8 tests/test_performance_backend_contract.py` (1,396 lines, 112 tests, new) -> `Ran 112 tests in 57.457s / OK`, exit 0, after the two red runs above. `python -X utf8 -u tools/mutation_check.py tests/test_performance_backend_contract.py tools/mutations_performance_backend.py` (702 lines, **93** defects, new) -> **87 caught by a named test, 6 went unnoticed, and all six are named here instead of being counted as covered**: [14] and [15] delete the two `psutil` `cpu_percent(interval=None)` priming reads, [19] turns `if self.gpu_reader is None:` into `if False:`, [74] and [75] put the progress bar's range back to `(0, 0)` on the single-run and full-run paths, [79] takes `progress_callback` out of `worker_func`'s signature. Every one of the six reports `0 failures: none`, which means **no test in this suite reaches that line at all** - holes in the suite, not defects in the product, and each has a named test owed to it in the next commit. Siblings that share these files: `tests/test_hardware_report.py` -> `Ran 118 tests in 36.550s / OK`; a 5-entry probe of the retargeted `expect`s in `tools/mutations_hardware.py` -> 4 caught, [59] (*"the hardware test can die with nobody told"*) survived, which is what `test_177c` was written for; `tests/test_preview_render_contract.py` -> `Ran 54 tests in 74.176s / OK` (52 before the two supersample tests that landed with this pass). `tools/check_mutations_absent.py` (89 lines, new) reads every `tools/mutations_*.py` list and asks whether any product file is currently holding a borrowed defective line - `--staged` asks it of the staged blob instead of the file on disk, which is the question to ask in the last second before a commit. On this tree the worktree run found exactly one, the live preview sweep's entry as it moved through `src/processing/preview_render.py` (it reported entry 6, then 16, then 17 in three runs five minutes apart, which is the sweep working, not a stuck file), which is why that file is **not** in this commit; the staged run reported `staged blobs checked: 156, clean`, so no file in the commit is holding a line any list owns. An earlier version of the checker compared the replacement line against the whole file as a substring and flagged four false positives (`pass`, `status = 'healthy'`) that are ordinary lines elsewhere in those files; it now compares whole lines and requires the `find` line to be gone as well, so a green answer means what it should. `run_audit.py` has not been re-run on this tree, because the chain's sweeps own the process budget while they run; it lands with the next commit, and until then the 25-suite score is this area's missing evidence, not a claim.
- Accounting for this area: `src/features/performance_analyzer.py` +1,074/-560 (the 560 deletions are `PerformanceMonitor._update_metrics`'s wall-clock `fps`/`frame_time_ms` arithmetic and its `else 0.016`, `get_average_metrics`/`get_peak_metrics` averaging those fields, `BottleneckDetector.analyze_metrics`/`get_performance_score` - the two names that disappear from the file, replaced by `analyze`/`score`/`not_measured`, and nothing else references them (`grep` over `src`, `tests`, `main`: 0 hits); `OptimizationSuggester`'s "30-50%" and "double or triple FPS" strings; the bodies of `PerformanceLogger.log_metrics`/`log_profile_results`/`get_session_logs` - all three still exist, rewritten to name the file they wrote and to answer a log directory that is not there; the module's two `run_cpu_benchmark`/`run_gpu_benchmark` bodies whose `score` was `int(ops_per_second * 1000)`; the old `__init__`/`start_monitoring`/`stop_monitoring`/`clear_history` and the facade's `start_profiling`/`end_profiling`/`clear_all_data` lines those rewrites touched); `src/ui/diagnostics_tab.py` +729/-210 (deleted: the seeded `sample_reports` and the fourteen-line state dump named above, `take_memory_snapshot`'s price list - HEAD `:1054-1077`: `3D Viewer: 245 MB`, `Model Data: 156 MB`, `Textures: 89 MB`, `Cache: 67 MB`, `UI Framework: 34 MB`, `Other: 28 MB`, `Total: 619 MB`, `Large Objects (>1MB): 23`, `Cached Models: 8`, `Active Buffers: 156`, `Memory Pools: 12`, and the two recommendations offering to free amounts nothing in the app measures - the two `=== SYSTEM INFORMATION ===` blocks that asked psutil twice on the interface thread, the `Rating: {mem_bench['rating']}` and `Overall Performance: {cpu_bench['rating']} CPU, {mem_bench['rating']} Memory` lines and the five references to `diagnostic_engine.PerformanceBenchmark` they came with, the four `Status: ... completed`/`Status: Test failed` pairs that reported a run which had not returned, and the four `datetime.datetime.now()` stamps that dressed a made-up block as a fresh reading); `src/features/diagnostic_engine.py` +347/-48 (deleted: the whole `class PerformanceBenchmark` - its `benchmark_cpu`, which summed `math.sqrt` over a million integers and graded `score = 1000 / elapsed` as Excellent/Good/Average, its `benchmark_memory`, which timed building a million-element list and graded the same way, and their `f"{elapsed:.3f}s"`/`f"{score:.2f}"` rows - plus the single `self.log_dir = Path(log_dir)` line the rewritten log-directory handling replaces); `src/ui/performance_tab.py` +125/-82 (deleted: `run_gpu_benchmark` whole with its `if not self.enable_gpu_cb.isChecked(): pass` no-op and its `# Auto-enable for benchmark if possible?` comment, the `GPU Benchmark` button's three widget lines and its `clicked` connection, the `["Component", "Score", "Performance", "Rank"]` header row, both `def worker_func():` bodies and their nested `def on_result(result):` closures - the `f"{result['score']:,}"` cell, the four-way `Top 1%`/`Top 10%`/`Top 25%`/`Average` rank ternary and the `"Excellent" if score > 10000 else "Good"` verdict - both `self.benchmark_progress.setRange(0, 0) # Indeterminate` lines, `worker.signals.result.connect(on_result)` in both methods, and the `PerformanceMetrics, ProfileResult` names from the import the pane no longer uses); `src/utils/worker.py` +2/-1 (the one deleted line is `self.kwargs['progress_callback'] = self.signals.progress`); `src/ui/model_viewer.py` +8/-0 (the `from ..utils import frame_clock` line and the three stamps: `note_blocked` with the PyOpenGL sentence, `note_frame` after a finished `paintGL` under the view's own object name, `note_failure(e)` in its `except`); `main.py` +17/-5 (the bare `except:` around the non-existent `log_error` and its three neighbours), `run_audit.py` +7/-0 (the new suite registers as the 24th of 25, with its own 180 s budget); `tests/test_hardware_report.py` +747/-0 (32 new tests, `test_150`-`test_180`: the log window and the custom range that refuses by name, the row summary and "no rows is not a zero percent of nothing", the four report kinds and the kind the engine cannot fill, the object walk that asks no object for anything, the process peak as a reading and `test_158`'s four shaped `memory_info` records, the dump naming the interpreter this process really runs and the frozen build saying so, frames printed with no view in reach, the snapshot counting objects and refusing the price list, the report row carrying the file it wrote, the preview showing no crash that never happened, the analysis keeping no invented statistics, the crash count read from the file it lives in, no measurement on the interface thread, `test_177b`/`test_177c` for the hardware button's worker signature and its error channel, a closed pane holding no log handler, `test_179`/`test_180` for the crash record `main.py` now writes and the crash path that swallows nothing); `tools/mutations_hardware.py` +163/-3 (22 new defects on top of 6j-1's 41, so the list is 63; the three deleted lines are entries 2/3/4's `expect` strings, retargeted to `test_15_default`/`test_14_prefix`/`test_11_longest`, and nothing else in the file changed); `tools/mutations_pipeline.py` +1/-1 (entry 4's `expect`, retargeted to `test_29_staging`); `REPAIR_LOG.md` +29/-6 (the six deleted lines are six cross-phase citations re-measured - `point_clouds_tab.py:1534`/`point_cloud_processor.py:653` now marked *at HEAD, before 6h replaced them*, `mesh_tools_tab.py:1876` disambiguated from `:1853`, `postprocessor.py:15` corrected to the measured `:16`, `automation_engine.py:1035-1096` corrected to `:1036-1096`, `tools/probe_backend_safety.py` 137 -> its actual 138 lines, and `MeshUV.parametrize` re-pinned at `mesh_processor.py:1471` - plus this entry's own paragraphs). New files: `src/utils/frame_clock.py` (134 lines), `tests/test_performance_backend_contract.py`, `tools/mutations_performance_backend.py`, `tools/check_mutations_absent.py`. Deliberately **not** staged: `src/processing/preview_render.py` (its only difference from HEAD right now is the sweep's borrowed line) and `tests/test_preview_render_contract.py` + `tools/mutations_preview_render.py`, which are #29's area and land when that sweep reports. The stage captures (`hw6j2*.txt`, `perf6j2*.txt`, `muthw5.txt`, `mutperf.txt`) are deleted once the numbers above were read from them; `chain.txt`, `prev6j2.txt` and `mutprev.txt` are still being written by the background chain and go the moment it reports, because `tools/check_mutations_absent.py` is what proves the borrowed line in `preview_render.py` is back where it belongs.
- **What this area does not prove, stated so a green suite is not read as more than it is.** No real render was run and none is claimed: this machine still has no renderer, `frame_clock` is proven by stamped synthetic frames, and the only view that stamps (`ModelViewer3D`) was never opened with a real GL context here - on this build its `note_blocked` path is the one a user would reach if PyOpenGL were missing, and nothing observed it drawing. Six sweep holes are open and named above, so 87 of 93 is the coverage number, not 93 of 93; the `psutil` priming pair in particular means a first sample can still cover no window without any test noticing. The Diagnostics and Performance panes' numbers come from offscreen Qt drives - nobody has clicked either pane with a real mouse, so the interactive pass is still the user's. `get_current_analysis()` remains a backend no control reaches (only `export_performance_report`, which nothing calls): the findings, score, advice, trends and profiler are 6j-3's, along with the pane's six receiver-less buttons and the `max_threads` knob duplicated in `postprocessing_tab.py:510`. The `gpu` refusal, the `geometry` kind's device name and the storage figures are all this-machine answers: another machine will report different numbers through the same sentences, and the history column is deliberately only this app's own earlier runs.


## Known-deferred

- ~~`tools/` is gone. Its job - the pymeshlab name/parameter/enum truth - is now `tests/_pymeshlab_probe.py` + `tests/test_pymeshlab_calls.py`, which run inside the audit instead of being remembered by hand. Nothing references `tools/` any more.~~ **Corrected in Phase 6c**: `tools/` came back, with one job and nothing else - `tools/mutation_check.py` applies one defective line at a time, runs the named suite, requires a named test to go red and puts the file back byte-for-byte, and `tools/mutations_automation.py` is the 30-defect list for the automation pane. That is the checker the earlier phases re-typed as a throwaway script each time; the pymeshlab truth still lives in `tests/`, where the audit reaches it.
- **One string enum is still invisible to the checker**: `_transfer(attribute, ...)` in `texture_baker.py` receives `attributeenum` as a parameter, so it parses as computed. It is covered at runtime instead - `test_02` proves `'Vertex Color'` rasterises colour, `test_04` proves `'Vertex Normal'` rasterises normals - and splitting it into two literal call sites was rejected because the only thing gained would be a second copy of the same eleven lines. Say so rather than let the green checker imply full coverage.
- `MeshAnalyzer.is_manifold` / `mesh_tools_tab.update_mesh_info` still report `is_winding_consistent` under the label "Manifold". Kept open because a correct test needs a real edge-manifold check, which belongs with the quality-scoring work, not silently patched here.
- Running the audit dirties the working tree: tests write into tracked files (`config/batch_templates.json` records a `created_at` timestamp; `tests/test_data/repaired.obj` and `simplified.obj` are regenerated outputs, not inputs - nothing reads them). They were restored with `git restore` before the Phase 2 commit, so every commit here is clean, but the suites need to write to a temp directory. Belongs with the dead-file/phase-8 hygiene pass, not a behaviour fix.
- ~~`format_converter.py` calls `os.makedirs(os.path.dirname(output_file))` at lines 262, 290 and 313, which raises for a bare filename (`dirname` is `''`). Latent - every UI path passes a directory - and not reached by any test yet. Fix with the format-claims phase.~~ **Fixed in Phase 5a** - one `_prepare_output_dir()` (`format_converter.py:81`) with the `or '.'` the empty dirname needs, and `test_16` converts to a bare `result.stl` from a changed cwd so it stays reached.
- `ModelViewer3D` defines `set_render_mode` twice (`:1605` and `:1860`) and `set_use_vertex_colors` twice (`:1865`/`:1889`) in the same class. Last definition wins, and both pairs do the same thing, so the Rendering tab's view modes reach the body they expect - but a reader cannot tell which one a control reaches without scrolling to the end. Left for the dead-code pass rather than edited blind.
- The Point Clouds hand-off is **one-way** (found in 6h, not fixed there): `add_to_library_requested` reaches the Library (`main_window.py:533`) and a generated surface reaches Mesh Tools, but nothing opens a model *in* the Point Clouds pane - `MainWindow.open_in_point_clouds` exists and is connected to no control, and the Library's context menu has no such entry. Belongs with the dead-module/stale-wiring pass (#10) so the wiring and the deletion land together.
- Cloud verbs never overwrite, so a chained run grows its suffix every click (`scan_downsampled_filtered_uniform_centered_normalized_deduped_normals.ply`). That is the no-overwrite rule working, not a leak, and `output_dir` is the tidy answer for a task - but the pane has no "start a new chain from this file" control, so a long chain in one folder is what the user gets today. Name it as UX debt, not a bug.
- `run_audit.py` gives every suite a flat 120 s. `verify_all_fixes.py` needs 40.7 s idle and reported `[ERROR] (120.19s)` on a run where other work shared the machine - the same tree, the same code. Read a timeout as a budget first and re-run the suite by hand before chasing a defect; a per-suite timeout for the known-slow suites belongs with the temp-directory hygiene item above.
- **`tests/verify_all_fixes.py test_09` fails on the cleanup of its own temp directory, on this machine, sometimes** (found in 6k, re-measured in 6j-2b, still open). The test writes `test.obj` in a `tempfile.TemporaryDirectory()` and builds a `MainWindow()` on it (`tests/verify_all_fixes.py:155`); when the `with` block exits, `tempfile.py:893 onerror` gets `PermissionError: [WinError 32] ... test.obj`, and the suite reports `FAILED (errors=1)`. **6k's sentence - "only inside the audit chain" - is now corrected: it is not chain-only.** Measured on one tree in one hour: the full suite `FAILED (errors=1)` in 63.726 s, then `OK` in 38.144 s; `test_09` alone `OK` three times out of three; the `test_08` + `test_09` pair `FAILED` once and `OK` once; and the chain itself `26/26` with this suite at 44.20 s. What the app was proven *not* to do, measured the same pass: a probe ran each of the six routing calls against its own fresh `.obj`, pumped the event loop, waited two seconds and called `os.remove` **while the `MainWindow` was still alive** - every one deleted, including all five routes over one shared file, and `psutil.Process.open_files()` listed no path in the temp tree at any step. So no enumerable handle in this process is held. Two candidates survive: a **memory-mapped view** (which `open_files()` does not list and which does block a delete on Windows), or **another process** - Defender or the search indexer holding a file it watched being written, which is what "fails when the machine is busy, passes when it is idle" describes. This is not closed and must not be papered over: if the mapped-view half is right, RenderFox genuinely locks a file Blender or Unity is trying to re-export over, and that is a release defect, not a test defect. What is owed: (1) the same per-step delete probe extended to the paths it could not reach - the viewer's queued load, which offscreen never runs (`DEBUG: Viewer not valid, queuing model` is printed instead of a paint), and Post-Processing over a real non-empty PNG; (2) then either release the holder in the product or make the suite close the window and retry the removal with a bounded delay, saying which of the two the measurement supports. It belongs with the "suites need to write to a temp directory" hygiene item above, not with any behaviour fix.

**Corrected by 6l, which is the pass this bullet asked for.** Both owed items are done, and neither candidate turned out to be the mechanism. (1) The delete probe was extended to everything it named and more: `tests/test_all_tabs_headless.py test_15` drives all six `open_in_*` routes against their own copies with the window still open, and `test_16` asks the same question of ten readers - trimesh twice, Open3D's mesh and cloud, pymeshlab, `MeshProcessor.load_mesh`, the thumbnail, the raster, the viewer's texture read and the Rendering pane's material slot - each with the object it returned kept alive until **after** the delete, so garbage collection cannot be credited with a release the code never made. All ten release. **That retires the memory-mapped-view half for the paths that could be enumerated**, and no mapped view survives a `close()` plus a delete in the same process. (2) Both halves of the suite's own answer shipped: the product now releases what it was genuinely locking (`_read_texture_file` closes the handle even when the read raises and the exception is retained - `test_19`), and `verify_all_fixes test_09` no longer leaves the cleanup to `tempfile`'s single attempt on interpreter terms; it closes the window itself and retries the tree with bounded patience, then names any file that survives with its `winerror` (`Ran 9 tests in 34.909s / OK`). **What the measurement did not reach, so this bullet stays open rather than being deleted:** the *other* candidate - another process, Defender or the indexer, mid-read of a file the suite just wrote - cannot be disproved from inside this process; it now shows as a named, retried, eventually-deleted file instead of an error attached to a cleanup, which is the difference between a release defect and an idle-machine artifact. The viewer's **queued** load is still unreached (offscreen prints `DEBUG: Viewer not valid, queuing model` instead of painting), so that one path's release is still unproven and still owed. And the third mechanism 6l found was never a lock at all - it was `0xc0000374`, a run still in flight when the window went; see the 6l entry.

- ~~**The Open3D window 6m deleted from the tagger is still in the Library's thumbnail thread** (found while writing 6m; opened as #44, not touched there). `src/core/thumbnail_generator.py` `_generate_thumbnail` constructs `o3d.visualization.Visualizer()` at `:247`, calls `poll_events()` at `:304` and `capture_screen_float_buffer(do_render=True)` at `:311`, and it is reached from the interface: `library_grid.py:738` calls `generate_thumbnail(file_path)` for each uncached file and `_process_queue` (`:409-416`) starts a `ThumbnailGenerationThread` per file. The thread's `cancel()` sets `self._cancelled` at `:148`, which a thread blocked inside a native call never reads, so this is the same guaranteed hang 6m measured at 392 s in another pane - with the app's own software raster already one call away.~~ **Closed by Phase 6p, and the sentence "it is reached from the interface" was wrong.** `LibraryGridWidget` is constructed by nothing in `src/`, `tests/` or `tools/`, so the thread never ran, no uncached Library thumbnail ever hung, and the retarget this bullet asked for would have kept a second cache, a second thread-per-file and a second set of signals for a widget no pane shows. Both files are deleted (1,307 lines), which is 2 of #10's 7 names executed. What *was* live was the other copy of the same window, in `version_control._generate_thumbnail`, on the interface thread, one press of Commit away from freezing the whole app - that one now uses the raster, and its picture is finally shown by the pane that records it. `tests/test_thumbnail_owner_contract.py` guards both halves.
- ~~**The Versions pane cannot commit the two file shapes a DCC actually hands it** (found while writing 6p; opened as #49, not touched there). `save_version` (`src/features/version_control.py:116`) reads its metrics off whatever `trimesh.load` returns - `len(mesh.faces)` at the top of its own `try` - and trimesh answers per file, not per wish. A multi-material `.obj`, which is what an export writes and what `MeshRepairer.auto_repair` itself produces, loads as a **Scene**: `Error saving version: 'Scene' object has no attribute 'faces'`, `None` back, zero rows. A faceless `.ply` loads as a **PointCloud**, same raise, same `None`, and `version_control_ui.py:684` then tells the user "No changes detected. Version not saved." - a statement about a comparison that never ran. Both leave the snapshot copy on disk with no row pointing at it. The worse half is the reporting: `tests/test_full_flow.py:93` prints `✅ Committed version: None` and `✅ History entries: 0` and then "🎉 Full Integration Test Passed!", because it asserts neither - so the app's own integration run has been green over a commit that never happened, which is #8's fabricated-result class sitting in a suite rather than in a pane. The normalising pattern the fix should reuse is already written, at `thumbnail_renderer.py:98-102`.~~ **Closed by Phase 6q, and the fix reused a different copy than this bullet named.** All three shapes commit: the multi-material `.obj` is recorded as 12 faces over 20 vertices (the Scene's three groups, summed), the faceless one as three vertices with the face count and the watertight claim left absent, and an empty or garbled file is refused **before** the copy with its reason named - so no orphan snapshot and no picture for a row that does not exist. `test_full_flow.py` now checks the claim it used to print and exits nonzero: `✅ Committed version 1: 12 faces, 11 vertices, 1 row`. Two corrections to the sentence above. First, the pattern copied is not `thumbnail_renderer.py:98-102` but `format_converter.as_trimesh`, which was already the app's shared normaliser and is simply no longer private (`_as_trimesh` -> `as_trimesh`); taking the renderer's copy instead would have made a sixth. Second, the two copies that the earlier phases' "no second implementation" rule still owes are untouched: `library_manager.py:525-539`, `model_tab.py:41-54`, `model_tab_open3d.py:33-46` and `thumbnail_renderer.py:95-100` each still join a Scene themselves, which is #13's business and is named there rather than half-done here. What 6q could not do is make the lazy import free: `import src.processing.format_converter` costs 1.544 s on this machine (pymeshlab), against 0.007 s for this module's own imports, so the call sits inside `_normalise` and the **first commit in a process** pays that second and a half while startup does not.
- **`tests/test_full_flow.py` reports its own code defect as an expected skip** (found while writing 6q, in a step that phase did not own). Its tagging step is `tagger = AIAssetTagger()` then `if tagger.model is None and tagger.processor is None:` (`tests/test_full_flow.py:64-65`) inside a `try`, and `AIAssetTagger.__init__` (`src/features/ai_asset_tagger.py:57`) assigns **neither name** - a grep for `self.model` and `self.processor` across that whole file returns nothing. So the guard can never run: the `if` raises `AttributeError: 'AIAssetTagger' object has no attribute 'model'`, the bare `except` at `:70` catches it, and `:71` prints `⚠️ Tagging skipped/failed (expected if no model)` - a sentence about this machine having no CLIP model, printed over a line that has never worked on any machine. The run stays green because the step asserts nothing about either branch. What is owed: ask the tagger the question it can answer (`self.use_clip`, which is `use_clip and CLIP_AVAILABLE` at `:70`), and let an `AttributeError` fail the step instead of dressing it up as news. Not touched in 6q: the tagger is `test_ai_tagger_contract.py`'s area, and a two-line change here would move the flow run's verdict with no test behind it.
- **`tests/verify_all_fixes.py` fast-fails at interpreter shutdown, so the chain reads it red on a run whose nine tests all passed** (measured while writing 6q, in a suite 6q does not own; opened as #50). `run_audit.py:261` judges a suite by `proc.returncode == 0`, and this process prints `Ran 9 tests in 20.080s / OK` to stderr and then dies with `0xc0000409` - the code Windows gives a `__fastfail`, which 6o measured as what happens when the last reference to a **running** `QThread` goes - so a green suite arrives as `Core Backend & Regression Suite [FAIL]` and costs the chain 4 percentage points. Rate measured on both trees with the same driver, six subprocess runs each: **3 of 6 at HEAD `470f3c1`, 1 of 6 on the 6q tree**, so it predates this pass. What #50 owes before "the app shuts down cleanly" is committed as fact is the instrument 6o used: run the suite under `faulthandler` and an `atexit` hook until the abort names the object whose destructor causes it - one of `MainWindow()`'s panes, a thread still alive at shutdown, or a native teardown no Python-level close-walk can reach - and then make the code 0 for that reason rather than retrying the chain. #41 closed the `PermissionError: [WinError 32]` half of this suite's intermittency; this is the other half, in a different shape.
