The first is the reference-drop class this log measured at `model_tab.py`, the second is 6l's queued-verdict class arrived at by a route the helper has no way to ask about; what #45 owes before either sentence is committed as fact is a probe that measures the plain-Python drop, and a holder that answers the close for the downloader dialog.~~ **Closed by Phase 6o**, and the probe changed one of the two sentences: the plain-Python drop was measured, and what it measured is that dropping the last reference of a **running** `QThread` ends the process at the drop (`0xc0000409`), while dropping a finished one survives - and the shape that actually did it here was `ModelDownloaderDialog()`'s own default `auto_start=True`, which starts a download inside `__init__` so that the next statement is the drop. Both holders now answer the close: the dialog through `shutdown_runs` + `done()` and a stop that lands on a step boundary (`model_downloader.py:26-33`, `:55`, `:76`, `:101`, `:205-217`), parented to and held by the Settings pane (`settings_tab.py:716`); the pane's plain workers through `_spawn_action_run` / `shutdown_runs` (`automation_tab.py:1613-1641`) and the engine's own `run_task` (`automation_engine.py:1730-1748`), with `wait_for_runs` learning the difference between `join` and `wait` (`run_teardown.py:115-117`). The scheduled-task route also stops being a thread beside a list, which is what `live_work` reports. What the phase leaves open is stated in its own paragraph: the download stops between steps and not inside one, `silence(self)` is permanent for a pane whose close a test refused, and no entry plants the *absence* of the pre-claim a worker re-makes too fast to see.


### Phase 6k - the Quality pane's Fix buttons reach the engine, the report that never existed gets written, and a safety pass I had already described is measured away

**What was false before, and where.** Every one of this pane's five `Fix` names was a `QMessageBox` in front of an `if/elif` that knew four of them: `'repair_mesh'`, `'merge_vertices'`, `'remove_degenerate_faces'`, `'remesh'` were dispatched (`quality_analyzer_ui.py`, HEAD `:528-548`) and **anything else returned `f"Fix '{action}' not supported automatically yet."`** - so `fix_normals`, `generate_uvs` and `pack_uvs`, whose backends exist in this very file's imports, were refused as unsupported; `generate_lods` and `resize_texture` were refused the same way even though the Pre-Processing pane does both. The analyzer wrote `one_click: fix_type in ['repair_mesh', 'merge_vertices', 'generate_lods', 'remove_degenerate_faces', 'fix_normals']` (`quality_analyzer.py` HEAD `:488`), a second list of the same names, and it kept `generate_lods` and `fix_normals` while the pane's `if/elif` kept neither - the row the user saw and the code that would answer it were two lists that had never been compared. `normalize_uvs` had a description (HEAD `:554`) and no emitter, no verb and no test. Fix All ran `processor.repair(path, output_path, operation='auto')` and then marked **every** row `"(Applied)"` (HEAD `:605`, comment `# Mark all as applied (visual only)`), so closing a hole dressed a UV suggestion as done, and its confirmation sentence named three filters for an engine that runs seven. The output path came from `path.replace('.', '_fixed_all.')` (HEAD `:594`), which rewrites **every** dot: a model under `C:\Users\a.b\mesh.obj` became `C:\Users\a_b_fixed_all_obj`. `QualityAnalysisWorker` declared `finished = pyqtSignal(dict)` - a subclass signal shadowing `QThread.finished`, the one Qt emits - and a second `error = pyqtSignal(str)` that nothing ever emitted, while `SafeWorker.run` emits `error_occurred`; the pane connected both of the dead ones (HEAD `:366-367`), so a model that could not be opened left the bar spinning and the Analyze button disabled forever, and `analyze_quality`'s own `{'error': ..., 'score': 0, 'grade': 'F'}` was painted as a grade of F for a file the app had never read. `ComparisonDialog.update_info(label, stats1['metrics'])` (HEAD `:91`, `:96`) indexed the key that is absent from exactly that error dict - KeyError inside a dialog constructor. And `export_report` called `self.analyzer.export_quality_report(self.analysis_result, str(output_path), self.current_model_path)` (HEAD `:642-646`) against `export_quality_report(results, output_path)` - a TypeError raised in a slot, swallowed by the `except` that only logged. **The Quality report had never been written on any machine since the button was added.** Repaired, the call reached the template and raised a *second* time: the block ended in `""".format(len(results))` (HEAD `:634`) and every CSS rule in it is a brace, so it died on `KeyError: ' font-family'` before writing a line - the two causes are different bugs and the old log named only the first. `set_model` (HEAD `:315`) had `if self.original_model_path is None:` sitting **above** its own docstring, so the guard was the docstring's first line and the docstring was dead code.

**`apply_fix_action` (`quality_analyzer_ui.py:57`) is the pane's only dispatch, and the vocabulary is the analyzer's.** `QualityAnalyzer.ONE_CLICK_FIXES` (`quality_analyzer.py:77`, seven names), `OWNED_ELSEWHERE` (`:81`) and `FIX_ALL_COVERS` (`:91`) are now the lists the pane builds `FIX_VERBS` (`:32`) against; `test_01` walks the analyzer's own `'fix': '...'` literals and fails on any name neither table accounts for, `test_03` proves `one_click`/`other_pane` come from the shared tuples rather than a second literal list, and `test_07` (`:254`) asserts `set(FIX_VERBS) == set(ONE_CLICK_FIXES)` outright, so a promise cannot outlive a verb in either direction. A routed name returns `{'success': False, 'error': <the sentence naming the pane that owns it>}` and writes nothing; an unknown name returns `f"no backend is registered for fix '{fix_action}'"`; `fix_normals` reaches `MeshProcessor.repair(operation='fix_normals')` -> `MeshRepairer.fix_normals` (`mesh_processor.py:790`), which is trimesh-only and was never broken by a filter-name sweep because no filter name is in it. `fix_output_path` (`:43`) is `os.path.splitext`, which is why `test_13` puts the model in a folder named `models.v2`. Both Fix paths go through the house `Worker`, so both `worker_func`s take the `progress_callback` the Worker always injects (`test_24`) and both connect `signals.error` (`test_25`). `FIX_ALL_ASK` (`:51`) names all seven filters and `test_14` reads `inspect.getsource(MeshRepairer.auto_repair)` for the `apply_filter('...')` literals to keep it true, which is the only reason the sentence is a fact rather than a hope. The two reload prompts now have an answer each: `test_31` and `test_37` accept the dialog and require `current_model_path` **and** the next `analysis_result['model_path']` to be the file the fix wrote.

**The claim this log owed a correction, and the measurement that withdrew it.** Earlier in this same pass I described a `MeshSafety` class - a door that re-exported any `.obj`/`.ply` whose listed `v` count exceeded what trimesh read, wired into six verbs, with three new fields in every verdict. Its premise was that PyMeshLab 2025.7 builds face indices past the vertex array from a file that lists a position twice and then either saves those indices or raises `0xc0000005`. **Re-measured before committing, that premise does not hold on this tree**: `MeshSafety.prepare` and a no-op stub, run alternately over the pane's verbs on the doubled file and on the cube, returned the same scores and no error in every cell - `repair_mesh` 80/85, `merge_vertices` 65/80, `fix_normals` 60/80, `remove_degenerate_faces` 70/85, `remesh` 60/75, with the last two re-run three times per mode to look for the nondeterminism I had reported and not find it; the doubled file's five runs through the seven-filter chain each saved a file whose indices stay inside its vertex array, and `pymeshlab.MeshSet().load_new_mesh` reads **all ten** listed positions with `face_matrix().max() == 7`. The access violation I saw during probing came from the probe's own bad filter names, not from the engine. So the door is deleted, its 111 lines and six wirings are back at HEAD, and what replaces it is `Test31RepeatedVertexLines` - four tests that pin the engine's real answer (positions listed == positions read == max index bound; the weld reports the two positions it joined and writes a file with no repeated row; a textured file keeps its UVs through three verbs). If a future pymeshlab starts to do what this one does not, those tests go red first.

**What the re-run did find, in the app's own reader.** With the door gone, the strengthened suite hit `AttributeError: 'Scene' object has no attribute 'visual'` - and the reason is a live defect: `MeshUV.uv_rows` and `MeshUV.strip` asked `trimesh.load(path, process=False)` for `mesh.visual` directly, and a `.obj` with more than one `usemtl` group - which is what PyMeshLab's material sidecar produces - comes back from that call as a **Scene**. `uv_rows` caught the AttributeError in its `except Exception: return None`, so every multi-material file was reported as having *no* texture coordinates, and `parametrize`'s `uv_rows_written` verdict field - the number that decides whether the UV fix says "success" - was measured on the wrapper rather than the mesh. `MeshUV.read_unprocessed` (`mesh_processor.py:1434`) is now the one reader both call (`:1450`, `:1467`): it joins the scene's meshes and refuses a scene with no triangle mesh by name. `test_35` pins the premise (`assertIsInstance(trimesh.load(path, process=False), trimesh.Scene)`) so the test cannot rot into a check of a format nobody writes.

**The hole the sweep found in my own checker list.** The first run of `tools/mutations_quality_analyzer.py` (38 defects) came back `1 SURVIVED, 1 CRASHED`. [11] - *"a folder whose name contains a dot is cut in half"* - survived because its `replace` line used `rsplit('.', 1)`, which cuts at the last dot in the whole path and so **is** correct behaviour; the entry described a defect it did not create. Retargeted to `model_path.split('.')`, it is caught by `test_13`. [31] - *"a bare string among the issue rows"* - CRASHED, because expressing it as a single line inside the analyzer's multi-line dict literal produced a syntax error, and a suite that dies at import stands in for a named failure without proving anything; per this file's own rule that defect is unclosed. The claim was therefore moved to the place it can be tested: `on_analysis_complete` now paints a row that is not a dict as `Issue: <the sentence>` instead of raising inside a Qt slot where the exception prints to a console nobody watches and the rest of the list silently disappears (`quality_analyzer_ui.py:474-477`), `test_38` drives it directly, and entry [31] mutates that one guard line. Both were re-run singly, then the whole list: **38 of 38 caught by a named test, no crashes, no survivors.**

- Proof: `python -X utf8 tests/test_quality_analyzer_contract.py` (891 lines, **37** tests, new) -> `Ran 37 tests in 2.144s / OK`, exit 0, after the red runs above. `python -X utf8 -u tools/mutation_check.py tests/test_quality_analyzer_contract.py tools/mutations_quality_analyzer.py` (304 lines, 38 defects, new) -> `all 38 defects were caught by a named test`, exit 0, with `--list` first reporting `38 anchors are unique`. Siblings that share `mesh_processor.py`, run one at a time on the finished tree: `tests/test_pipeline_contract.py` -> `Ran 55 tests in 7.133s / OK`; `tests/test_hardware_report.py` -> `Ran 118 tests in 54.391s / OK`; `tests/verify_all_fixes.py` -> `Ran 9 tests in 77.459s / OK`; `tests/test_full_flow.py` -> `Full Integration Test Passed`; `tests/test_e2e_workflow.py` -> `Ran 1 test / OK`; `tests/e2e_backend_test.py` -> `All backend workflows passed successfully`. `grep -rn "MeshSafety\|input_vertex_lines\|input_vertices_merged"` over `src/`, `tests/`, `tools/`, `main.py` -> no source hits (only a stale `__pycache__`), so the deleted door leaves no dangling reference. The 6k entry in `run_audit.py` (the 25-suite chain) carries the description of what the suite now proves. `python -X utf8 run_audit.py` has now been run on this tree, and its score is **`Overall Health Score: 96% (25/26 suites passed in 549.97s)`, exit 1** - the sibling line above is a standalone run, and the one red in the chain is not this area's file. `Core Backend & Regression Suite` (`tests/verify_all_fixes.py`) is the failure: `Ran 9 tests in 74.652s / FAILED (errors=1)`, and the error is not an assertion but the cleanup of a `with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory()` block, `PermissionError: [WinError 32] The process cannot access the file because it is being used by another process: '...\tmp5s57bn00\test.obj'` raised in `tempfile.py:893 onerror`. `test_09_main_window_tabs_and_routing` (`tests/verify_all_fixes.py:155`) writes `test.obj` into a temp directory, builds a `MainWindow()` on it, and Windows refuses to delete the tree while some handle in the app still holds the file. The same suite run alone on the same tree, immediately after, is `Ran 9 tests in 50.693s / OK`, exit 0 - so this is intermittent and load-dependent (it needs another process holding the machine while the suite runs), it predates 6k, and no line in this commit touches `verify_all_fixes.py`, `main_window.py` or the temp-directory handling. It is named in Known-deferred as a defect to close, not counted as covered by the 96%.
- Accounting for this area: `src/ui/quality_analyzer_ui.py` +149/-70 (the 70 deletions, line by line: the `MeshProcessor`-only import the router replaces; `finished = pyqtSignal(dict)` and `error = pyqtSignal(str)` and the `self.finished.emit(result)` that went with them - `error_occurred`, which `SafeWorker.run` really emits, is now the connected channel and no signal was dropped, only the two that could never fire; the worker's unused `self.processor` and `self.threadpool`; the comparison dialog's four lines (`stats1`/`stats2` and their two `update_info(...['metrics'])` calls) and its `def update_info(self, label, metrics)` signature; the `if self.original_model_path is None:` pair that sat above the docstring; the two `self.worker.finished/error.connect` lines; the issue loop's `issue.get('type', 'Issue')` and `issue.get('message', '')` and its three `issue.get('severity')` tests, all six now reading `fields`; `suggestion.get('fix_action', 'Fix')`, which turned a `None` into the string `'None'`; the single-fix `worker_func(processor, path, action)` whole - its inline `os.path.splitext`, its `result = {'success': False, 'error': 'Not implemented'}` seed, its four-branch `if/elif` and its `f"Fix '{action}' not supported automatically yet."` escape, which is the sentence this area exists to delete; the `# Map action to processor method` / `# Supported: ...` comment pair; Fix All's `"""Apply all fix suggestions."""` docstring, its two `# For now...` / `# Running multiple fixes sequentially is complex` comments, its three-filter `QMessageBox.question` text, its `def worker_func(processor, path)` and the `path.replace('.', '_fixed_all.')` line inside it, its `# Mark all as applied (visual only)` comment and the `item.setText(f"{suggestion['title']} (Applied)")` it excused; `export_report`'s `"""Export quality report to HTML."""`, its three-argument call lines and its trailing `logger.info(f"Quality report exported to: ...")`, which claimed a file the call never wrote); `src/features/quality_analyzer.py` +46/-13 (the 13 are: the bare `'issues': ['Empty scene (no geometry found)']` and `'suggestions': ['Check model export settings']` rows, replaced by dict rows the pane can read; the two-line `one_click: fix_type in [...]` literal, replaced by membership in `ONE_CLICK_FIXES`; the `normalize_uvs` description, whose name no emitter, no verb and no test had; `html = """`, the `🎯` heading line, the `{}` count line and `""".format(len(results))` - the four that made the report impossible; the `model_name` line that ran after the `continue` it needed, so the error branch had no name to print; and the `✅ Yes / ❌ No` and `✅ No issues detected! Model is production-ready.` lines, both rewritten as plain words under the no-emoji rule); `src/processing/mesh_processor.py` +18/-2 (the two deletions are `uv_rows`' and `strip`'s `mesh = trimesh.load(mesh_path, process=False)`, each replaced by the one reader; nothing else in the file changed, and the 111-line `MeshSafety` class and its six wirings that this session also wrote are **reverted to HEAD, not deleted from it** - they were never committed, so `git diff` shows neither); `run_audit.py` +7/-0 (the suite registered as the 25th, its description rewritten to name what it now proves, including the second export cause and the scene-shaped reader); new files `tests/test_quality_analyzer_contract.py` (891 lines, 37 tests) and `tools/mutations_quality_analyzer.py` (304 lines, 38 defects). The capture files this pass read its numbers from (`q6k.txt`, `mutq.txt`, `sib6k.txt`, `probe_tdup.py`, `probe_writecheck.py`) are deleted.
- **What this area does not prove, stated so a green suite is not read as more than it is.** No render was run and none is claimed: the pane's 3D surfaces (`unified_viewer`, `ComparisonDialog`'s two viewers) were opened offscreen and their *labels* were checked, nothing observed a frame. The three-verb UV round trip in `test_34` proves coordinates survive and the analyzer re-reads the file; it does not prove an atlas a texture painter would like - the packing quality of `Space-optimizing` over a real UV shell was never inspected, and `pack_uvs`/`generate_uvs` still report `knobs_not_applied` when the flat-plane method ignores `texture_size` and `border_pixels`. `test_08`'s 14 cells and `test_34`'s 3 are this machine's pymeshlab 2025.7 and trimesh answers; the repeated-position premise withdrawn above is a statement about *this* build, and `Test31RepeatedVertexLines` is the tripwire for another one, not evidence that no build has the hazard. The pane still has no progress percentage: `apply_fix_action` verbs report no fraction of work, so the bar stays indeterminate - said here rather than dressed up. `resize_texture` and `generate_lods` remain routed to the Pre-Processing pane by sentence, which is the honest refusal this rule allows; the cross-pane jump (open that tab with the file loaded) is not built. The exporter still writes one HTML file with no per-model comparison and no filter, and the report's grade colours are the same six buckets the pane paints, not a scoring model anyone has audited.


### Phase 6i-3 - the raster gets the mutation list its two earlier phases owed it, and nine defects stop hiding behind a dead process

**What this pass owed, in the log's own words.** Phase 6i-1a closed with "Not covered here. No mutation list yet - `tools/mutations_preview_render.py` belongs with the engine's own sweep in 6i-1c, so the source-contract assertions are currently the only guard on the four Open3D traps", and 6i-1b repeated it: "The raster still has no mutation list of its own - three of the 19 entries touch the engine's image call sites, but `tools/mutations_preview_render.py` and the four Open3D traps stay guarded only by `test_preview_render_contract`'s source-contract assertions". Phase 6j-2 then wrote the supersample code and left its proof out of that commit on purpose - "Deliberately **not** staged: ... `tests/test_preview_render_contract.py` + `tools/mutations_preview_render.py`, which are #29's area and land when that sweep reports". This entry is that landing: the list, the sweep, and the nine defects the sweep found.

**The list is 52 one-line defects, and the first run of it split into three kinds, not two.** `python -X utf8 -u tools/mutation_check.py tests/test_preview_render_contract.py tools/mutations_preview_render.py` on the tree as it stood came back **`4 defect(s) were detected only by killing the suite` and `5 of 52 defects went unnoticed`** - so 43 of 52 were caught by a named test, and the report's own two categories are what this pass had to close. `tools/mutation_check.py` has always separated them, and this is the first area where the separation was the finding: **a defect that kills the process is detected without being closed**, because a dead process names no test and a reader who counts named failures sees a clean run.

**The four crashes were the module's own honest behaviour, tested through the wrong door.** A mesh painted with the point-cloud channel, an extrinsic that puts the eye behind the model, a fitted eye sitting inside the geometry, and a camera standing on the point it looks at all reach those lines through `render_file`, which opens a real Open3D window - and the window takes the process down before unittest can print anything. The fix is not a softer defect: it is the same line reached where a report is possible. `_extrinsic`, `_fit_distance`, `_display_colors` and `_paint` are pure, so `SeamCase` calls them directly; `_place` needs a view control, so the class hands it a stand-in that does one thing - keeps whatever the product writes into `parameters.intrinsic.intrinsic_matrix` and `parameters.extrinsic` - and the assertions are about those numbers. No stand-in decides the answer: reverting any of the four lines now produces a `FAIL:` with a test name under it.

**The five that went unnoticed were five different kinds of weak test, and two of them named a test that cannot see behaviour at all.** [15] "the height map ramps along x" survived because `test_a_height_map_is_a_ramp` only counted `distinct_colors > 100`, and a ramp along any axis produces that; the new pin builds points that vary along exactly one axis and asks each of the four coordinate modes which axis it read, then adds the case the count could not make - a column varying only in x must print **flat** under `height`. [19] "the principal point is off to one side" survived because the fitted-frame test asks only whether the model is *in* the picture, and `width / 4` keeps it there; the new pin reads `intrinsic[0, 2]` and `intrinsic[1, 2]` back. [45] "a one-pixel-wide model reports a silhouette of zero pixels wide" survived because `test_the_silhouette_is_measured_from_the_pixels` compares a tall box's width against its height, and a difference of one pixel never shows in that comparison; `WrittenFileCase` now hands the pipeline a frame that is background with one lit column and asserts the file measures 1 wide, 200 tall, 200 covered. [39] and [21] named `SourceContractCase` tests, which grep the source and **cannot fail when code that matches the grep behaves differently** - that is why a source pin is a supplement and never a closer; [39] now has a `_write` that produces a file of nothing but the background colour, so the refusal branch is reached on purpose instead of by luck, and [21] is answered by the extrinsic invariant (the eye maps to its own origin, the target to positive depth).

- Proof: `python -X utf8 -u tools/mutation_check.py tests/test_preview_render_contract.py tools/mutations_preview_render.py` (407 lines, **52** defects, new) -> **`all 52 defects were caught by a named test`**, exit 0, with `--list` reporting `52 anchors are unique` before and after the nine `expect` retargets, and the product file verified identical to HEAD afterwards (`git diff --stat src/processing/preview_render.py` empty - the runner restores byte-for-byte, and its own digest check reported no restore failure across all 52). The nine closures were measured first in isolation - `--only 5,15,19,21,22,25,27,39,45` -> `all 9 defects were caught by a named test` - and then inside the full run above. `python -X utf8 tests/test_preview_render_contract.py` (734 lines, **63** tests, 52 at HEAD) -> `Ran 63 tests in 56.053s / OK`, exit 0, re-run on the restored tree after the sweep. The sibling that reaches the raster: `python -X utf8 tests/test_pipeline_contract.py` -> `Ran 55 tests in 4.420s / OK`, exit 0. The captures this entry read its numbers from (`mutprev.txt`, `mutprev2.txt`, `mutprev_full.txt`, `prev29a.txt`, `prev29b.txt`, `pipe29.txt`) are deleted, so the sweep is re-runnable rather than remembered.
- Accounting for this area: **no product line changed** - `src/processing/preview_render.py` is identical to HEAD, which is the point of the phase: the raster's behaviour was already measured in 6i-1a and 6j-2, and what was missing was proof that the suite would notice it breaking. `tests/test_preview_render_contract.py` +209/-0 (nothing deleted; 24 of those lines are the two supersample tests 6j-2 wrote and deliberately left unstaged - `test_supersample_draws_one_frame_at_the_multiple_and_reports_it` and `test_a_supersample_that_will_not_fit_is_cut_and_says_so`, the proof the four supersample defects [49]-[52] now point at - and the other 185 are this pass's 9 tests, their two classes and the module docstring paragraph naming the four rules a window cannot report). `tools/mutations_preview_render.py` (407 lines, 52 defects, new) - nine `expect` strings retargeted, and its docstring sentence about "the four supersample entries name the claim the suite has not yet made" deleted and replaced, because that claim is now a test; it was true when 6i-1a wrote it and stopped being true when 6j-2 drew the multiple. `run_audit.py` +1/-1 (the one deleted line is the preview entry's description, rewritten to name the sweep, the four window-killing defects and the two file-level verdict rules; the suite's 180 s budget is unchanged and 63 tests take 56 s here). `REPAIR_LOG.md` - this entry, plus 6i-1a's and 6i-1b's two "Not covered here" sentences left exactly as they are: they are the record of what was owed, not a claim to correct.
- **What this area does not prove, stated so a 52-of-52 sweep is not read as more than it is.** `SeamCase` pins what the product computes and writes into the camera parameters; it does not pin what Open3D 0.19's window then does with them - that half is still `FramingCase`, `CameraCase`, `LightCase` and the real frames, and both halves are needed. The stand-in exists because this machine's window segfaults on a nan camera, which is a fact about this build's Open3D/GLFW, not a property the module can rely on elsewhere. `WrittenFileCase` forces two verdict branches by replacing `_write` and `_capture`; the branches are real, but a blank JPEG from a real encoder and a genuinely one-pixel-wide model were never produced, so the frame-level versions of those two rules stay unproven here - and so does any claim that supersampling looks better: the multiple is one frame drawn at N times the edge and folded by Lanczos, which the suite proves was drawn and reported, not that a viewer prefers it. No DCC ran, no final image was made, and nobody has clicked the Pipeline pane's Render node with a real mouse - every drive in this file is offscreen or direct-to-module.


### Phase 6j-2b - the six holes the performance sweep named get tests, all four of the pane's threads get reached, and the alias verb is deleted where a suite can see it

**What was owed, in the previous phase's own words.** 6j-2 ended `87 caught by a named test, 6 went unnoticed` and wrote *"[14] and [15] delete the two `psutil` `cpu_percent(interval=None)` priming reads, [19] turns `if self.gpu_reader is None:` into `if False:`, [74] and [75] put the progress bar's range back to `(0, 0)` on the single-run and full-run paths, [79] takes `progress_callback` out of `worker_func`'s signature. Every one of the six reports `0 failures: none`, which means **no test in this suite reaches that line at all** - holes in the suite, not defects in the product, and each has a named test owed to it in the next commit."* The same phase owed one deletion in words rather than in code: *"`run_cpu_benchmark` (`:1005`) is an alias that nothing in `src/` or `tests/` calls - the pane reaches `engine.run('cpu')` - so it goes in the sweep-hole commit, where a suite can prove the deletion rather than me asserting it."* Both halves land here: the six entries have tests, and the alias is gone with `test_91b` as the tripwire.

**The six were six different kinds of weak test, and saying which kind is the point.**

- **[14] and [15] - the prime produced a number that was in range either way.** `psutil.cpu_percent(interval=None)` answers `0.0` on the first call in a process and the share of the time *since its previous call* after that, which is why `start_monitoring` calls it twice before the timer starts (`performance_analyzer.py:158-159`). With real psutil on an idle machine, deleting those two lines still yields a float between 0 and 100, so an assertion on the figure cannot see the prime at all. `Test10bCpuPriming` hands the module a psutil that behaves exactly as its documentation says - silence first, a figure afterwards - through `ANALYZER.psutil`, and `test_10b`/`test_10c` require **two reads** of each reader and the number the second read makes possible (`42.0` for the machine, `17.0` for this process). The prime is now proved by the reading it protects, not by grepping the call.
- **[19] - the failure sentence still matched the assertion.** `if False:` in place of `if self.gpu_reader is None:` makes the monitor *call* a reader it was never given, and `_read_gpu`'s own `except` (`:183-184`) turns that into `the GPU reader raised TypeError: ...`. `test_05` asserted only that the reason contained `GPU reader`, so it read the absence of a reader as a reader that failed - which is exactly the confusion the field exists to avoid. The test now requires `not given a GPU reader` in both `gpu_load_percent` and `gpu_memory_percent` and **forbids** `raised`.
- **[74] and [75] - the bar was inspected after the run, when the evidence had been overwritten.** `QProgressBar` discards `setValue` while `minimum == maximum`, and `finish_benchmark` (`performance_tab.py:999`) re-stamps `setRange(0, 100)` before the old assertion looked. So a mid-run discard leaves the bar looking perfectly correct at the end. `spy_progress_bar` now records the pair `(value, bar.maximum())` as each call arrives, and `test_101` and `test_105` require every percentage the engine reported to have landed in a bar whose maximum was already 100.
- **[79] - the sweep mutated a line on a path the suite never drove.** `def worker_func(progress_callback=None):` occurs **four** times in the pane (`:812` Detect, `:847` Storage, `:940` one-kind benchmark, `:1013` four-kind benchmark) and the entry carried `which: 1`, the Detect button - which had no test at all. The product was right; the suite had never pressed two of the pane's four buttons. `test_106b` drives `detect_gpu_hardware()` and requires the placeholder to leave `gpu_info_display`, the report to arrive, the button to be locked during its own run and released after; `test_106c` drives `refresh_storage_info()` and requires the volume rows to fill without the failure wording. The list now carries four entries on that one anchor, `which` 1 through 4, each expected by the test that reaches it: 106b, 106c, `test_101`, `test_105`.

**The alias verb is deleted where a suite can see it.** `BenchmarkRunner.run_cpu_benchmark` (`performance_analyzer.py:1005` at HEAD) returned `self.cpu_benchmark(duration=duration)` - a second door to the cpu work with no `record` flag, no `progress_callback` and no caller in `src/`, `tests/` or `main.py`. `test_91b_the_alias_verb_that_no_pane_could_call_is_gone` asserts the `def` is gone from the engine, that the pane never reaches `benchmark.run_cpu`, that both `engine.run(` and `engine.run_all(` are how it times work, and that `run_gpu_benchmark` **stays** - it is not a second door to a kind, it is the answer to the label the Geometry button used to carry, and `test_79` reads its refusal rather than trusting this sentence.

- Proof: `python -X utf8 tests/test_performance_backend_contract.py` (1,537 lines, **117** tests: 112 + `test_10b`, `test_10c`, `test_91b`, `test_106b`, `test_106c`) -> `Ran 117 tests in 33.043s / OK`, exit 0. `python -X utf8 -u tools/mutation_check.py tests/test_performance_backend_contract.py tools/mutations_performance_backend.py --only 79,80,81,82` -> `all 4 defects were caught by a named test`, each by exactly the test named above, and the same file's `--list` reports `96 anchors are unique`. The whole list on the finished tree: `python -X utf8 -u tools/mutation_check.py tests/test_performance_backend_contract.py tools/mutations_performance_backend.py` (726 lines, 96 defects) -> **`all 96 defects were caught by a named test`**, exit 0 - 93 of 6j-2's 93 plus the three new `which` entries, so the coverage number this area ships is 96/96 and not 87/93. Siblings that share these files, run one at a time: `tests/test_hardware_report.py` -> `Ran 118 tests in 37.745s / OK`; `tests/verify_all_fixes.py` -> **`Ran 9 tests in 63.726s / FAILED (errors=1)`** on the first run and `Ran 9 tests in 38.144s / OK` on the second, the same tree both times, and `test_09` alone -> `OK` three times out of three. `tools/check_mutations_absent.py` after the sweep -> `clean: every find line is in place in every file the lists touch`, which matters here because the first sweep run was cut short by the shell at entry 6 and left `answer['frame_time_ms'] = round(1000.0 * max(gaps), 2)` in `src/utils/frame_clock.py` - the checker named the file, the entry and both lines, and that file is byte-identical to HEAD again before this commit. `python -X utf8 run_audit.py` -> **`Overall Health Score: 100% (26/26 suites passed in 448.19s)`**, exit 0 - the first fully green chain this file has recorded (6k's was `96% (25/26 in 549.97s)`), with the Performance suite `PASS (32.76s)`. The 26/26 is not the whole story and is not written as if it were: the `Core Backend & Regression Suite` that passed here at `44.20s` is the same suite that came back `FAILED (errors=1)` on an earlier run of this identical tree and `OK` on a later one, so the chain being green today is one more sample of an intermittent cleanup, not a fix - see the Known-deferred entry, which this pass rewrote because its premise changed.
- Accounting for this area: `src/features/performance_analyzer.py` +2/-5 (the five deletions are the two comment lines that called the aliases "the verbs the panes call", `def run_cpu_benchmark(self, duration: float = 2.0) -> Dict[str, Any]:` and its `return self.cpu_benchmark(duration=duration)`, and the blank line between it and `run_gpu_benchmark`; the two new lines are the comment that says why `run_gpu_benchmark` is the one verb that is not a kind); `tests/test_performance_backend_contract.py` +146/-5 (the five deletions are `spy_progress_bar`'s four docstring lines - the old text said the bar is only worth watching as the calls arrive but recorded no range beside them - and its bare `painted = []`, which becomes a `Painted(list)` carrying a `ranges` list; nothing else was removed, and no assertion got weaker: `test_05` gained two checks and lost none, `test_101`/`test_105` each gained one `assertEqual` over the recorded ranges); `tools/mutations_performance_backend.py` +28/-4 (the four deletions are [14] and [15]'s `'expect': 'test_02'` strings, retargeted to `test_10b_...`/`test_10c_...`, and [79]'s `'expect': 'test_101'` plus its note `the house Worker always passes progress_callback, and this one cannot take it`, which described the benchmark button while the entry mutated the Detect button); `run_audit.py` +1/-1 (the Performance suite's description, rewritten to name what it now proves - the two priming reads, the reader-absence sentence, the range observed at the moment each percentage lands, all four worker threads driven, and no second name for the cpu work). The captures this pass read its numbers from (`perf40a.txt`, `perf40b.txt`, `perf40c.txt`, `perf40sweep.txt`, `sib_perf.txt`, `sib_hw.txt`, `sib_vaf.txt`, `vaf_rerun.txt`, `vaf_89.txt`, `chain40.txt`, `probe_h.txt`, `probe_handle.py`) are deleted.
- **What this area does not prove, stated so a green sweep is not read as more than it is.** `Test10bCpuPriming` tests a **model** of psutil, not psutil: the fake is built from the documented first-call contract, re-measured on this machine before the class was written (psutil 6.0.0 answers `0.0` to the first call of **both** readers - `psutil.cpu_percent(interval=None)` and `Process().cpu_percent(interval=None)` - and on an idle machine its later call is `0.0` too, so this machine cannot tell a primed zero from an unprimed one, which is the reason the fake exists rather than a second assertion), and if a future psutil answered the first call with a real window the fake would keep passing while the product no longer needed the prime. Two of the pane's four threads are now driven only on their **success** path: `test_106b`/`test_106c` prove the worker takes the callback and the answer arrives, but disconnecting `worker.signals.error.connect(self.on_machine_reader_failed)` (`performance_tab.py:818`, `:853`) is still not pinned by a failing test, because neither test makes its reader raise - that pair belongs with #32, whose scope is exactly the pane's unwired failure channels. No real render was run and no frame was observed: the frame clock remains proven by stamped synthetic frames and `ModelViewer3D` is still the only view that stamps. The 42.0 / 17.0 / `GB` / `Machine reader failed` figures are this machine's answers through offscreen Qt, and nobody has clicked the Performance pane with a real mouse. `verify_all_fixes` is green and red on the same tree depending on what else is running - see the Known-deferred entry below, which this phase's measurements changed.


### Phase 6l - a file this app read is free again, and a close over a run in flight is a stop, not a heap fault

**What was owed, in this file's own words.** The Known-deferred entry that 6j-2b rewrote closed with: *"What is owed: (1) the same per-step delete probe extended to the paths it could not reach - the viewer's queued load, which offscreen never runs (`DEBUG: Viewer not valid, queuing model` is printed instead of a paint), and Post-Processing over a real non-empty PNG; (2) then either release the holder in the product or make the suite close the window and retry the removal with a bounded delay, saying which of the two the measurement supports."* That entry listed two candidates - a memory-mapped view, or another process - and said *"if the mapped-view half is right, RenderFox genuinely locks a file Blender or Unity is trying to re-export over, and that is a release defect, not a test defect."* This area answers both halves, and the answer is neither candidate: **three mechanisms, none of them the one the log predicted.**

**Mechanism one, and the only one that was ever a real in-app lock: a texture read that fails keeps the file open while the failure is on screen.** `Image.open` does not read the pixels, so a file Pillow can identify but not finish raises *after* the handle exists - a truncated PNG is that case - and the caller stored the exception. A retained exception keeps its traceback frame, that frame keeps the local `img`, and `img` keeps the OS handle: `os.remove` answers `PermissionError [WinError 32]` for exactly as long as the pane displays the error. Measured with the exception held in a dict (`kept['error'] = problem`) - the lock reproduces; measured with the fixed reader - it does not. `ModelViewer3D._read_texture_file` (`model_viewer.py:1825-1836`) is now the app's one texture reader: `img = Image.open(...)`, then `return np.array(img.convert('RGBA'), dtype=np.uint8)` inside a `try` whose `finally` is `img.close()`. **The shape is `try`/`finally` rather than `with` on purpose**: the `with` version's release could only be broken by deleting a block opener, which is a syntax error, which crashes the suite at import - and this file's own rule is that a defect caught only by a crashing suite is unclosed. `set_material_texture` (`:1838`) also reads **before** its GL gate now, so a wrong path is reported whether or not a context is up; only the upload needs one (`:1853` still calls `_load_gl_texture`, which takes the RGBA uint8 array it always took - the returned shape is `(32, 64, 4)`, measured).

**Mechanism two: the texture control was implemented twice, and the copy the panes reach was unreachable code.** `ModelViewerWidget.set_material_texture` (HEAD `:2469-2510`) was a 42-line byte-identical duplicate of the viewer's GL body, opening as `if not self.isValid() or not self.gl_ready:` - and `QWidget` has no `isValid()`, so every call died on its own first line with an `AttributeError` that the body's trailing `except Exception as e: print(f"Error setting material texture: {e}")` buried in a console nobody watches. Its private uploader (`:2565-2597`, 33 lines) existed only to serve that body. Both are gone: the control is now the same three lines as its eight siblings (`:2488-2491`), and the uploader went with its only caller rather than staying as a second GL implementation no test could reach. One honest caveat the delegation inherits: `ModelViewerWidget.__init__` (`:2425-2428`) puts a `SimpleModelViewer` in `viewer_3d` when PyOpenGL is missing, and that class defines no `set_material_texture` - so with the `hasattr` gate the control does nothing on a machine without GL, which is what the other eight controls on that class already do, and `SimpleModelViewer._load_gl_texture` (`:67`) is its own dead copy for the dead-code pass (#10), not something this area reached.

**Mechanism three, which is the one that ended the process: a worker still running when the window goes.** `verify_all_fixes test_09` started dying with exit 127 and `Windows fatal exception: code 0xc0000374` - a heap fault, not a traceback - inside the Point Clouds pane's verdict handler. The route is `open_in_point_clouds` -> `_run('analyze', ...)` -> `CloudRunThread.run` -> `verdict_ready.emit(...)`: the emit is queued, the window is torn down, and the queued call is delivered into widgets Qt has destroyed. `PointCloudsTab.shutdown_runs` (`point_clouds_tab.py:221`) is the answer - take `verdict_ready` and `finished` off **first**, then `request_stop()` (`:113`), then `wait(timeout_ms)`; a thread that ended is dropped from `_workers`, and one that refused is **counted and kept**, because dropping the last Python reference of a running `QThread` is the other way this process ends. `MainWindow.closeEvent` (`main_window.py:458-466`) now asks every pane that carries the verb, after the batch and the scheduler are stopped, so the contract is stated app-wide even while only one pane answers it today. `_release_worker` (`:211`) exists because of a crash measured during the sweep, not a prediction: `shutdown_runs` removes the same thread from `_workers` on its way out, so the old `thread.finished.connect(lambda: self._workers.remove(thread))` raised `ValueError` inside a slot, and PyQt6 answers an exception escaping a slot with `STATUS_FAIL_FAST_EXCEPTION (0xc0000409)`.

**The suite, because none of the three is a claim without a measurement.** `tests/test_all_tabs_headless.py` is 21 tests (15 -> 21), and six of them are this area. `delete_with_patience` (`:254`) is the instrument: eight quarter-second attempts, `processEvents()` between them, and the file name plus `winerror` printed while the lock is still being measured - two seconds of patience for the antivirus that reads every file this machine writes, and nowhere near enough to hide a mesh the app itself kept open. `test_15` (`:279`) hands each of the six `open_in_*` routes its own copy of a real `.obj`, drains the loop and deletes **with the window still up**; `test_16` (`:310`) asks the same question of ten readers directly - trimesh with and without `process`, Open3D's mesh and cloud reads, pymeshlab's `load_new_mesh`, `MeshProcessor.load_mesh`, the thumbnail painter, the preview raster, the viewer's texture read, and the Rendering pane's own material slot through `on_material_updated` - and every result goes into `alive` before the delete and is dropped after it, so refcounting cannot be credited with a release the code never made. **That is the disproof of the mapped-view half, for those ten paths.** `test_17` (`:386`) records the pair the wrapper forwards, drives the live `model_tab.model_viewer` too, and keeps `viewer_3d = object()` as the fallback safety; `test_18` (`:429`) puts `RGB`, `RGBA`, `L`, `LA` and `P` through the reader and demands `(6, 8, 4)` uint8 with the palette's pixels cross-checked, because a grayscale texture uploaded as one channel is a black material; `test_19` (`:464`) is mechanism one - the truncated PNG, the retained exception, and a delete that has to succeed; `test_20` (`:497`) drives the **real** close over a run provably in flight and requires four things back: the verb was asked to stop, nothing is still running, `_active_thread` is None, and the pane's own sentence is still what the label says; `test_21` (`:564`) gives the pane a verb that ignores the stop and requires `shutdown_runs` to answer `1` and keep the thread. Both test backends delegate to the real processor (`SlowBackend`, `DeafBackend`), because a stub verdict of a shape this test invented is the only thing a handler that *should* have been disconnected could trip over - and the first sweep proved that by crashing on it.

**Two holes the injection pass found in the teardown suite itself.** [03] (`thread.request_stop()` -> `pass`) **SURVIVED** for one reason: `test_20`'s own `finally` set `thread._stop_requested = True` before waiting, so the test answered the question it was asking. It now waits with `thread.wait(15000)` on the workers exactly as they are, and the backend's deadline is 3 s, so the wait that ends the run is the product's, not the test's. [02] (disconnecting nothing) reported **CRASHED - the suite died without naming a test**, and chasing it found two separate causes: the unguarded `list.remove` above, and the stub verdicts. Both fixed, it is caught by `test_20` as an assertion - `'Measuring the cloud...' != 'Stopped: stopped because the window went away'`.

**A premise I was about to write down wrong, caught by measuring the fixture.** `test_21`'s plan said the `.ply` was refused by Open3D and the asset had to change to `clean_manifold`. Run against the real reader on this machine: `o3d.io.read_point_cloud` gives the trimesh-exported `07_noisy_cloud.ply` **1650 points**, and refuses `01_clean_manifold.obj` with `Read geometry::PointCloud failed: unknown file extension` and zero points. So the PLY stays and the delegated backend's answer is an accepted verdict; the planned change would have swapped in a refusal for no reason.

### Phase 6m - the AI tagger's picture is drawn by the raster this app already has, and the two claims sitting on it are measured

**What was found, and how.** This did not start as a tagger task. #42's holder table gave `tests/test_all_tabs_headless.py:test_22` a row that starts the tagger's real auto-tag run and asks the pane to close over it; the row timed out. First at 25 s (the run answered in 107 s), then at 180 s with a poll to zero, then still owed at **392 s** with `-X faulthandler` on, whose stack put the worker inside `open3d.visualization.Visualizer.poll_events` at HEAD `src/features/ai_asset_tagger.py:232`. The same call in a fresh process took 1.5 s. So the run was not slow, it was never going to end: a second GL window in one process does not answer, and a thread blocked inside a native call answers no cancellation verb - which is exactly the class of holder #42 exists to stop, and the one holder the close cannot reach because there is nothing to wait for.

**The claim I was about to write down wrong, and measured instead.** The deleted code tried `trimesh`'s `scene.save_image` first and fell back to Open3D. Earlier drafts here - in this file's own text and in two docstrings - said the first attempt "fails in this build because pyglet is not installed". That is false, and `pyglet` is installed. Measured on this machine in a fresh process: `save_image(resolution=(224, 224))` on the **main thread** answers in **0.58 s** with a real PNG, and on a **worker thread** answers in **0.06 s** with `RuntimeError: EventLoop.run() must be called from the same thread that imports pyglet.app`. `analyze_model` runs on the tagger's worker, so the fallback branch was not a rare path, it was the **only** path ever taken - the hang was guaranteed, not occasional. The half that works is its own reason to leave: the main-thread picture is **2 colours over 50176 pixels**, a flat unlit silhouette, and CLIP's category is a judgement about that picture.

**The fix is a deletion, not a new renderer.** `_render_model_preview` (`src/features/ai_asset_tagger.py:146`) now takes the model path, writes to a `mkstemp` file in the app's own temp directory and asks `generate_3d_thumbnail` - the QPainter raster the Library and the Automation already use, and the one the Pipeline's Render node was moved to in 6i-3 - for the frame; it then re-opens the file (`:176`) and hands back a copied `RGB` image, with the temp file removed in a `finally`. Two deletions come with it: the module-level Open3D `try: import` block and `_render_with_open3d`, the 50-line fallback. The app has one picture maker again, and `test_04` reads the syntax tree so a second one cannot come back as an import or as a branch.

**`ai_powered` became a measured claim.** It used to be set from `self.use_clip` - whether a model is installed. Now the analysis dict opens with `'ai_powered': False` (`:390`) and `analyze_model` sets it True only inside `if preview_image:` (`:403`), next to the `ai_category` and `ai_descriptive_tags` a frame actually produced. A refused draw therefore reports a fallback rather than a judgement: `rendered: False` returns None at `:177` even though the raster left a file at that path, and the geometry answers alone.

### Phase 6n - every holder of a run answers the close, the mechanism lives in one file, and the four defects that only ever showed as a dead process get a suite that cannot die

**What was found, and how.** 6l closed with the sentence that is this phase's brief: the teardown contract was *"stated app-wide and implemented in one pane"*, and it named the four holders that answered nothing - `preprocessing_tab.py:333`, `performance_tab.py:152`, `diagnostics_tab.py:145-146`, `pipeline_editor_tab.py:866`. Writing the same eleven lines into each of them is how this file came to describe five copies of one mechanism, and a sixth copy is exactly what 6l's sweep could only detect by crashing the suite. So the mechanism moved into one file before any pane was touched: `src/utils/run_teardown.py` (172 lines) performs four steps in the only order that is safe - **silence** every channel Qt says that runner can emit, read off the runner's own meta-object rather than a list typed beside the pane, because a hand-written list silently forgets the signal somebody adds later and the forgotten one is the one that arrives after the window is gone; **ask** it to stop through whichever of the app's four verb spellings it happens to have; **wait**, one thread at a time but a pool as a pool, because `QThreadPool.waitForDone` answers "did the queue drain" and nothing more specific, so pool work handed over with no pool comes back as still running instead of as a lie; then **release** only what ended, because the reference of a run still working is the only thing between this process and Qt's abort. Fifteen `shutdown_runs` methods in fourteen files now call that helper; not one of them restates it.

**The close asks the tree, not the fifteen tabs.** `closeEvent` (`src/ui/main_window.py:468`) used to loop over `self.tab_widget.count()`, and that loop cannot see a run that does not live on a tab page. Four do: the Quality pane inside Models, the Open3D preview inside Mesh Tools and Point Clouds, the tagger inside Library, and a `TextureGeneratorDialog` that outlives its own `exec()` as a child of the Rendering tab. The walk is now `[self] + self.findChildren(QWidget)` with an `hasattr` gate, which is the one list of holders that cannot forget a pane because the pane declares itself. `test_25` replaces the walk with a spy on every holder **instance** and asserts the set asked equals the set that exists, then closes a second time from a hidden window and asserts nothing was asked again - the second half is there because Qt answers a close of an already-hidden window by doing nothing at all, and a contract that only works once is not a contract. It reports **14 kinds across 16 instances**, the instance count being over the class count deliberately: the test builds two extra dialogs, one parented to a tab page and one to the window, because a walk scoped to the tab pages still asks one dialog and misses the other, and a comparison of class names cannot see the difference.

**Three shapes of holder, and the test that tells them apart.** A list of `QThread`s (`point_clouds_tab`, `preprocessing_tab`, `pipeline_editor_tab`), a list of `QRunnable`s on a pool (`mesh_tools_tab`, `performance_tab`, `diagnostics_tab`, `quality_analyzer_ui`), and one attribute per run because that is what the pane's own Cancel button reads (`library_tab.scanner_worker`, `ai_asset_tagger_ui.tag_worker`/`bulk_worker`, `texture_generator.thread`, `postprocessing_tab.worker`, `scripting_tab.executor`, `unified_viewer.preview_worker`, `model_tab`'s two holders). The Quality pane is the one holder that carries both pool work and a thread, so its `shutdown_runs` runs `drain` over the fix list and `drain_attributes` over the analysis thread and adds the two answers; `model_tab.py` is the file with two holder **classes** in it - the tab and the format-converter dialog, which is why the count is fifteen methods in fourteen files - and the tab's own method keeps a list of threads *and* the `analysis_worker` attribute, clearing the attribute only for a run the wait saw end. `test_26` is the test that refuses to let a pane answer the wrong question: pool work with no pool named is owed, not finished. `test_22` then drives five real backends through their own pane entry point with the backend delayed - Performance storage read 3.4 s, Diagnostics object walk 2.5 s, AI tagger auto-tag 2.3 s, Mesh Tools clustering simplify 2.0 s, Library scan 2.1 s - and requires three facts of each: the run in flight is *counted* as owed rather than reported as stopped, it is still referenced afterwards, and `silence()` comes back empty because nothing may still be wired to a widget the close destroys.

**Two of the sixteen defects were the crash itself, not the contract.** `preprocessing_tab.py` carried `thread.finished.connect(lambda: self._workers.remove(thread))`, which is the shape 6l predicted and this phase measured: `shutdown_runs` releases the same worker on its way out, the second unguarded `list.remove` raises inside a slot, and PyQt answers an exception escaping a slot with `0xc0000409` - a fail-fast, not a traceback. It is now the guarded `_release_worker`, the same helper the other three list-holders use. `model_tab.py` overwrote `self.analysis_worker` with a new `ModelAnalysisWorker` on both call sites (`:2530`, `:2549`); the previous thread could still be running, and dropping the last reference of a running thread is the abort, so both sites go through `_keep_worker` now. And the seventeen lines of Point Clouds' hand-written disconnect loop are gone, replaced by `running = drain(self._workers, timeout_ms)` plus `return len(running)` - the pane no longer names its own signals, which is what made its list wrong the moment `verdict_ready` stopped being the only channel that carries a verdict.

**Four of the sixteen defects cannot be caught by any suite that runs the app, so they got a suite that does not.** A mutation that files a `Worker` in nobody's list leaves the Python wrapper collected while a pool thread is still inside `run()` - `0xc0000005` - and a mutation that skips the silencing leaves a verdict queued into destroyed widgets - `0xc0000374`. Neither is an exception anything can catch, and `unittest` prints the names of what went wrong only in its end-of-run summary, so a process that dies in the fifth test reports *nothing* and `tools/mutation_check.py` can only answer CRASHED. That is the same hole 6i-3 named for the raster, and the same answer: `tests/test_run_teardown_contract.py` (167 lines, 2 tests) asks those questions in a file that never starts a run - a `Worker` that is handed to the pane's own keep-helper and never to the pool, then released by the pane's own `shutdown_runs` against a pool that is provably empty (`drain_global_pool` refuses to measure against a busy one, because `waitForDone` answers for every run queued, including another pane's). The four entries that used to report CRASHED are now read by `test_01` and `test_02`, and each carries its own `suite` key so the checker runs it against the file that can survive it.

**The last defect survived for a reason worth writing down.** [16], "one of the two buckets is left out of the stop", came back **SURVIVED with 0 failures** against the new suite. Diagnostics' close ends with `bucket[:] = [worker for worker in bucket if worker in running]` for *both* lists, so cutting `_measure_workers` out of the sum handed to `drain` still leaves that bucket empty - which was the only fact `test_02` asserted. What the mutant actually skips is the silencing half: the run stays connected to the report view it writes into. The test now asks three facts of every bucket, filed / emptied / **and nothing left connected**, which is the helper's own order, and [16] fails on an assertion whose message names the re-filter. A defect that survives because the test asks a weaker question than the mechanism does is a test defect, and this one was found by reading the mutant's actual behaviour rather than by re-running the sweep until it agreed.

**A docstring that would have shipped a wrong number, and one that shipped a control that does not exist.** `postprocessing_tab.py:1151` said the helper "knows the app's three spellings" of a cancellation verb; `_STOP_VERBS` is four (`request_stop`, `request_cancel`, `cancel`, `stop`), and `test_24` is the test that reads the source and fails if a fifth appears. `model_tab.py:346` said the batch-analysis wait is "what #15's Cancel control is for" - a task number inside product code, pointing at a Stop control the Models pane does not have (its only Cancel is the converter dialog's button box at `:228`). It now states the measured fact: those workers declare no cancellation, so the close can only wait, up to the timeout. Neither edit changes a line of behaviour, and no mutation entry is affected because every `find` line in the lists is code. `tests/test_postprocessor_paths.py` re-run after them: 6 tests, 0.130 s, OK.

- Proof: `python -X utf8 tests/test_run_teardown_contract.py` -> `Ran 2 tests in 1.865s / OK`, exit 0. `python -X utf8 tests/test_all_tabs_headless.py` -> `Ran 27 tests in 32.545s / OK` on the tree committed here (34.172 s on the same tree before the two docstring corrections below), exit 0, with `14 kinds across 16 instances` and the five backend timings above. `python -X utf8 tools/mutation_check.py tests/test_all_tabs_headless.py tools/mutations_run_teardown.py` (158 lines, 16 defects, four carrying their own `suite`) -> **`all 16 defects were caught by a named test`**, no `SURVIVED` and no `CRASHED`; every file byte-identical to before each entry (`tools/check_mutations_absent.py` and `git status`). `python -X utf8 tools/check_unreachable_code.py` -> `No function body continues past a statement that ends it.` **The full chain on this tree: `python run_audit.py` -> 28/28 suites in 563.87 s, 100%**, which is the first chain since 6j-2b and includes the two suites this phase and 6m added (`ai_tagger_contract` 16.95 s, `run_teardown_contract` 5.28 s) and the headless file at 54.45 s inside the chain.
- Accounting for this area: new `src/utils/run_teardown.py` (172 lines), `tests/test_run_teardown_contract.py` (167), `tools/check_unreachable_code.py` (63). `src/ui/main_window.py` +10/-6 - the six are the four lines of the tab loop (`for index in range(self.tab_widget.count()):`, `pane = self.tab_widget.widget(index)`, `if hasattr(pane, 'shutdown_runs'):`, `pane.shutdown_runs()`) and the two comment lines above it, all four now living in the walk and the comment rewritten to say what the walk covers; no object that used to be asked is asked less. `src/ui/point_clouds_tab.py` +7/-17 - the seventeen are the three docstring lines that described the hand-written loop, `still_running = 0`, the `for thread in list(self._workers)` line, the five-line `for signal in (thread.verdict_ready, thread.finished)` / `try:` / `disconnect()` / `except TypeError:` / `pass` block, `thread.request_stop()`, `if thread.wait(timeout_ms):`, the guarded remove's two lines, `else:` / `still_running += 1` and `return still_running`;
