the 44 substantive additions are the helper pair and the pool-shaped `shutdown_runs`. `src/ui/diagnostics_tab.py` +44/-2 and `src/ui/performance_tab.py` +41/-2 - in each file the two deletions are the bare `self._…_workers.append(worker)` lines at the start sites, each replaced by the keep-helper call that appends *and* wires the release; the Diagnostics helper takes its bucket as an argument, which is what the [16] test now holds it to. `src/ui/quality_analyzer_ui.py` +43/-0, `src/ui/ai_asset_tagger_ui.py` +16/-0, `src/ui/pipeline_editor_tab.py` +16/-0, `src/ui/texture_generator.py` +16/-0, `src/ui/library_tab.py` +14/-0, `src/ui/postprocessing_tab.py` +14/-0 (plus one word of that file's docstring corrected in the paragraph above), `src/ui/scripting_tab.py` +14/-0, `src/ui/unified_viewer.py` +14/-0 - nine files of pure additions, no line removed from any of them. `tests/test_all_tabs_headless.py` +336/-0: eight tests (`test_20`-`test_27`) added, no existing test changed or weakened. `tools/mutations_run_teardown.py` +123/-25 - the five deleted header lines described a six-entry list aimed at one pane, and the twenty are four entries' worth of `file`/`find`/`replace`/`expect`/`note` lines that pointed at Point Clouds' hand-written loop and its `return still_running`; every one of those four now points at the helper step it breaks, because the pane-side line they defected no longer exists in the tree - an entry whose `find` line has been deleted cannot fail, which is the same hole 6i-3 named. `run_audit.py` +14/-0 (two suite entries; the `ai_tagger_contract` one belongs to 6m). No control was deleted anywhere in this area, and no pane's Cancel button lost a verb.
- **What this area does not prove.** **Two runs are still outside the reach of the walk, opened as #45 with the lines that say so.** `model_downloader.py:159` starts a `ModelDownloaderThread` held only by `self.thread` of a dialog that `settings_tab.py:711` constructs **parentless** and as a function-local, so the wrapper is collected when `exec()` returns while a multi-gigabyte download is still running - the reference-drop class measured at `model_tab.py`, inherited rather than re-measured here, which is what #45's probe owes. `automation_tab.py:1609` and `:2281` start `threading.Thread(daemon=True)` held by nobody, and both report across a thread boundary into this pane's widgets (`:1617` emits the pane's own `auto_actions_finished`, connected at `:60`; the engine emits `task_started`/`task_completed` to slots connected at `:48-54`), so the delivery is queued into the widgets the close destroys and no join exists anywhere. Neither is a `QThread` or a pool `Worker`, so the helper has nothing to say to them and there is no mutation entry for them yet. Second, the engine-side threads are stopped by their own verbs and not by this contract: `closeEvent` calls `automation_engine.stop()` before it asks the holders, `job_manager.stop_processing()` joins its queue with a 5 s patience at `:196`, and `render_engines.py:304-306` joins its own stdout/stderr readers inside the render call - none of which this pass measured under a real renderer. Third, `src/ui/model_tab_open3d.py:293` and `:1490` start two more `QThread`s in a file that no `src/` or `tests/` module imports (the only mention anywhere is line 146 of this log), and `system_utils.py:68` `SystemMonitor.start_monitoring` spawns a daemon thread in a class whose only in-app look-alike is `performance_tab.py:41 SystemMonitorWidget`, a different class - both are dead code, which is #10's job, not a holder to add. Fourth, this phase corrects two notes that 6m carried forward without measuring them: `library_manager.py:22` `worker.start()` is docstring example text and not a call site - the real `LibraryScannerWorker` is made at `library_tab.py:1038`/`:1043` and is one of the holders now - and the 6m line "the tagger row now takes 2.4 s" is superseded by this phase's measurement of the same row, **2.3 s**. Fifth, `test_22` drives five of the fifteen holders' real backends; the other ten are proven by their own phase's suite and by `test_20`/`test_21`/`test_25`/`test_27` at the close, which is a weaker claim than "every holder was seen with a run in flight" and is not being made. Nothing here was clicked with a real mouse and no GPU render was run or claimed.

### Phase 6o - the two holders the walk cannot see answer for themselves from inside, a plain thread is waited on the way a plain thread is waited on, and three defects stop hiding behind a dead process

**What 6n left owed, and the probe that pays for it.** #45 was opened with two sentences: `model_downloader.py` started its `QThread` in a dialog that `settings_tab.py` built **parentless** and as a function-local, and `automation_tab.py` started two `threading.Thread(daemon=True)` runs that nothing held and nothing joined. 6n also recorded what that owed before either sentence could be committed as fact - a probe of the plain-Python drop. Measured, in a subprocess, three ways: dropping the last reference of a **running** `QThread` ends the process at the drop itself, exit `0xc0000409` (`3221226505`, which Git Bash reports as `127` and the terminal reports as nothing but `...`); dropping a **finished** one survives; and assigning onto a bare `QDialog` - the same statement, with no thread inside - survives, so the abort is the thread and not the widget. The shape that made it a bug rather than an experiment is the constructor: `ModelDownloaderDialog(auto_start=True)` starts a real download **inside `__init__`**, so the next statement after `dialog = ModelDownloaderDialog()` is the moment a running thread loses its last reference. Every test here therefore passes `auto_start=False` and calls `start_download()` itself, and the dialog's own `thread` attribute is what holds the run.

**A plain thread is not a `QThread`, and the helper now knows the difference.** `wait_for_runs` (`src/utils/run_teardown.py:115-117`) gained an `isinstance(runner, threading.Thread)` branch - `join(timeout_ms / 1000)`, then classify on `is_alive()`, because `join` takes seconds where `wait` takes milliseconds, and a plain thread answers neither `disconnect` nor any of the four stop spellings, so the wait is the entire answer it can be given. The Automation pane is the holder that hands it one: `_spawn_action_run` (`src/ui/automation_tab.py:1613-1623`) prunes the workers that already ended, files the new one in `self._action_threads` (declared at `:69`) and starts it, and `shutdown_runs` (`:1625-1641`) drains that list and then calls `silence(self)` - in that order, and on **the pane**, because an Auto Actions worker's verdict travels on the pane's own `auto_actions_finished`, not on a channel of the thread's, which is the half of the job a `QObject`-less runner cannot be handed over for. Mutation [22] is that second call, and it fails on the pane's receiver count.

**The engine claims a run before its thread has had a chance to run.** `AutomationEngine.run_task` (`src/features/automation_engine.py:1730-1748`) replaces the raw start `run_task_now` used to do beside the list, and routes through `_spawn`, which is the one place `_worker_threads` is filled - `stop()` (`:2045`) joins exactly that list and `live_work()` (`:2027`) reports exactly it, so a task begun from the interface is now work a close can name, wait for, and refuse to start twice. The `if task.enabled:` on `:1745` is not decoration: `execute_task` (`:1698`) refuses a disabled task at `:1703`, **before it claims any status**, so a claim made for a task that never ran would leave it looking live to `live_work` and in the row, and the next close would offer the user the chance to cut off a run that had done nothing at all. `test_20` of the tasks suite now asserts `live_work() == []` after that refused press, and entry [25] inverts the guard (`if task.enabled:` -> `if True:`) so the regression the guard exists to stop is the one that is planted.

**The dialog answers for its own download, on the way out.** A network call is not interruptible from outside, so the only stop a close can ask of a download is a step boundary: `request_stop` (`src/features/model_downloader.py:26-33`) sets one flag, the two step guards (`:55`, `:76`) read it, and `:101` ends the run before it can announce "All downloads complete!" over a download that ran nothing. `shutdown_runs` (`:205-217`) is `drain_attributes(self, ('thread',))` - the attribute is what the pane's own Cancel guard reads and what keeps Qt from the drop above - and `done()` (`:219`) asks it, because the Close button, the title-bar cross, Escape, the completion path and the error path all funnel into `done()`, so stopping there covers a caller that never thinks to call a close verb. `settings_tab.py:716` now constructs the dialog with the pane as its **parent** and keeps it as `self.model_manager`: parenting is what puts the run inside the walk's reach, and the attribute is what keeps the reference alive after the call returns. `test_05` reads the answer off the fake hub's own record - `['sd']`, and no `clip` - which is the half no `silence` can cover.

**The fake hub is what makes "the second step never started" a fact about this code.** `_FakeHub` (`tests/test_download_teardown_contract.py:46-71`) is a `types.ModuleType` installed in `sys.modules` for the length of one test, whose `hf_hub_download` records the call, signals it entered, and then blocks - because the only interesting moment of a stop is the one where a step is already in flight. `ModelDownloaderThread.run` builds its paths from the module's own `__file__`, so the fixture moves that into a temp tree and restores it in `finally`, which is where `ai_models` lands somewhere a test may delete. Nothing in this phase touched a network or a model file.

**Three sweep holes, and what each one actually was.** All three were found by the same method, now written into `tools/mutation_check.py`'s own output as the difference between an answer and a dead process: back the file up, plant the one line by hand, run with `-X faulthandler -v`, restore from the backup and check the sha256.
- **[05] reported CRASHED** - four named errors, then exit `0xc0000409`, with the verbose log's last line the *start* of `test_05`. This one was not the slot class: `QDialog.done()` is a reimplemented virtual Qt calls, so a raise that escapes the override - `reject()` -> `done()` -> `shutdown_runs` -> `silence` raising where the mutated clause no longer catches it - ends the process the same way, and a test that **enters** that frame can never be the one that reports a defect in the helper the raise passes through. The answer was to split the suite rather than to re-run the sweep until it agreed: `tests/test_run_teardown_contract.py` keeps the three tests that call the helper as the plain Python functions they are - and its `tearDownClass` now asks each pane's close verb inside its own guard, because a raise there would strand every pane behind it - while `tests/test_download_teardown_contract.py` (new, 244 lines, 4 tests) holds the dialog tests, whose fixture records every `ModelDownloaderThread.start`, releases the blocking step and waits for each thread before the frame dies. Two files, each answering its own defects as names.
- **[30] reported SURVIVED with 0 failures.** `finished` is emitted from inside the worker, so it reaches a main-thread receiver as a **queued** event: `thread.wait()` says the thread ended, not that the verdict arrived, and reading `ended` before an event-loop pass reads an empty list whatever the product did. `test_07` calls `processEvents()` between the wait and the assertion, and the boundary that announces a complete download over an empty one now fails by name.
- **[25] reported SURVIVED with 0 failures** for a different reason: the first version of the entry planted `if task.enabled:` -> `if False:`, which removes a *pre*-claim that `execute_task` re-makes on the worker a moment later, so no test written against a real thread can see it deterministically. The entry now plants the other direction, which is the defect that actually matters, and `test_20` catches it.

- Proof: `python -X utf8 tests/test_run_teardown_contract.py` -> `Ran 3 tests in 2.612s / OK`, exit 0. `python -X utf8 tests/test_download_teardown_contract.py` -> `Ran 4 tests in 2.589s / OK`, exit 0. `python -X utf8 tests/test_all_tabs_headless.py` -> `Ran 27 tests in 24.880s / OK`, exit 0, and `test_25` run alone (`2.938s`) reports **16 kinds across 18 instances**, up from 6n's 14/16: `ModelDownloaderDialog` and `AutomationTab` are now names the walk must reach, and the test builds the manager parented to the Settings pane exactly the way the pane does. `tests/test_automation_contract.py` -> `Ran 40 tests in 5.320s / OK`; `tests/test_automation_tasks_contract.py` -> `Ran 63 tests in 14.917s / OK`. `python -X utf8 tools/mutation_check.py tests/test_all_tabs_headless.py tools/mutations_run_teardown.py` (302 lines, **32 defects**: twelve read by the window suite, and twenty carrying their own `suite` - ten the teardown contract, seven the new download contract, three the scheduled-task contract) -> **`all 32 defects were caught by a named test`**, exit 0, no `SURVIVED` and no `CRASHED`; `--list` confirms all 32 anchors unique, and every file came back byte-identical (`tools/check_mutations_absent.py`: 13 lists read, 543 worktree files, clean). `python -X utf8 tools/check_unreachable_code.py` -> `No function body continues past a statement that ends it.` **The full chain on this tree: `python run_audit.py` -> 29/29 suites in 444.21 s, 100%**, with the new suite registered beside the teardown one - `AI Model Manager Teardown Contract` 2.82 s and `Run Teardown Contract` 7.53 s inside the chain - and both re-run alone after the two docstring corrections that writing this entry itself produced (`Ran 3 tests in 2.604s`, `Ran 4 tests in 2.669s`); neither line is behaviour, and both are numbers this file now quotes.
- Accounting for this area: new `tests/test_download_teardown_contract.py` (244 lines). `src/utils/run_teardown.py` +11/-3 - the three are the `wait_for_runs` docstring's own paragraph, rewritten to say that both kinds of thread are asked; `import threading` is the addition that makes the branch possible. `src/features/model_downloader.py` +49/-6 - the six are two trailing-whitespace-only blank lines inside `run` (each replaced 1:1 by an empty line), `if self.download_sd:` and `if self.download_clip:` (both now carry `and not self._stop_requested`), `def __init__(self, auto_start=True):` and `super().__init__()`, which become `(self, parent=None, auto_start=True)` / `super().__init__(parent)` / `self.thread = None`; no download step was removed and no signal lost a receiver. `src/ui/settings_tab.py` +8/-3 - the three are the one-line docstring, `dialog = ModelDownloaderDialog(auto_start=False)` and `dialog.exec()`, which become the parented, attribute-held pair at `:716-717`; the docstring grew the reason. `src/ui/automation_tab.py` +36/-5 - the five are `threading.Thread(target=self._emit_auto_actions, ... ).start()` (two lines) and `threading.Thread(target=self.automation_engine.execute_task, ... ).start()` (two lines) - each replaced by the call that files the run where the close can see it - plus the one-line docstring of `run_task_now`, which now states that the engine owns the thread. `src/features/automation_engine.py` +20/-0, a pure addition (`run_task`); nothing that used to run runs less. `run_audit.py` +8/-1 - the one deletion is the `run_teardown_contract` description line, whose "never starts a run" claim the split makes true again, and the new suite is registered beside it. `tests/test_run_teardown_contract.py` +90/-5 - the five are the docstring's "file that never starts a run" sentence, `test_01`'s two closing lines, the `for pane in (...)` / `pane.shutdown_runs(...)` pair that the guard replaces; the file's own content shrank to three tests by moving four of them into the new file, and every moved line is in that file unchanged. `tests/test_all_tabs_headless.py` +10/-1, `tests/test_automation_contract.py` +8/-1, `tests/test_automation_tasks_contract.py` +36/-0 - one deleted line each in the first two (the expected-holder tuple, widened by two names; and `test_23`'s one-line docstring, which now also states where the worker is filed). `tools/mutations_run_teardown.py` +153/-9 - the nine are the header paragraph that described twelve entries read by two files, replaced by the two-file/one-reason version above, plus seven `suite` lines re-pointed at the new file. No control was deleted anywhere in this area, and no pane's Cancel button lost a verb.
- **What this area does not prove.** **A download is stopped at its boundary and nowhere inside it.** `hf_hub_download` cannot be interrupted from outside, so a close during the multi-gigabyte step waits for that step - `shutdown_runs` reports the run as owed rather than as ended, and the reference stays with the dialog; what is *not* proven is the patience of a user in front of a progress bar that keeps moving after they pressed Close, because nothing here measures the wait a real download makes. Second, `silence(self)` in the Automation pane's `shutdown_runs` is **permanent for the life of that pane**: `test_03` of the contract suite drives a refused close, and after it the pane's own `auto_actions_finished` has no receivers left, so a watched file in the same session would run its actions and log nothing. The app's close ends the process either way, which is why the ordering is safe in product and a fact to know in a test. Third, the engine-side threads are still stopped by their own verbs and not by this contract - `closeEvent` calls `automation_engine.stop()` before it asks the holders - and `job_manager.stop_processing()` and `render_engines.py:304-306` join their readers on paths no renderer was run on. Fourth, mutation [25]'s lesson is a limit on what a thread-starting suite can pin: the *pre*-claim on `run_task:1746` is exercised by `test_17b`, which asserts the claim and the double-press refusal, but no entry plants its absence, because the worker re-makes the same claim too quickly for a test to see the difference. Fifth, #44 is untouched - `thumbnail_generator.py:247`/`:304`/`:311` still build an Open3D visualizer on a worker the Library grid starts per file, reached from `library_grid.py:738` - and #10's dead `model_tab_open3d.py` / `SystemMonitor` are still dead. Nothing here was clicked with a real mouse, no model was downloaded and no GPU render was run or claimed. **This fifth sentence is wrong and Phase 6p corrects it below: the thread was not live, and the file that started it is deleted.**

### Phase 6p - the app is left with one picture maker, the Versions pane commits through a QPainter instead of a GL context, and the thumbnail every row has carried since the first commit is finally shown

**#44's premise was the wrong one, and that changed the fix from a retarget to a deletion.** 6m and 6n both recorded that `src/core/thumbnail_generator.py` "is live", because `src/ui/library_grid.py:738` calls `generate_thumbnail(file_path)` for every uncached file and `_process_queue` (`:409-416`) starts a `ThumbnailGenerationThread` per file. Measured before anything was written: `LibraryGridWidget` is constructed by **nothing**. A grep for `LibraryGrid` / `library_grid` across `src/`, `tests/`, `tools/` and `main.py` returns exactly one hit - the string this phase's mutation list plants - and the Library pane uses `library_tab.py`, a different widget. So the thread never ran, no uncached thumbnail ever hung, and the sentence that said it was live was a reading of a caller nobody calls. That makes #44's requested fix actively wrong: retargeting it to the app's raster would have kept a second cache, a second `QThread`-per-file, and a second set of `thumbnail_ready` / `generation_failed` / `progress_updated` channels, all maintained, for a widget no pane shows. The two files are deleted instead - `src/core/thumbnail_generator.py` (487 lines) and `src/ui/library_grid.py` (820), 1,307 lines and the app's second Open3D thumbnail path gone with them - which also executes 2 of the 7 names in #10's dead-file list. `test_01` is what makes "deleted" mean something: it checks the files are absent, that no import node anywhere names either, and - because a lazy `importlib` or a string entry point is invisible to the AST walk - that no file **inside the app** still contains the words.

**The live half of the same defect was the Commit button, and it was worse than the dead half.** `AssetVersionControl._generate_thumbnail` built `o3d.visualization.Visualizer()` + `create_window(visible=False)` + `poll_events()` + `capture_screen_image()` on **the caller's thread**, which for the Versions pane is the interface thread: one press of Commit stopped the whole window from painting, on the call 6m measured at 392 s with a progress bar still spinning and a `cancel()` flag a thread blocked inside a native call never reads. `destroy_window` sat *after* the capture, so any raise between the two left the window alive in the process, and the entire body was wrapped in `try/except Exception` that logged `Error generating thumbnail: {e}` and returned `None` - so the freeze was also silent, and the row simply had no picture. It is now one call to `generate_3d_thumbnail`, the QPainter raster the Library, the Automation and the tagger already use, with no context to win and nothing to leak. The name it writes changed too, because the old one was a second bug: `f"{model_path.stem}_v{version_number}.png"` gives two different files both called `chair.obj` the same `chair_v1.png`, so the second commit overwrote the first row's picture with the other chair's - the picture a version shows was sometimes not that version's model at all. The file is now named for the snapshot its row points at, which is also how `create_branch` (`:411`) hands a new branch row the source version's picture.

**A refusal is a fact with a file attached, and the premise was measured before the guard was kept.** The raster answers `{'success', 'rendered', 'faces', 'error'}`, and `rendered` is the difference between a drawn model and a badge: `test_05` asks it for a faceless file and asserts *both* halves - `rendered` is False **and** the refused draw did leave a wireframe crate on disk, which is what makes deleting that file worth guarding rather than decoration - then asserts the backend leaves `thumbnails_dir` empty and the log carries the raster's own reason (`faceless.obj holds no triangle mesh`), not a generic failure. Recording the path anyway is exactly the lie this phase exists to stop: a timeline row claiming a picture of a model that was never drawn.

**The column every row carries, and the three kinds of nothing.** `version_control.py:193`, `:702` and `:712` have written `thumbnail_path` into every row since the first commit, and until this phase no widget read it - the grep at HEAD returns the Library's different column and one signal argument, no reader - while `version_control_ui.py`'s own header promised "a timeline with thumbnails". The Version Details group now holds the row's picture beside its numbers, in a `QHBoxLayout` inside the existing group, and its stylesheet is copied value for value from `library_tab.py:63` (`#14151b`, `border-radius: 4px`, `1px solid #232530`), which is the Library's thumbnail frame: the same 150 px box and 148 px picture the Library already shows, no new style invented. Three refusals, three sentences, because they are three different facts - no row selected, this row's picture was never drawn, its picture file could not be read - and `test_06` asserts each one separately, plus that the picture on screen is the file *this* row names: the fingerprint test measures 1.2-1.7 mean distance to its own row and 54 away from the other row's, and asserts both orderings, because `QPixmap.scaled` and `QImage.scaled` go through different scalers and pixel equality is not the fact being claimed.

**Two lines came out of the pane because the platform already did their job, and the mutation that found it came back SURVIVED.** Entry [09] planted the missing `self.version_picture.setPixmap(QPixmap())` clear. It reported **SURVIVED with 0 failures**, and the reason was not a weak test: on Qt 6.7.1, `setText` with a sentence clears a label's picture, `setText('')` does not, and `setPixmap` clears the label's text - measured in a probe on this machine before any line was deleted. So the explicit clear at the top of `_show_version_picture` and the `setText('')` before the paint were both doing nothing on every path that function can take. They are gone; `test_06` pins the three Qt answers in a `QLabel` of its own so the deletion is a documented dependency rather than a coincidence; and [09] now plants the defect that *does* matter - a refusal that says nothing (`setText('This version has no picture')` -> `setText('')`), which leaves the newest row's picture on screen over a row that has none. This is the same rule the log has applied to every guard since 6j: measure the premise, and when the answer is "the platform already does it", delete the line and pin the platform.

**`test_07`'s two dead lines, deleted while the file was open.** `save_version` had `logger.error("Model not found: ...")` and `return None` sitting *after* `raise IOError(...)` inside the disk-space branch - unreachable by construction, and `tools/check_unreachable_code.py` exists to find exactly this shape. The real not-on-disk check is the one at `:133`, and `test_07` now pins that it is the answer the pane gets: a missing file is refused by that fact, at ERROR, with no row written, rather than by a loader exception the commit handler swallows.

**What this found and did not fix, in the same function.** Two shapes reach `save_version` that its own metrics line cannot answer, because `len(mesh.faces)` assumes `trimesh.load` returns one kind of object. A multi-material `.obj` - what a DCC export writes, and what `MeshRepairer.auto_repair` itself produces - loads as a **Scene**: `Error saving version: 'Scene' object has no attribute 'faces'`, `save_version` -> `None`, zero rows. A faceless `.ply` loads as a **PointCloud**: same raise, same `None`, and the pane answers "No changes detected. Version not saved." (`version_control_ui.py:684`), which is the wrong fact, since the file was never compared to anything. Both leave the snapshot copy on disk with no row pointing at it (`snapshots -> ['cloud_v1_20261107_025942.ply']`, `rows: []`). The version of this that is worse than the bug is the reporting: `tests/test_full_flow.py:93` prints `✅ Committed version: None` and `✅ History entries: 0`, then "🎉 Full Integration Test Passed!", because it asserts nothing about either - the app's own integration run has been green over a commit that never happened. Opened as #49 with those lines; the normalising pattern the fix should reuse is already in `thumbnail_renderer.py:98-102`.

New `tests/test_thumbnail_owner_contract.py` (427 lines, 7 tests) and `tools/mutations_thumbnail_owner.py` (214 lines, 23 defects). `src/features/version_control.py` +27/-43 - the 43 are the four-line `try: import open3d as o3d` block, the two lines that sat after the disk-space `raise` and could never run, the old call site, and the 36 lines of the Open3D function body; the 27 are the raster import, the new call site, and `_generate_thumbnail` (def, its 16-line docstring, 8 lines of body), so the file is 748 lines where it was 764. `src/ui/version_control_ui.py` +48/-2 - the two are `details_layout.addWidget(self.details_text)` and the blank line above it, both re-expressed inside the new row; the additions are the 150 px label carrying the Library's own stylesheet values, the `QHBoxLayout` that puts it beside `details_text`, the two calls that hand it the selected row, and `_show_version_picture`, whose docstring now carries the Qt dependency the deletion above relies on. `run_audit.py` +7/-0, the suite registered as the thirtieth entry with a 240 s budget. `tests/test_data/repaired.obj` and `tests/test_data/simplified.obj` show as modified in the worktree and are **not** committed here: they are the fixtures the repair and LOD suites rewrite on every run, and no repair commit has ever included them. No control was deleted anywhere in this area - the Versions pane gained a picture, and the only things removed are two unreachable statements, one unreachable widget, and the two files that could not be reached without it.
- **What this area does not prove.** **No GPU render was run or claimed, and `test_04`'s byte-equality is narrower than "the picture is right".** Every image in this phase is the QPainter raster's; the test compares the recorded PNG against a second raster draw of the same snapshot made inside the same test, which proves the backend and the test ask the raster the same question, not that a real renderer would show the same frame. Second, the raster simplifies above 2,500 faces (`thumbnail_renderer.py:104-112`), so a committed picture of a dense model is a decimated one, and the comparison cannot notice because both sides go through it; 256 px is a choice copied from the Library, not a measured number. Third, `test_03` proves the *set* of files that open an Open3D window did not grow, and never re-measures the four that stay - the viewer, the Pipeline's capture, the point picker and `model_viewer_open3d.py` are their own suites' business, and the last is #10's to delete. Fourth, `test_01` proves absence of an import node and of the two words in `src/`; a class built from a string assembled at runtime would still slip past it, which is why the text scan exists and why it is not called a guarantee. Fifth, **nothing asserts that a branch row inherits its source's picture** - `:411` passes `thumbnail_path=source_version['thumbnail_path']`, the docstring says so, and no entry in the list plants it, so the sentence is a reading of the code rather than a guarded fact. Sixth, rows written before this phase are not migrated: a version whose window draw succeeded still names `{stem}_v{n}.png` and the pane will happily paint it if the file is there, and one whose draw failed names nothing - both are honest, but nothing in this phase measured a pre-6p database. Seventh, snapshot names are `{stem}_v{version}_{timestamp}` to the second (measured: `cloud_v1_20261107_025942.ply`), so two *different* files that share a basename and share a next version number, committed inside the same wall-clock second, would write the same snapshot - the batch-over-two-folders shape, not measured as a live failure, and the reason `test_04` sleeps 1.05 s instead of depending on it. Eighth, #49 is open: a multi-material `.obj` and a faceless `.ply` still cannot be committed at all, and `tests/test_full_flow.py` still prints a green run over a commit that answered None. Nothing here was clicked with a real mouse.

### Phase 6q - the Versions commit reads the shape a DCC actually hands it, an absent number is kept absent and said in one voice, and the flow run has to prove its own commit

**What was false, measured on HEAD before any of this was written.** `save_version` did `mesh = trimesh.load(str(model_path))` and then read `len(mesh.faces)` into its metrics dict, so it asked one question of three different objects. Measured: the suite's multi-material `.obj` (`mtllib` plus two `usemtl` groups - the same shape `MeshRepairer.auto_repair` writes, with `repaired_model.obj.mtl` beside it) loads as a **Scene** and the commit logged `Error saving version: 'Scene' object has no attribute 'faces'`, returned `None` and wrote **zero rows**, while `snapshots` kept `multi_v1_...obj` - a file no row names, invisible to `restore_version`, never deleted by anything. A three-line faceless `.obj` loads as a **PointCloud** and produced the same raise, the same `None` and the same orphan. A `.txt` raises `NotImplementedError: file_type 'txt' not supported`, a path that is not there raises `ValueError: string is not a file`, and an empty or garbled `.obj` loads as a Scene with **no geometries** - so four genuinely different facts, and `version_control_ui.py` answered all of them with the one fixed string "No changes detected. Version not saved.", which is a sentence about a comparison that had not run. Behind it, `_save_version_to_db`'s column defaults - `metrics.get('polygon_count', 0)`, `('is_watertight', False)`, `diff_metrics.get('volume_delta', 0.0)` - were going to invent a flat-shaded, open model out of a point cloud, and `_calculate_diff`'s `except: return {'polygons_delta': 0, 'vertices_delta': 0, 'volume_delta': 0}` reported "no change" for every failure it caught, including a parent snapshot that had been deleted from disk.

**The read is the app's one normaliser, and the measurement moved in front of the copy.** `_measure_model` (`src/features/version_control.py:61`) asks `trimesh.load` once, then `format_converter.as_trimesh` - the shared Scene join, made public for this (it was `_as_trimesh`; its two internal call sites `:462` and `:773` and the debug text `:119` moved with the name, so the app has one join where it had a private one and a fifth copy in `version_control`). A mesh gives faces, vertices and `is_watertight`; a `PointCloud` gives vertices and **nothing else** - `test_02` asserts on this machine that `.faces`, `.is_watertight` and `.volume` each raise `AttributeError` on it, which is why its face count and its sealed-ness are recorded as absent rather than as `0` and `False`; a file with no geometry at all gives `None`, and `:256-258` refuses on that fact **before** `shutil.copy2`, at ERROR, with `NO_GEOMETRY` in the sentence. The copy and everything after it (`_generate_thumbnail`, the parent lookup, the diff, the row) now sit in one `try` whose `except` takes the copy back - and the picture too, which is why `thumbnail_path` is seeded to `None` at `:269`: a failure between the copy and the picture used to leave a `.png` in `thumbnails_dir` that no row names, and the Library grid picks up files from that folder by name. `test_04` proves both halves by patching `_save_version_to_db` and `_generate_thumbnail` to raise; `test_03` proves the refusal leaves `snapshots_dir` and `thumbnails_dir` empty and no row. The import is lazy on purpose, and the cost was measured rather than assumed: `import src.processing.format_converter` is 1.544 s on this machine (pymeshlab), against 0.007 s for this module's own imports - so startup stays cheap and the **first commit in a process** pays the second and a half.

**A delta is subtracted from rows, not from a second opinion about them.** `_delta` (`:56`) returns `None` when either side has no number, and `_calculate_diff` (`:805`) takes the parent's counts from the parent's **row** - the two numbers the pane prints directly above - instead of re-reading its file, and claims a volume only when both meshes answer `is_volume`. Measured through the API, on one file: `creation.box` at 1 unit then 2 units is `polygons_delta 0`, `vertices_delta 0`, `volume_delta` exactly `1.0`; the hand-written pyramid over it is `-6` faces, `-3` vertices and **no** volume, because trimesh answers `is_volume` False for a surface whose base is wound the wrong way even though it is watertight; a box over a point-cloud row is `+12` faces recorded, `polygons_delta` NULL and `vertices_delta +5`; and a box over an open parent is `+6`/`+3` with the volume NULL. `test_06` pins the deleted-parent case separately: the counts still come out (`0` and `0`, because the row is intact), the volume is not claimed, and the warning names the snapshot it could not read.

**The words for an absent number live in one module and the pane quotes them.** `NO_FACES`, `NO_WATERTIGHT`, `NOT_COMPARABLE` and `NO_GEOMETRY` are at `:39-43`, `describe_polygon_count` / `describe_watertight` / `describe_volume` / `describe_metrics` at `:90`-`:114`, and `test_12` fails if a second module starts writing them. Six print sites read them: the timeline's Metrics column (`version_control_ui.py:566`), its green/red colouring (`:577-580`), the statistics block (`:534-542`), the details pane (`:618-626`), the comparison dialog's volume cells (`:162-164`) and the HTML export (`:669`, `:684`). A cloud row therefore says `V: 3, no faces` in the column, `Polygons: no faces` and `no faces to close` in the details, and the statistics line says `not comparable - one version of this file has no faces` rather than subtracting across it; `test_07` asserts all three **through the widget**, not only against the helpers.

**The pane says which fact stopped the commit, in the backend's own words.** `explain_no_commit` (`:317`) answers `{'kind', 'reason'}` - the refusal shape the app's other engines already use - by asking the same three questions the commit asked: is the file there, does it hold geometry, are these bytes already the newest row on this branch. It keeps no note of the last answer, so a second reader gets the same verdict; `commit_version` shows its `reason` verbatim under the title "Nothing Committed" and logs the `kind` (`:693-699`). Five kinds, and `test_03`/`test_08` each reach a different one, including the case where the sentence "no changes detected" is genuinely true.

**`tests/test_full_flow.py` had been green over a commit that never happened.** Its Step 4 printed `Committed version: {version_hash}` and `History entries: {len(history)}` with check marks over whatever came back, and what came back was `None` and `0`, because the repairer's own output is a multi-material `.obj`; `test_full_flow()` then returned `None`, `main` ignored it, and the process exited 0 with "🎉 Full Integration Test Passed!". The step now refuses to report what it did not check: no id or no single row fails, a snapshot in `.versions/snapshots` that no row names fails, a row pointing at a file that is not on disk fails, a recorded count of zero fails, every early exit is `return False`, and the module ends in `sys.exit(0 if test_full_flow() else 1)`. Measured on this tree: exit 0 and `✅ Committed version 1: 12 faces, 11 vertices, 1 row` - 12 faces and 11 vertices being the Scene's own sum, which the suite's independent oracle (`geometry_of`, reading trimesh directly and not through this backend) agrees with for its fixture at 12 faces and 20 vertices.

**Three guards were about to be dropped as untestable, and the list is bigger because they were not.** The first sweep run had 46 entries and I had decided to leave out any plant whose `replace` line I could not point a test at: the `or not parent_mesh.is_volume` half of the volume guard, the pane's `if first_polygons is None or latest_polygons is None` and its two `polygons_delta is not None` colouring tests. That is the same shape of hole this file has called a hole in the suite, so instead of passing on them I measured the cases they cover - `test_05` gained a box committed over an **open** parent (counts `+6`/`+3`, volume absent), and `test_07`'s existing rows already carry a NULL delta on a first version and a cloud row. The three plants went in as [23], [41] and [42] and each is caught by a named test rather than by a dead process, which is also the re-measurement this rule asks for before a guard is kept: all three `None` tests are load-bearing, not habits. One retarget came out of writing the list itself: both duplicate-check entries first carried `'file': src/ui/version_control_ui.py`, and `--list` refused them as anchor-not-found - the checks live in the backend (`:242` and `:337`), which is exactly the reason to run `--list` before a sweep rather than after.

- Proof: `python -X utf8 tests/test_version_metrics_contract.py` (565 lines, **13** tests, new) -> `Ran 13 tests in 12.381s / OK`, exit 0 - re-run after the sweep put the three mutated files back, so the number belongs to the tree committed here. `python -X utf8 tools/mutation_check.py tests/test_version_metrics_contract.py tools/mutations_version_metrics.py` (471 lines, **49** defects, new) -> **`all 49 defects were caught by a named test`**, exit 0, with `--list` first reporting `49 anchors are unique`, no `SURVIVED` and no `CRASHED` line anywhere in the run, 46 of the 49 caught by exactly one named test and 3 by two or three; the three entries written after the first 46-entry sweep were proved alone (`--only 23,41,42` -> `all 3 defects were caught by a named test`) before the whole list ran. `python -X utf8 tools/check_mutations_absent.py` -> `mutation lists read: 15; worktree files checked: 615` / `clean: every find line is in place in every file the lists touch`. `python -X utf8 tools/check_unreachable_code.py` -> `No function body continues past a statement that ends it.` `python -m compileall -q src tests tools main.py` -> clean. `python -X utf8 tests/test_full_flow.py` -> exit 0 on the final tree, step 4 printing `✅ Committed version 1: 12 faces, 11 vertices, 1 row` and line 9 still printing the tagger's wrong-reason sentence named below. **The full chain on this tree: `python -X utf8 -u run_audit.py` -> `Overall Health Score: 96% (30/31 suites passed in 484.37s)`, exit 1** - the thirty-first entry is this phase's `Version Metrics Contract` at `17.03s`, and the one red is not this area's file: `Core Backend & Regression Suite` (`tests/verify_all_fixes.py`) is `[FAIL] (22.14s)` on a process whose own stderr ends `Ran 9 tests in 20.080s / OK`, because `run_audit.py:261` reads a suite by `proc.returncode == 0` and that process fast-fails at interpreter shutdown. The attribution was measured rather than read: the same suite run six times as a subprocess of one driver in a throwaway `git worktree add --detach` of HEAD `470f3c1` (removed after the run; nothing in this tree was touched by it) returned `0xc0000409` **3 times of 6**, and on this tree **1 time of 6** - the abort predates 6q, and this pass neither causes it nor cures it, and cannot be reaching it: `version_control.py`'s only new import is the lazy one inside `_normalise`, and the string `version` in either case does not appear anywhere in `tests/verify_all_fixes.py`, so no test in that suite calls the function 6q made importable. Opened as #50 and named in Known-deferred, because a 30/31 quoted as 31/31 would be the fabricated result this file exists to stop. Of the 30 greens, the ones that would notice this commit's rename and its new module are: `One Picture Maker Contract` `PASS (10.43s)`, which shares `version_control.py`; `Format Conversion Contract` `PASS (9.53s)`, `Pre-Processing Pane Contract` `PASS (7.37s)` and `Automation Batch Operations Contract` `PASS (30.24s)`, which reach `as_trimesh` through the two internal call sites; and `Mesh Tools UI-to-Backend Contract` `PASS (20.12s)`, which is the pane whose output is the multi-material `.obj` this suite commits.

- Accounting for this area: `src/features/version_control.py` +244/-70 (922 lines where it was 748; the 59 non-blank deletions, line by line: the `Returns: ... None if no changes detected` line, replaced by the four facts and a pointer to `explain_no_commit`; the `# Load mesh for metrics` comment and the `mesh = trimesh.load(str(model_path))` it introduced, which is the read this file no longer makes in two places; the metrics dict built after the copy - `len(mesh.faces)`, `len(mesh.vertices)`, `model_path.stat().st_size`, `mesh.is_watertight` - with its `# Calculate metrics` comment; the five statements that ran outside that protection (`shutil.copy2`, `_generate_thumbnail`, `_get_latest_version`, the `if parent_version:` pair and the whole eleven-line `_save_version_to_db(...)` call) which now sit inside the `try` whose `except` removes the files; the two un-normalised `trimesh.load` lines in `compare_versions` and its three volume fields, where `else 0` became `else None` and the `delta` line stopped subtracting volumes that were never both measured; the export's `version.get('polygons_delta', 0)` default and its `<strong>Polygons:</strong> {version['polygon_count']:,}` formatting; `_calculate_diff`'s old signature (`(self, mesh, ...)`, which is why it could only ever be handed a mesh), its one-line docstring, its `parent_mesh = trimesh.load(...)`, its four-field return and the `except:`/`return {... 0 ...}` pair that made every failure look like no change; and the six `.get(key, default)` column defaults in `_save_version_to_db`); `src/ui/version_control_ui.py` +31/-18 (the 18 are the one-name import line, the dialog's three `:.4f` volume cells, the statistics block's four unguarded arithmetic lines including the `<span style='color: {color}'>` one it fed, the timeline column's `f"P: {version['polygon_count']:,}, ..."` with its three delta lines, the colouring pair that read `version.get('polygons_delta', 0)`, the details pane's `Polygons: {version['polygon_count']:,}` and its `{'✓ Yes' if version['is_watertight'] else '✗ No'}` - the two-way reading of a three-way fact - and the dialog's `"No Changes"` / `"No changes detected. Version not saved."` pair; **no control was deleted and no widget changed size**, the pane's only new element is the `QLabel` 6p added); `src/processing/format_converter.py` +7/-5 (the five are the private `def _as_trimesh(obj)` name, its one-line comment, the debug string that named the private helper, and the two internal call sites; the 7 are the same four with the public name plus the two-line comment saying what a `PointCloud` and an empty Scene both answer); `tests/test_full_flow.py` +34/-9 (the 8 non-blank deletions are two bare `return`s that exited a `True`-shaped function, the `version_hash =` line and the two check-mark prints it fed, another bare `return`, and the `test_full_flow()` call that threw its answer away - all replaced by `return False` and the assertions named above); `run_audit.py` +7/-0, the suite registered as the thirty-first with a 240 s budget; new files `tests/test_version_metrics_contract.py` (565 lines, 13 tests) and `tools/mutations_version_metrics.py` (471 lines, 49 defects). `scratch_patch_vc.py`, the one-shot patch script this pass used to apply the multi-line backend edits, is deleted, and so are the two sweep logs the numbers above were read from - the sweep is re-runnable, which is what makes it a checker rather than a memory.

- **What this area does not prove.** **No render was run, and no picture is claimed:** the commit path still draws through the QPainter raster 6p gave it, and `test_04` only asks that a failed write take the file back. Second, the Scene's `is_watertight` is measured on the **concatenation** of its groups, so a multi-material model whose shells are individually sealed but overlap is recorded as not sealed; that is what `as_trimesh` answers and the row says what the read says, but nobody inspected a real DCC export's answer here. Third, the volume column is claimed only when both sides answer `is_volume` - and every hand-written solid in these fixtures does not, so the one positive `volume_delta` this suite proves (`1.0` for a doubled `creation.box`) is a fact about a primitive, not a trend measured across exported models; a user with a genuinely closed export will see `no faces to close` in the dialog where a number could have been, and that trade was made on purpose because `0.0000` was a lie. Fourth, `restore_version` and `create_branch` were not touched: a branch row copies its source's snapshot, so its `0`/`0`/`0` delta literals describe the same file rather than a comparison that never ran, and `test_version_metrics_contract` does not test them - the duplicated docstring inside `create_branch` stays #10's. Fifth, nothing migrates an existing database: pre-6q rows can only be mesh rows, because a Scene or a cloud could not be committed at all, but no test reads a pre-6q file, so "the old rows still print correctly" is a reading of `describe_*` rather than a measurement. Sixth, the refusal re-reads the file: `explain_no_commit` asks `_measure_model` again after `save_version` already did, so a refused commit on a large export costs the read twice - not measured as a timing, and not worth a cache until it is. Seventh, the flow run's `12 faces, 11 vertices` is the repairer's own output on this machine with this trimesh; a different version summing a different Scene would print different numbers, which is exactly why the assertion is about "nonzero and one row" rather than about the twelve. Eighth, `tests/test_full_flow.py` still reports its tagging step as `⚠️ Tagging skipped/failed (expected if no model)` over an `AttributeError: 'AIAssetTagger' object has no attribute 'model'` - a wrong-reason sentence in the tagger's area, named in Known-deferred and not fixed here. Nobody clicked the Versions pane with a real mouse.

### Phase 6r - the dead-file list becomes an instrument, 4,265 unreachable lines leave the tree, and the Feedback dialog finally gets a control that presses it

**What was false, measured before any of this was written.** This log's **Dead files** paragraph - `:146` when the phase opened, `:149` now, because the prose above it grew - named nine files as dead, "6,075 raw / 4,619 code", with a line count beside each - and the nine counts add to exactly 6,075 (`1871+1065+1059+821+488+367+264+6+134`), which is the arithmetic that let this phase check the list against itself. Two of those nine (`library_grid.py` 821, `thumbnail_generator.py` 488 - the 1,309 that 6p deleted, which it quoted as 1,307 because it measured `wc -l`) had already gone, so **seven files and 4,766 lines were still standing** when the phase opened. Walking the import graph - parse every `Import`/`ImportFrom` node in the tree, resolve the relative ones the way the interpreter does, follow the edges out of `main.py` - answers `files walked: 151; modules under src/: 74; reachable from 1 root(s): 69 of them` on `92a5961` (the 151 includes the checker itself, copied into a clean `git archive` export of that commit because it is new in this phase; the commit holds 150 `.py` files, 74 of them under `src/`). Five of the seven are genuinely unreachable, 4,265 lines, and the list had all five. **Two of the seven are live and always were**: `src/utils/file_utils.py` has four importers (`ai_asset_tagger.py:168`, `diagnostic_engine.py:219`, `performance_analyzer.py:871`, `pipeline_editor_tab.py:31`) and `src/ui/feedback_dialog.py` has one (`main_window.py:27`), and all five of those lines are relative - `from ..utils.file_utils import get_temp_directory` does not contain the string `src.utils.file_utils` anywhere, so a grep for the module's dotted name returns nothing under `src/` and reads as "no importer". The list's counts were right (`1871/1065/1059/264/6` is `splitlines()`; the three longest files have no trailing newline, so `wc -l` says `1870/1064/1058`) and its *method* was the defect. The second thing measured was the dialog itself: `main_window.py` has had an `open_feedback` no control calls since the first commit, and `submit_feedback` answered a `save_feedback` that returns nothing, ignores whether the write worked and `print`s to stdout, with **"Feedback Sent - Thank you for your feedback!"** on screen and `self.accept()` behind it - so a folder that could not be made, or a disk that refused the file, produced a thank-you, a closed dialog and no record. Its own tooltip also promised an "app version" that does not exist anywhere in the tree.

**The claim is now an instrument, and the instrument is the thing under test.** `tools/check_dead_modules.py` (163 lines) prints what it walked, what it reached, and what it could not reach - then asks the second question, which files still *spell* each unreachable name, because `importlib.import_module`, a plugin path and a spec file are all invisible to a graph built from import nodes; it reports those as `unreached but named elsewhere` and refuses to call them dead on evidence it cannot see. `--strict` exits 1 unless the two sets agree, and `test_03` runs it that way. Because "everything is reachable" is also what a checker that reaches nothing says, `test_15` plants two modules under `src/utils/` - one whose name the suite never writes as a single word, one whose name it does - and requires the report to disagree with itself by exactly two, to print `dead:` for the first and `unreached but named elsewhere` for the second, to withhold the all-clear sentence, and to exit 1. `test_17` asks the word search about a name with four known callers and asserts it finds all four and does not credit the definition with naming itself; `test_18` asserts the walk never reads `dist/` (3,585 `.py` files) or `patch_script/` (141), which are frozen copies of this tree - 3,726 candidate hits for a word search that means to ask about the source, which is what the first version of the checker read and reported as *named elsewhere*, and is the reason the test asserts both folders stay out of the walk rather than trusting the `SKIP` set to be obvious. On the finished tree the same command says `files walked: 151; modules under src/: 69; reachable from 1 root(s): 69 of them` / `every module under src/ is reachable by an import from the entry point`, exit 0. One guard was measured away rather than shipped: the walk used to shorten a resolved name to its longest existing prefix (`src.ui.styles.BLUE` -> `src.ui.styles`) in case an import named a symbol instead of a module - with that loop and without it the reachability sets are **identical at 69 of 69**, so the loop is deleted, and the prefix loop that *does* carry weight is planted in the mutation list instead (`head = dep` -> `head = ''` -> `test_03` goes red, because `src.core`, `src.features`, `src.processing` and `src.ui` are reached by nothing else).

**What left the tree, and what stays as the answer to the same question.** 4,265 lines by `splitlines()` (4,262 by `wc -l`), one revertable commit, no control deleted with them: `src/ui/model_tab_open3d.py` (1,871) and `src/ui/model_viewer_open3d.py` (1,065) are a second model tab and a second viewport beside the `ModelTab` the window constructs at `main_window.py:80` and the viewports that really reach the screen - `unified_viewer.py:115 UnifiedOpen3DViewer` is built six times in four panes (`mesh_tools_tab.py:826`, `point_clouds_tab.py:1016`, `quality_analyzer_ui.py:120` and `:129`, `version_control_ui.py:70` and `:80`), and `model_viewer.py`'s three classes are imported by `model_tab.py:19`, `preprocessing_tab.py:21`, `painting_tab.py:14`, `rendering_tab.py:25` and `animation_tab.py:42`; `src/utils/system_utils.py` (1,059) is a grab-bag whose GPU answer is a same-named function to the live one - `get_gpu_info` at `system_utils.py:269` against `diagnostic_engine.py:294`, which the Diagnostics pane actually calls - and whose subprocess runners and executable lookups (`run_command` `:532`, `find_executable` `:564`, `check_dependencies` `:615`) exist as `automation_engine.py:203-207`, `render_engines.py:281`/`:399`/`:818` and `video_exporter.py:17`/`:38`/`:152`; `src/ui/weight_painting_tab.py` (264) is a second `PaintingTab`, and `test_09` now fails if the window stops constructing the live one or the tab label `Painting` stops being shown; `src/features/mesh_processor.py` (6) was `from ..processing.mesh_processor import *`, and of the **eighteen** files that spell the word `mesh_processor` - seven under `src/`, eleven tests and tools - every import line means `src/processing/mesh_processor.py` and not one names `features.mesh_processor`, so the shim was a second spelling of a live module's name and nothing else (the checker's own line truncates its hit list at six and prints `...`, which is exactly the number this sentence used to quote instead of counting). `tests/test_thumbnail_owner_contract.py`'s window list drops its entry for the deleted viewer, which is why that suite was re-run green as part of this phase rather than left to the audit.

**`src/utils/file_utils.py` keeps one function.** 367 lines to 30. The 22 functions and the `FileWatcher` class around it were named by no import in the app, the tests or the tools: the automation watcher is its own `class FileWatcher(QObject)` at `automation_engine.py:1099`, the Library answers the same two questions with its own code - the extension lists it filters and searches by are written inline at `library_tab.py:544` and `:2071`, and its asset-properties box takes the size off `os.stat` itself at `library_tab.py:3415-3416` (`stats.st_size / (1024 * 1024)`) rather than from `format_file_size` or `get_file_metadata` - and `get_file_metadata` / `validate_file_path` / `backup_file` / `create_archive` each answered a question this app answers somewhere else and had no caller to disagree with. `get_temp_directory` stays because four modules ask for it - the tagger's scratch write, the diagnostics pane's free-space answer, the performance report's disk question and the pipeline's staging root, which calls it twice - and `test_06`/`test_07` pin the two facts the deletion leaves exposed: the folder is created, and `exist_ok` makes a second ask not an error. `test_08` asserts the module has exactly one public name, so a second answer cannot come back quietly.

**The record the dialog writes says what it did.** Help gains `Send Feedback` (`main_window.py:270-272`), and `open_feedback` - the method this log has carried as unreachable since the first commit - is reached from a control for the first time. `test_10` asks the menu bar itself rather than the method: the entry is present under `Help`, its `trigger()` builds a dialog whose `parent()` is the window, and the list of dialogs constructed equals the list shown, which is the pair that a `QAction` connected to a method that forgot to `exec()` would otherwise survive. `save_feedback` returns `(path, reason)` instead of nothing, `submit_feedback` branches on it, and the two sentences are now different facts: `Saved on this computer:\n<path>` at INFO, or a `critical` box naming the failure while the dialog stays open with the text still in it (`test_14` reads the log at ERROR and checks `result() != Accepted`). The write is `open(path, 'x')` inside a retry that appends the attempt number, because the old guard - `%f` in the filename - was measured and does not guard: two `datetime.now()` calls in a row on this machine answered the **same** microseconds, which is what the previous version of this file asserted without asking the clock. `test_13` stops the clock instead of hoping for a fast run, and reads the first record back to prove the second save did not truncate it; `test_11` checks the JSON carries exactly the four fields the dialog showed and that the popup names the file it wrote; `test_12` drives `submit_feedback` with the checkbox ticked because the checkbox is only read there, and `test_16` proves an empty report is refused with a `warning` and writes nothing. The tooltip now promises operating system, Python and processor details, which is what the block holds; the app version it used to promise does not exist in the tree. `QSettings` is not consulted anywhere in this dialog, so there is no credential path to leak in it.

**The sweep also caught a list this phase did not write.** `--list` was run across all sixteen `tools/mutations_*.py` files after the deletions, and one anchor refused: `tools/mutations_thumbnail_owner.py` entry [01] asked for `thumbnail_path = self._generate_thumbnail(snapshot_path)` at twelve spaces, and 6q moved that statement inside the new `try` at sixteen, so the 6p list could no longer be run at all - the same class of staleness #37 was opened for, found by the checker rather than by memory. Retargeted to the line as it now reads and re-swept, all 23 defects caught again (see Proof).

**The chain then failed a suite this phase had not touched, and the defect was in the engine, not the test.** The first full run after the deletions answered `Overall Health Score: 93% (30/32 suites passed in 483.20s)`, `AUDIT_EXIT=1`. One of the two failures was `verify_all_fixes`, which printed `Ran 9 tests in 19.033s / OK` and was still recorded `[FAIL] (20.9s)` - the exit abort #50 carries, unchanged here. The other was [Automation Batch Operations Contract] `[FAIL] (30.09s)`, and its single failure was this diff on `test_69_stop_leaves_the_file_in_hand_to_finish`:

```
    - Stopping - the file in hand finishes first.
    + Stopped after 1 of 3: 1 ok, 0 failed, 2 never ran
```

The pane's sentence was not the bug; `BatchProcessor.process_batch`'s `finally` was. It cleared `current_batch`, `is_processing`, `_stop` and `_pause` and only then posted `batch_completed` (`automation_engine.py:1467` is the emit's place now, `:1468-1471` the four flags beneath it), so a reader on the main thread that asks the engine whether the run is over and then looks at the label could land in the window where the run had ended while the notification that writes the label's last line had not been queued yet - and the text still on screen was whatever Stop had left there at `automation_tab.py:2041`, which is the `-` line above. A settle loop that polls a flag *is* that reader, so the order is the product fix: emit first, flags after, with the reason written above the line that depends on it. Nothing on the pane side can time the gap it closes - a PyQt receiver is bound to the thread that called `.connect()`, so a callback attached to `batch_completed` is queued behind the very flags it would have to read, which is what the first version of the new test tried and why it answered `AssertionError: Lists differ: [True] != [False]` instead of a fact - so the contract reads the order off the function that decides it. `test_69b_the_summary_is_posted_before_the_run_is_marked_over` takes `inspect.getsource(BatchProcessor.process_batch)`, requires the emit's index to come before the index of `self.is_processing = False`, then runs one real file and requires the settled pane to read `Finished 1 file(s): 1 ok, 0 failed`. Mutation [80] of `tools/mutations_automation_batch.py` (now 80 entries, every anchor still unique) puts the flag back on the line before the post and is reported caught by `test_69b` by name, exit 0. The batch suite then ran 111 green and the Monitor suite 88, and the whole chain was re-run rather than trusted on those two.

- Proof: `python -X utf8 tests/test_dead_modules_contract.py` (448 lines, **18** tests, new) -> `Ran 18 tests in 10.121s / OK`, exit 0, on the tree committed here. `python -X utf8 tools/mutation_check.py tests/test_dead_modules_contract.py tools/mutations_dead_modules.py` (323 lines, **33** entries: 3 on `file_utils`, 16 on the dialog, 4 on the window, 10 on the checker) -> **all 33 defects were caught by a named test**, exit 0. `python -X utf8 tools/mutation_check.py tests/test_thumbnail_owner_contract.py tools/mutations_thumbnail_owner.py` re-swept after the retarget -> **all 23 defects were caught by a named test**, exit 0. `python -X utf8 tools/check_dead_modules.py` on the finished tree -> `files walked: 151; modules under src/: 69; reachable from 1 root(s): 69 of them`, the all-clear sentence, exit 0, and `--strict` exit 0; the same checker on a `git archive` export of `92a5961` -> 151 / **74** / 69 with three `dead:` lines and two `unreached but named elsewhere` lines, which is the before-and-after pair the whole phase is claiming. `python -X utf8 tools/check_mutations_absent.py` -> `mutation lists read: 16; worktree files checked: 649`, clean, so every sweep restored its files byte-exactly. `python -X utf8 tools/check_unreachable_code.py` -> `No function body continues past a statement that ends it.` `python -m compileall -q src tests tools main.py` -> exit 0. The 32-suite chain is recorded in the two bullets after this one. `python -X utf8 tools/mutation_check.py tests/test_automation_batch_contract.py tools/mutations_automation_batch.py` (603 lines, **80** entries) after the reorder -> **all 80 defects were caught by a named test**, exit 0; `python -X utf8 tests/test_automation_batch_contract.py` -> `Ran 111 tests` (the three `mutation_check.py` lines above first had to be corrected to the runner's real two-positional form - a quoted command that ends in exit 2 is not evidence even when the sweep behind it was run); `python -X utf8 tests/test_automation_monitor_contract.py` -> `Ran 88 tests`; both exit 0.
- **The chain, twice, and what each run said.** Before the reorder: `93% (30/32 suites passed in 483.20s)` / `AUDIT_EXIT=1`, the two failures being [Core Backend & Regression Suite] at 20.9s on `OK` output (#50) and [Automation Batch Operations Contract] at 30.09s on `test_69` (quoted above), while this phase's own 32nd suite, `Reachable Modules & Feedback Record Contract`, passed in 19.81s. After it: `100% (32/32 suites passed in 650.70s)` / `AUDIT_EXIT=0`, with Batch `[PASS] (40.65s)`, Core Backend `[PASS] (57.02s)` and the new suite `[PASS] (39.28s)`. **Read that as one clean run, not as two fixes.** The batch FAIL is the one the reorder owns: it was reproducible in the chain, it is gone from the chain, and `test_69b` plus mutation [80] are what keep it gone. The `0xc0000409` abort is not closed - it did not recur in the second run, and between one run in which a suite reported `OK` and was scored `FAIL` and one in which the same suite scored `PASS` there is only a scheduling difference, so #50 stays open and 32/32 is this tree's current answer rather than a promise about the next one. The two runs also did not take the same time (483.20s against 650.70s, with Preview Raster 37.18s against 64.06s on identical code), which is the reason the wall clock is quoted per run here instead of being averaged into a single figure.

- **Why the second run's 100% is not the number to put in a release note.** It is the score of a 32-suite chain on a machine with no renderer installed, in `QT_QPA_PLATFORM=offscreen`, against fixtures and `tests/mock_dcc`. It says every contract this repo can write down holds; it says nothing about a Blender or Maya process on a real desktop, and the exe in #38 was not rebuilt from this tree, so nobody has run the artifact a user would run.

- The walked total did **not** fall by five, and the reason is on this disk rather than in the repo. `92a5961` holds 150 `.py` files, 74 under `src/`; this phase deletes 5 modules and adds 3 files (the checker, the suite, its mutation list), so the committed tree arrives at 148 with 69 under `src/`. The walk says 151 because three more `.py` files live here and are gitignored, so `git archive` never had them: `output/model_tab_new.py` (122,593 bytes, 2025-08-27), `output/model_tab_old.py` (92,139, 2025-08-27) and `temp/weight_painting_tab_old.py` (11,204, 2025-12-19) - the last a copy of the tab this phase deleted. They are outside `src/`, so the checker walks them but never judges them dead, and `RenderFox.spec:8` bundles `src`, `resources` and `lib/assimp-vc143-mt.dll` only, so none of the three can enter the exe. They were **left in place on purpose**: three files dated before this repair, in folders `.gitignore:31` and `:25` claim as scratch, are the user's to delete, not this phase's to quietly remove while claiming nothing was lost.
- Accounting for this area: **deleted** `src/ui/model_tab_open3d.py` (1,871 lines), `src/ui/model_viewer_open3d.py` (1,065), `src/utils/system_utils.py` (1,059), `src/ui/weight_painting_tab.py` (264), `src/features/mesh_processor.py` (6) - 4,265 by `splitlines()`, 4,262 by `wc -l`, all five unreachable by the walk and each pinned by a named test. `src/utils/file_utils.py` **-358 / +21** (367 -> 30): every deleted line is one of the 22 functions in `DEAD_HELPERS` or its own `import`/docstring, and the +21 is the docstring that names the four importers with their file:line plus the one surviving function. `src/ui/feedback_dialog.py` **+41 / -16** (134 -> 159): the 16 are the one-line description of the promised "app version" in that tooltip, the `QPushButton("Send Feedback")` label, `self.save_feedback(feedback_data)` whose return value was thrown away, the `QMessageBox.information(self, "Feedback Sent", ...)`, the `"""Save feedback ... (mocking server send)."""` docstring, the fixed filename line, the `open(..., 'w')` pair, the two `print`s and their blank/`except` lines - each replaced by the `(path, reason)` return, the retry loop, the two branch messages and the `logger` calls, none dropped. `src/ui/main_window.py` **+4 / -0** - the three Help-menu lines at `:270-272` (`QAction('Send Feedback', self)`, its `triggered.connect(self.open_feedback)`, `help_menu.addAction`) and one blank; `open_feedback` itself at `:586-589` was already in the file and is unchanged, which is the whole point of the phase. `run_audit.py` **+7 / -0** - one 7-line entry, the 32nd suite. `tests/test_thumbnail_owner_contract.py` **+4 / -3** - the deleted viewer leaves the window list, and the comment says why the set shrank. `tools/mutations_thumbnail_owner.py` **2 / 2** - entry [01]'s two anchors re-indented from twelve spaces to sixteen. **New**: `tools/check_dead_modules.py` (163), `tests/test_dead_modules_contract.py` (448), `tools/mutations_dead_modules.py` (323). `REPAIR_LOG.md` **+39 / -4** in four one-line rewrites and one append: the dead-code table's closing note at `:59` (1 line out, 4 in - the sentence that called that row a stale number keeps its own words and gains 6r's re-count beside them), the **Dead files** paragraph at `:149` (1 for 1), the `system_utils` bullet at `:876` (1 for 1, gaining "Phase 6r landed that commit") and the `file_utils` corrections bullet at `:891` (1 for 1, gaining the fourth importer) - each rewritten on the line it corrects, no paragraph removed, which is why Phase 2's "6,075 raw / 4,619 code" still reads as it was written and now says whose number it is; then this section, 32 lines from `:1092`. Appending it moved the **Dead files** paragraph from `:146` to `:149`, and the three citations of `:146` this phase had just written were the first thing to go stale because of it, so they now name the paragraph instead of the line. `src/features/automation_engine.py` **+7 / -1** (2,084 -> 2,090) - 1 line out and 1 of the 7 in are the same `self.batch_completed.emit(summary)`, lifted above the four flag assignments, and the other 6 are the comment that says why it cannot go back; the flags, the summary keys and `return results` are otherwise untouched. `tests/test_automation_batch_contract.py` **+28 / -0** (1,592 -> 1,620) - `import inspect` and `test_69b`; no existing test was edited. `tools/mutations_automation_batch.py` **+9 / -0** (594 -> 603) - a section heading and entry [80]. `tests/test_data/repaired.obj` and `simplified.obj` are dirty because the mesh suites rewrite them on every run; they are deliberately **not** staged.
- **What this area does not prove.** **The graph is import nodes and nothing more.** A module reached only through a name assembled at runtime - `importlib.import_module(prefix + 'x')`, a `QPluginLoader` path, a string a future installer adds - is printed as unreachable, and `mentions()` is a word search that *hints* rather than decides; `--strict` passes today only because both sets are empty, and the sole proof that the two sets can disagree at all is `test_15`'s planted pair. Second, **the deletion is proven as unreachability, not as "nothing was lost".** `test_09` guards the Painting tab's construction and label, the re-run thumbnail suite guards the viewer list, and nothing guards `get_gpu_info` against `get_gpu_info` with a test that calls both on one machine and compares - `92a5961:src/utils/system_utils.py:269` against `diagnostic_engine.py:294`, where only the second survives on disk now, so that pair is a file:line reading and a `git show`, not a guarded fact, and it is the claim in this section a future reader should re-check first. Third, the exe is not rebuilt here (#38): the spec copies the whole `src/` directory, so a packaged build from this tree carries 69 modules where the last packaged build carried 74, and no one has run the frozen exe since. Fourth, the feedback record still goes nowhere - `save_feedback` writes JSON under `~/.renderfox/feedback` and the dialog says so in its own words; there is no server, no upload, and no account, so "Send Feedback" in the Help menu names a file the user must carry here themselves, which is stated rather than dressed up. Fifth, `open(path, 'x')` is proven against a stopped clock and a monkey-patched failing write (`test_13`, `test_14`), not against a real read-only home directory or a full disk. Nobody clicked Help -> Send Feedback with a real mouse, no render was run and none is claimed - every drive in this phase is offscreen Qt or a direct call into the module.
